# Tasks — Done Archive

Completed entries move here from `TASKS.md`'s `Backlog`, in the same commit that closes them out. This file is history, not a work queue — it is intentionally **not** part of the top-to-bottom read `TASKS.md` asks for when picking up new work, since a finished task has no bearing on what to do next. Consult it only when you need context on a specific completed task — its resolved decisions, exit criteria, or notes — that isn't already captured elsewhere (commit messages, code comments where warranted).

Entries keep their content as it was at completion. Don't retroactively edit a description or exit criteria after the fact; if something about a completed task turns out to be wrong or misleading, note that in a new entry or in code, not by rewriting history here.

### [T154.2] Add current-device logout and fresh guest return

- **Area:** Authentication session lifecycle
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add a Profile logout action using `signOut({ scope: 'local' })`, transition authoritative Auth state back to guest, and leave other device sessions untouched.
- **Exit criteria:** Provider and browser tests confirm logout invalidates the current browser session, returns to a usable guest workspace, does not request global sign-out, and handles offline or expired-session responses deterministically. Confirmed: the provider-layer `signOut` action (`{ scope: 'local' }`) and `SIGNED_OUT` → `guest` state derivation already existed and were already tested from earlier work (T153.4-era). Added a new self-contained `AccountSection` component (`src/features/auth/components/account-section.tsx`, mirroring `sign-in-screen.tsx`'s pattern) that renders `null` unless `authState.status === 'authenticated'`, shows the session email and a "Log out" button, and lets `AuthProvider`'s own `onAuthStateChange` listener drive the guest transition rather than forcing local state. Traced `@supabase/auth-js`'s `_signOut` source: an already-expired/invalid session (401/403/404) signs out successfully and silently to guest with no UI error; a genuine offline failure leaves the local session fully intact (no `SIGNED_OUT`) and is surfaced as a retryable `categorizeAuthError` → `'offline'` error via the same fixed-copy-map pattern as `sign-in-screen.tsx`, with the same-tick `isSubmittingRef` double-submit guard. Wired into `src/app/app/profile/index.tsx` as its own Account section (self-owns its Divider/label chrome so it collapses cleanly when not authenticated). New tests: `src/features/auth/components/__tests__/account-section.test.tsx` (initializing/guest/passwordRecovery render nothing; authenticated shows email + enabled button; success path tracks `log_out_success` and re-enables the button; offline path renders offline copy, calls `logAuthWarning`/`trackAuthEvent('log_out_error', 'offline')`, re-enables the button, and asserts the UI still reflects `authenticated` rather than faking guest; unknown-error path shows generic copy; no raw error message ever renders; same-tick double-press guard). Updated `src/app/app/profile/__tests__/index.test.tsx` with an `auth-provider` mock (defaulting to guest) and two thin integration assertions (Log out control absent for guest, present when authenticated). Full `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes.
- **Notes:** From S033. Depends on T153.4 and T154.1 per the umbrella note, but confirmed at implementation time that T154.1 (password/email-change UI, still Not started) is umbrella-sequencing only, not a technical blocker — no shared new model/type between the two disjoint routes. Before outboxes exist, this is the basic logout path; T158 later adds the unsynced-change guard and account-cache handling. No live Supabase round trip in verification (T153.1's local dev environment is unmerged, same precedent as T153.9): "browser walkthrough" here is the mocked-Jest + static web-export verification other pre-T153.1 Auth UI tickets have used.

### [T153.12] Invite a guest after their first completed Sesh

- **Area:** Post-Sesh Auth invitation
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Show a dismissible, optional signup invitation on the completion UI for the first completed Sesh in the current guest practice-history namespace. Derive eligibility from authoritative practice history and keep dismissal local to that completion UI rather than adding a profile field, storage key, or persistence domain.
- **Exit criteria:** Component/provider tests prove the invitation appears for an unauthenticated guest's first successfully recorded Sesh, can be dismissed or can open signup without changing the completed record, does not appear on later completions or after reloading the persisted first record, never appears for an authenticated user, and does not alter completion deduplication or any existing guest flow. Confirmed: added `isFirstEverRegularSession(priorSessions, origin)` to `src/features/stats/model/practice-history.ts` (excludes assessment-origin history), computed inside `Sesh`'s existing completion effect from the pre-dispatch `sessions` snapshot, and threaded as a plain `showSignupInvite` prop into `CompletedSesh` and a new self-contained `PostSeshSignupInvite` sub-component (own local dismiss state, `Link` to `/auth/sign-up`, no context reads) in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`. `Sesh` takes a new optional `authStatus?: AuthStatus` prop, supplied by `src/app/app/sesh/index.tsx` from `useAuthState()`; omitting it (as every pre-existing test does) fails closed to "invite never shown." New tests in `practice-history.test.ts`, `sesh.test.tsx` (`CompletedSesh signup invite`, `Sesh signup invite integration`), and a new `auth-provider` mock in `layout.test.tsx` cover: invite appears for a guest's first regular completion; dismissing or opening signup leaves the completed record and history unchanged; absent on a second completion in the same session; absent for an authenticated user and when `authStatus` is omitted; and a fresh mount seeded with one persisted regular session (via the real `PracticeHistoryProvider`/AsyncStorage path) lands on the picker, never the invite, proving the "after reloading" criterion follows from `Sesh`'s in-memory-only reducer state. Full `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes.
- **Notes:** From S032 and roadmap Phase 1 frontend item 6. Depends on T153.4 and T153.8. Guest-data import was not started here, per scope. Scoped to the single-trick `CompletedSesh` screen only; `MultiTrickSesh`'s separate wrap-up (assessment flow, multi-trick practice, stats sandbox) is out of scope for this iteration — same eligibility helper would extend there cheaply if wanted later. `/auth/sign-up` does not exist yet (T153.8 unmerged); the invite's `Link` forward-references the fixed path the same way other pre-T153.8 call sites already do, and `tsc --noEmit` passes because Expo Router's typed routes aren't checked against a committed route union in this repo (`.expo/` is gitignored).

### [T153.4] Add a static-render-safe web Supabase client and Auth state

- **Area:** Authentication state
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `@supabase/supabase-js`, a browser-persistent client configured only from `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, and one authoritative Auth provider modeled as `initializing`, `guest`, or `authenticated`. Configure `flowType: 'pkce'`, leave URL detection disabled so T153.7 owns the single code exchange, and ensure client construction and storage access are safe during Expo Router's Node static-render pass.
- **Exit criteria:** Provider tests drive missing/malformed configuration, initial session resolution, sign-in, sign-out, token refresh, Auth events, and offline initialization; children and every existing `/app` guest route remain renderable while Auth is `initializing` or unavailable; server rendering touches no browser globals; and `yarn expo export --platform web --output-dir /tmp/daily-dama-web-export` succeeds with only publishable Supabase configuration reachable from application code. Confirmed: added `src/features/auth/model/auth-state.ts` (pure `deriveAuthState`), `src/features/auth/persistence/supabase-config.ts` (pure env validator), `src/features/auth/persistence/supabase-client.ts` (lazily memoized client, AsyncStorage adapter, `flowType: 'pkce'`, `detectSessionInUrl: false`), and `src/features/auth/state/auth-provider.tsx` (three-state `AuthProvider` driven exclusively by `onAuthStateChange`, no `getSession()` call, no hydration gate). Mounted `AuthProvider` around `<Slot />` in `src/app/_layout.tsx`. 23 new provider/unit tests cover missing/malformed config, initial-session resolution (offline `INITIAL_SESSION` with and without a cached session), sign-in, sign-out (scope `local`), `TOKEN_REFRESHED`, synchronous-subscribe-throw fallback, unmount unsubscribe, and synchronous first-render proof that children never wait on a hydration gate. Ran the full local suite (841 tests, 75 suites) plus `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test, lint, typecheck, web export), and separately re-ran `yarn expo export --platform web --output-dir /tmp/daily-dama-web-export` with no `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` vars set — all 21 static routes (including every existing `/app` guest route) exported successfully with non-empty server-rendered HTML, proving the missing-configuration path renders safely and touches no browser globals during the Node static-render pass. Confirmed only `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` are referenced anywhere in `src/`; no service-role or secret key appears in application code.
- **Notes:** From S032. Depends on T145 and T153.1. T145 first moves rehydration-dependent domain providers under `/app`; mount Auth above the root `<Slot>` without adding an Auth hydration gate. The Auth state is the application's session/UI authority, not a substitute for later database RLS authorization. Keep a platform seam for Phase 5, but implement and verify web only. T153.1 has not merged yet; per this ticket's own feasibility framing, verification here used mocked `@supabase/supabase-js` responses and an unset-env export rather than a live backend, so this did not block on it.

### [T140] Trigger the screenshot showcase's entrance animation on scroll into view

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `ScreenshotItem`'s `FadeInUp` animation (added in T131) fires on initial page load, off-screen, so QA can't observe it without already knowing to scroll first. Trigger it instead when each item scrolls into the top two-thirds of the viewport.
- **Exit criteria:** Loading the landing page and scrolling down shows each `ScreenshotItem`'s entrance animation trigger only once it scrolls into the top two-thirds of the viewport (items already in that band on load animate in immediately); scrolling past and back doesn't need to replay it. Confirmed at both mobile and desktop widths via a browser check. Confirmed: added a `ScreenshotEntry` wrapper with a colocated `useHasEnteredViewport` hook (`landing-page.tsx`) that observes each item via `IntersectionObserver` with a `rootMargin` computed from `window.innerHeight` to represent the top two-thirds of the viewport, and swaps the inner `ScreenshotItem`'s `key` from `'pending'` to `'entered'` on first intersection to force the remount `entering` needs. Verified with a scripted Chrome DevTools Protocol browser check (no `preview_start`/GUI browser available in this session) at both 375×812 (mobile) and 1440×900 (desktop): each item's computed `opacity` and `entering`-driven animation only flip from `0`/not-set to `1` once its bounding rect crosses into the top two-thirds band while scrolling, stay at `1` afterward, and don't reset or replay after scrolling past all items and back to the top. No new console warnings beyond the existing benign ones. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export); `landing-page.test.tsx` passed unmodified, confirming the non-web/no-`IntersectionObserver` fallback (animate immediately, matching pre-T140 behavior) stayed intact.
- **Notes:** From story S010. Web-only concern (this project's active platform scope) — an `IntersectionObserver`-backed hook gating each item's `entering` prop is a reasonable approach, kept local to `landing-page.tsx`. Animation duration/easing (T139/S009) is unchanged — only the trigger condition changes. Raised from `Implementer` to `Required` during scoping review — `IntersectionObserver` isn't used anywhere else in this codebase, and it needs to interact correctly with Reanimated's `entering` prop (which normally only fires on mount), which is unfamiliar-API territory worth a planning pass first.

### [T135] Center the screenshot showcase items

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `Content` is a plain `View` with no `alignItems` set, so `ScreenshotItem`'s `max-width`-capped children default to the flex cross-axis start (left) rather than centering within the column. Center them at both mobile and desktop widths.
- **Exit criteria:** At both mobile and desktop viewport widths, `ScreenshotItem` renders center-aligned within its containing section — confirmed via a resolved-style unit test asserting a centering style (e.g. `align-items: center`) on the relevant container.
- **Notes:** From story S004. Independent of T134/T003 — a width-parity fix alone wouldn't center anything, since the underlying cause is missing cross-axis alignment, not sizing.
- **Resolution:** The actual flex ancestor governing `ScreenshotItem`'s cross-axis position is `ScreenshotSection` (the direct parent inside `Content`), not `Content` itself — `Content`'s own children (`Headline`, `Lede`, `FeatureList`, etc.) rely on its default `stretch` alignment to fill the column, so adding `align-items: center` there would have re-centered the whole page's text blocks as shrink-wrapped elements instead of just the showcase. Added `align-items: center` to `ScreenshotSection` (`landing-page.tsx`) and a `testID="screenshot-section"` for testability, scoping the fix to the screenshot showcase only. Verified via new resolved-style unit tests (`LandingPage screenshot showcase centering`) asserting `alignItems: 'center'` on `ScreenshotSection` at both a 375px mobile and a 1280px desktop mocked viewport. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).


### [T133] Fix the looping hero's overlap with page text on mobile

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** QA still finds the looping hero (`LandingHero`) overlapping "Why Daily Dama" on mobile despite T123.2's exit criteria claiming clearance at 375×812. Re-verify at more than one mobile width and fix whatever gap the original check missed.
- **Exit criteria:** At mobile viewport widths of 320px, 375px, and 414px, loading `/` shows `LandingHero`'s bounding box with no overlap onto the "Why Daily Dama" eyebrow or any text below it — confirmed via computed bounding-box comparison in-browser, not visual impression alone.
- **Notes:** From story S002. Check this after T142 (S012, enlarging the hero) if both are in flight — a larger hero directly affects this overlap and re-checking against the final hero size avoids finding-then-refinding the same regression.
- **Resolution:** The panel's hard-edged `boxShadow` (8px 8px 0, set at the JSX call site in `landing-hero.tsx`) rendered visually below/right of `HeroPanel`'s own box, outside what `getBoundingClientRect` reports. `HeroPanel` had no `margin-bottom`, so at every mobile width the shadow visually overlapped the immediately-adjacent "Why Daily Dama" eyebrow even though the two elements' bounding boxes never intersected -- exactly the kind of regression a pure bounding-box check can miss. Added `margin-bottom: 24px` to `HeroPanel` (`src/features/landing/components/landing-hero.tsx`), clearing the shadow with slack at 320px, 375px, and 414px. Confirmed via Playwright `getBoundingClientRect` at all three widths: hero-bottom-to-eyebrow-top gap is 24px (no overlap) at each, versus 0px before the fix.

Add them alongside the existing mobile assets in `assets/images/landing/` (e.g. a `-web` suffix or parallel subfolder — implementer's call).
- **Exit criteria:** Four new image files exist under `assets/images/landing/` showing the web layout of the four target screens under populated (non-empty, multi-item) fixture data. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` passes (asset addition alone doesn't require re-verifying the full suite, but run the full sequence once T117.2 wires them in). Confirmed: seeded realistic localStorage fixtures (4 standard tricks in rotation, 3 practice-history sessions across a 3-day streak, 2 in-progress goals with real progress) and drove the real dev server (port 8097) with a local Playwright install at a 1280x900 viewport to capture `home-screen-web.png`, `custom-trick-form-web.png` (name + Kentei rank 4 filled in), `trick-goals-web.png` (2 goals, 3/15 and 8/20 laces), and `sesh-first-lace-web.png` — the latter by genuinely practicing `swing-spike` (zero prior history) through the real trick-selection/countdown flow to one real "Laced" tap, confirmed via DOM text containing "FIRST LACE EVER / You just landed / Swing spike for the first time!". `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` passed all three non-export steps.

Direct-practice entry into Sesh (`/app/sesh?trickId=<id>&mode=practice`) still requires a "Start a sesh with <trick>" tap to confirm the plan before countdown begins; it does not skip straight to countdown. `playwright` was installed locally via `npm install --no-save` (chromium already cached) and fully removed afterward via `rm -rf node_modules && yarn install` so the shipped dependency tree and `yarn.lock` are unaffected.

### [T117.2] Render the visitor's platform-matched screenshot set

- **Area:** Landing
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** In `src/features/landing/components/landing-page.tsx`, swap between the mobile screenshot set and the web screenshot set (added in T117.1) based on the visitor's viewport width, using this repo's existing responsive patterns (e.g. `styled-components/native`'s `@media` usage in sibling components such as `app-tabs.web.tsx`'s `ResponsiveBrandText`).
- **Exit criteria:** A browser check at a mobile-width viewport shows the mobile screenshot set; the same check at a desktop-width viewport shows the web screenshot set. An automated test confirms both the mobile and web `SCREENSHOTS` arrays are defined and wired to a width-based condition. Full verification sequence passes. Confirmed: `landing-page.tsx` now exports `MOBILE_SCREENSHOTS`/`WEB_SCREENSHOTS` (built from a shared `SCREENSHOT_CONTENT` title/caption array plus per-platform asset arrays with their own measured `aspectRatio`), and `LandingPage` picks between them via `Platform.OS === 'web' && width >= 768` (the same guard already used in `multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx`/`onboarding-selection-step.tsx`), reusing `useWindowDimensions()`. A Playwright pass against the real dev server confirmed no horizontal overflow and the mobile screenshot alts at 320px/375px vs. the web screenshot alts at 1280px. `landing-page.test.tsx` adds a describe block asserting both arrays have length 4 and that mounting at a mobile vs. desktop-width mock renders the correct set's alt text exclusively. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test/lint/tsc/web-export).
- **Notes:** Depends on T117.1 (needs the web assets to exist first). Keep the existing four captions/T116 titles paired with whichever image set is showing — don't fork captions per platform unless the screens' content actually differs. Implementation notes/deviations: used the codebase's existing `Platform.OS === 'web' && width >= 768` responsive pattern (per the committed plan) rather than the Notes' originally-cited `styled-components/native` `@media` example, since that CSS-only pattern only toggles `display`, not which image asset is fetched. `jest-expo` defaults `Platform.OS` to `'ios'` in tests, so the web branch needed explicit mocking; mutating `Platform.OS` directly (precedent: `user-profile-provider.analytics.test.tsx`) worked, but `useWindowDimensions` needed `jest.spyOn` against a `require('react-native')` CommonJS reference rather than an `import * as ReactNative` namespace import — Babel's ES-module namespace-import interop wraps CJS modules in a fresh object, so a spy set on that wrapper wasn't visible to `LandingPage`'s own named `import { useWindowDimensions } from 'react-native'`, which resolves against the original `module.exports` object. Also avoided `jest.mock('react-native', () => ({ ...actual, ... }))`: spreading `jest.requireActual('react-native')` eagerly evaluates all of react-native's lazily-loaded native-module exports (e.g. `DevMenu`, `FlatList`), which throws under `jest-expo`'s native-module mocks in a way that isn't otherwise triggered.

### [T116] Give the landing screenshot showcase titles and a designed container

- **Area:** Landing
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** In `src/features/landing/components/landing-page.tsx`, add a short title to each of the four `SCREENSHOTS` entries, rendered above the existing caption in typography visually distinct from the page's `BodyText` style, and give `ScreenshotItem` a container with its own background color and border (distinct from the page's plain `AppColors.paper` background).
- **Exit criteria:** An automated test asserts each of the four screenshots renders both a title and its existing caption as distinct text nodes. A browser check confirms each screenshot sits inside a container with a visible background/border treatment distinct from the surrounding page background. Full verification sequence passes. Confirmed: added a `title` field to each `SCREENSHOTS` entry ("Your practice loop, at a glance," "First lace, first cheer," "Your move, your rank," "Progress you can point to") rendered via a new `ScreenshotTitle` styled.Text (19px/900) above the existing `ScreenshotCaption`. `ScreenshotItem` now carries a `paperStrong` background, a 2px `ink` border, `Radii.panel` radius, and a 6px hard-offset boxShadow (matching the `trick-card.tsx`/`CtaPanel` idiom), distinct from the page's `AppColors.paper` background. New `landing-page.test.tsx` asserts all four title/caption pairs render as distinct text nodes. A headless-Chrome render of the dev server confirmed the card background (`#f6efd9`) and `#18382e` ink border bands are visually distinct from the surrounding page background (`#f8f1dd`) at the screenshot-card locations. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** Four titles are new copy to draft in this task (e.g. "Your practice loop," "First lace," "Custom tricks," "Trick goals" — exact wording is the implementer's call, matching the page's existing voice). No change to which screenshots are shown or their source data — that's T117's concern. Promoted to `Planning: Required` at the user's explicit request via the scoping-review artifact (was `Implementer`).

### [T104] Clear a custom text color when a preset theme is selected

- **Area:** Profile / Theming
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** The `themeColorsUpdated` reducer case (`user-profile.ts:142-147`), fired by preset selection (`rainbow-theme-presets.tsx:30`), fully replaces `themeBackgroundColor`/`themeAccentColor` but never touches `themeTextColor` — so a previously saved custom text color survives a later preset selection instead of being replaced by the preset's look. Have preset selection also clear the custom text color, either by extending `setThemeColors`/`themeColorsUpdated` to reset `themeTextColor` to `undefined`, or by having the preset-tap handler additionally call the existing `setThemeTextColor(undefined)` action.
- **Exit criteria:** Reducer test: dispatch a custom `themeTextColor` being set, then apply `themeColorsUpdated` (a preset), and assert `themeTextColor` is `undefined` afterward. Component test: select a preset in `RainbowThemePresets` after a custom text color was saved, and assert the persisted profile no longer carries a custom text color. Existing tests for a direct, manual `ThemeColorPicker` text-color save (an unaffected path) continue to pass unmodified.
- **Notes:** Story S020. Marked `Required` because it changes reducer behavior for a persisted field, per this project's escalation rule for state-machine/reducer changes. Resolved by extending the `themeColorsUpdated` reducer case in `user-profile.ts` to always reset `themeTextColor` to `undefined`, since the exit criteria's reducer test dispatches that action alone. Flagged side effect: `ThemeColorPicker`'s manual background/accent saves also call `setThemeColors`, so they now clear a previously saved custom text color too — no existing test asserted that field survived those saves, so nothing broke, and this is arguably more correct (a background change can invalidate a tuned text-color contrast).

### [T098] Derive a secondary text color alongside the existing primary text color

- **Area:** Profile / Theming
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `resolveTheme()`/`ResolvedTheme` (`src/features/profile/state/use-resolved-theme.ts`) derives exactly one `text` color today, used identically by all three shared typography primitives (`Eyebrow`, `DisplayTitle`, `BodyText` in `src/components/ui/typography.tsx`) — every themed text role renders the same flat color. Add a second derived field (e.g. `textMuted`) by blending the resolved primary `text` color partway toward the resolved `background`, so it works uniformly whether `text` came from automatic derivation, a preset, or the user's explicit `themeTextColor` override. Wire `Eyebrow` and `BodyText` to the new muted color, leaving `DisplayTitle` on the primary `text` color as the anchor/majority role.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests on the theme-resolution logic assert `textMuted` is distinct from `text` and legibly blended toward `background` for (a) the default theme, (b) a custom background/accent/text combination, and (c) each T087 preset. Component tests confirm `Eyebrow`/`BodyText` render with `theme.textMuted` and `DisplayTitle` still renders with `theme.text`. `yarn tsc --noEmit` and the full existing test suite pass unmodified elsewhere.
- **Notes:** Deliberately minimal: two roles (primary anchor + one complementary secondary), not a full title/eyebrow/subtext-each-distinct system — this is the smallest change that satisfies "not one flat color repeated everywhere" from S010's acceptance criteria. A blend-toward-background algorithm was chosen over hand-picking new fixed `AppColors` constants (e.g. a cream-family equivalent of `inkSoft`/`textMuted`/`textSubtle`, which only exist for the ink family today) because it generalizes to any base color — default, preset, or custom override — without a second derivation path per direction. One-off styled-text usages outside the three shared typography primitives (e.g. `daily-home.tsx`'s `SectionTitle`, `NextTrickName`, etc., flagged as a known gap in T085's own notes) are out of scope for this ticket — a broader inventory/migration onto the shared primitives is a separate follow-on, not blocking here.
- **Verification:** `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`). New unit tests in `use-resolved-theme.test.ts` cover the default theme, a custom background/accent/text combination, and all seven T087 presets; new component tests in `typography.test.tsx` confirm `Eyebrow`/`BodyText` resolve `theme.textMuted` and `DisplayTitle` resolves `theme.text`. Added a generic `blendHexColors` helper (`src/shared/color/blend.ts`) and `deriveMutedTextColor` (65% text / 35% background linear-RGB blend) alongside the existing `deriveAutomaticTextColor`/`deriveAutomaticButtonBackgrounds` derivation functions in `use-resolved-theme.ts`; extended `DefaultTheme` (`src/types/styled-components.d.ts`) and existing hardcoded theme fixtures in `button.test.tsx`/`sesh.test.tsx` with `textMuted`.

### [T095] Initialize color pickers from the resolved default theme

- **Area:** Profile / Theming
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `ColorField` in `theme-color-picker.tsx` initializes `hexText` from `useState(persistedValue ?? "")` (line 81), where `persistedValue` is the raw, often-`undefined` stored preference (lines 32, 39, 53). Thread `useResolvedTheme()`'s `background`/`accent`/`text` output through as the fallback instead of `""`, so the picker shows the app's real current colors before any save.
- **Exit criteria:** A component test mounts `ThemeColorPicker` with no saved theme preference and asserts each `ColorField`'s swatch/hex text shows the resolved default theme's values (not blank); saving still works and persists identically to today.
- **Notes:** No new persistence field — purely an initial-value fix. Added `fallbackColor` prop to `ColorField`, sourced from `useResolvedTheme()` in the parent, and used it to seed `hexText`. Also had to change the dirty/`canSave` check from comparing against `(persistedValue ?? fallbackColor)` to comparing against raw `persistedValue` only — with the fallback in play, a typed value that coincidentally matches the resolved default (e.g. saving the default accent `#f4c94d` verbatim) would otherwise be treated as unchanged and block Save, which would have broken existing save behavior. New test asserts each field's initial hex-input value and swatch color equal `resolveTheme({})`'s output when no preference is saved.

Copy reads to the effect of "Select a theme that matches your setup." Skipping the step leaves the app on its default theme.
- **Exit criteria:** An onboarding-flow test drives through the new step, selects a preset, and verifies the selected preset's background/accent persist via the profile provider identically to selecting it from Profile; a second case confirms skipping the step completes onboarding normally with no theme preference set. Existing onboarding-flow tests through `completeOnboarding` → `router.replace('/sesh')` continue to pass with the new step inserted.
- **Notes:** Reuses T087's existing `RAINBOW_THEME_PRESETS`/`setThemeColors` mechanism — no new preset design needed. Marked `Required` because it changes the onboarding reducer's step sequence, which the project's `Required` criteria explicitly calls out (state machines). Inserted a new `themeSelect` reducer status between `selecting` and completion in `kentei-assessment.ts` (spreading `selecting`'s fields, guarded no-op elsewhere), moving `completeOnboarding`/`router.replace('/sesh')` out of `selecting`'s handler into the new step's. Extracted the existing swatch-grid UI out of `rainbow-theme-presets.tsx` into a new shared `ThemePresetGrid` component (`src/features/profile/components/theme-preset-grid.tsx`) so the new `OnboardingThemeStep` (`src/features/onboarding/components/onboarding-theme-step.tsx`) reuses it exactly; `rainbow-theme-presets.test.tsx` kept passing unmodified, confirming the extraction was behavior-preserving. Tapping a preset applies it via `setThemeColors` and immediately finishes onboarding; a separate secondary-variant "Skip for now" button finishes onboarding without touching theme colors. New `kentei-onboarding.test.tsx` drives the full reducer + component sequence end-to-end for both cases (no such test existed before this task) — confirms identical persisted background/accent to selecting the same preset from Profile, and confirms the skip path leaves `themeBackgroundColor`/`themeAccentColor` unset. Full web-only verification (`yarn test`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, web export) passes; native validation remains deferred under the active platform scope.

### [T085.1] Add automatic and custom text color to the theme model

- **Area:** Profile persistence / Theming
- **Priority:** Medium (inherited from parent T085)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required

Extend `ResolvedTheme`/`useResolvedTheme` (`src/features/profile/state/use-resolved-theme.ts`) with a `text` value: the user's custom `themeTextColor` when set, otherwise a value automatically derived from the resolved background/accent (e.g. a luminance-based light/dark choice), replacing today's fixed text color.
- **Exit criteria:** Reducer/persistence tests confirm `themeTextColor` round-trips through hydration, defaults to `undefined`, and doesn't affect existing profile fields. A test on the resolved-theme text logic confirms it returns the custom value when set and a derived light/dark value from background+accent when unset, covering at least one light-background and one dark-background case.

The automatic-derivation algorithm is an implementation choice, not specified here. Added `themeTextColor` to `UserProfile`, a dedicated `themeTextColorUpdated` reducer action (kept separate from `themeColorsUpdated` so T085.2's control can reset it independently) and matching persistence/provider wiring (`setThemeTextColor`). Refactored `use-resolved-theme.ts` into a pure, independently-testable `resolveTheme` function plus a thin `useResolvedTheme` hook; added `deriveAutomaticTextColor`, which picks whichever of `AppColors.ink`/`AppColors.cream` maximizes the minimum WCAG contrast ratio against both the resolved background and accent (text can sit on either surface). No schema version bump per AGENTS.md's provisional-v1 policy. No screen consumes `theme.text` yet (T085.3).

### [T085.2] Remove the contrast Save gate; add a custom text-color control

- **Area:** Profile / Theming
- **Priority:** Medium (inherited from parent T085)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** In `ThemeColorPicker`/`ColorField` (`src/features/profile/components/theme-color-picker.tsx`), remove `hasSufficientContrast` from the `canSave` gate (currently `canSave = isDirty && hasSufficientContrast`, `theme-color-picker.tsx:72`) and its blocking hint copy, so any valid hex pair saves regardless of contrast. Add a third `ColorField`-style control for a custom text color that writes to `themeTextColor` (T085.1) and can be reset independently.
- **Exit criteria:** Component tests confirm a background/accent pair that previously failed the contrast check now saves successfully with no blocking message, and setting/clearing a custom text color persists via the profile provider and is reflected in a live swatch/preview.
- **Notes:** Depends on T085.1. Touches the same file as T086 (S001's visual color picker) — whichever lands second should expect to rebase around the other's changes to `theme-color-picker.tsx`. Any leftover contrast-ratio copy must not block saving; remove it or make it purely informational. Removed `hasSufficientContrast` from `canSave` (now just `isDirty`); contrast ratio is now shown as non-blocking informational copy (`"Contrast vs. text: {ratio}:1. Recommended: {WCAG_AA_MIN_CONTRAST_RATIO}:1+."`) for all three fields. Added a third `ColorField` for `themeTextColor` (wired to `setThemeTextColor`), with its own independent reset button ("Reset text color") separate from the existing background/accent reset (relabeled "Reset background & accent" for clarity). `ColorField` now takes an explicit `referenceColor` prop instead of hardcoding `AppColors.ink`; background/accent keep `AppColors.ink` (out of scope to change), the new text field uses `themeBackgroundColor ?? AppColors.sage` (mirroring `resolveTheme`'s fallback).

### [T085.3] Apply theme text color across post-onboarding screens

- **Area:** Theming (cross-cutting)
- **Priority:** Medium (inherited from parent T085)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Replace fixed text colors on title/eyebrow/label/body text across post-onboarding screens — `Eyebrow`, `DisplayTitle`, `BodyText` (`src/components/ui/typography.tsx`) plus one-off fixed-color text such as `SectionTitle`/`NextTrickName`/`NextTrickMeta` in `daily-home.tsx`, and equivalents in `sesh.tsx`, `assessment-entry.tsx`, `multi-trick-sesh.tsx`, `multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx`, `laced-tricks-screen.tsx`, `neglected-tricks-section.tsx`, `stats/index.tsx`, `stats/[trickId].tsx`, `tricks/index.tsx`, `profile/index.tsx` — with the resolved `theme.text` value from T085.1, falling back identically to today's fixed colors when no theme is set.
- **Exit criteria:** Auto-derived and custom-override theme text colors are correctly applied across the enumerated screens; pre-onboarding yellow state and functional colors (errors/success) are unaffected. Relaxed by explicit user decision: an unset theme is no longer required to render identically to today's fixed colors for `Eyebrow`/`BodyText`-family text (see Notes).
- **Notes:** Depends on T085.1; can proceed in parallel with T085.2 since it doesn't touch the picker component. Mirrors T083.3's screen-sweep approach. File list was identified via `grep -rln "AppColors\.\(red\|ink\|inkSoft\|textMuted\)"` across post-onboarding areas and should be re-verified at implementation time since it will drift; `button.tsx`, `practice-history-provider.tsx`, and `user-profile-provider.tsx` also matched but looked like non-text or non-rendering usages — confirmed during implementation: `button.tsx`'s secondary-variant `ButtonLabel` was in scope and converted, `practice-history-provider.tsx`/`user-profile-provider.tsx` are hydration-fallback styles rendered before a profile exists and stay fixed. The Eyebrow/BodyText conversion (from `AppColors.red`/`AppColors.textMuted`/`AppColors.inkSoft` to `theme.text`) means an unset theme now renders these elements in the same ink-green as titles, rather than the prior red/muted shades — a real, visible unset-theme rendering change. A prior implementation pass flagged this as an internal tension with the original "renders identically when unset" exit-criterion wording and stopped short of committing; the user explicitly reviewed the trade-off and decided to accept the visual shift and relax that criterion accordingly (exit criteria text above reflects the relaxed wording). Verified in-browser: onboarding and a fresh unset-theme post-onboarding state keep the original palette look with the accepted ink-green shift on former red/muted text; setting a custom text color (`#7a1fa2`) via Profile propagates live across Home, Sesh, Tricks (button labels), and Stats, including on accent-colored surfaces, without affecting the Button primary-variant's `theme.accent` label; an invalid Sesh duration still shows the fixed functional red error color unaffected by the custom text color; `trick-directory.tsx`'s distinct raw-hex header color was left untouched per plan (not one of the four target tokens).

### [T063] Add a preselected-trick "Start sesh" variant for practice entry points

- **Area:** Sesh / Navigation
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Every existing "Practice" deep link into Sesh (`Link href={{ pathname: '/sesh', params: { trickId } }}`) still renders the full setup picker — format choice, duration/target input, and the complete trick list — with the linked trick only cosmetically pinned to the top and labeled "Up next." Add a distinct variant that shows just the preselected trick and a "Start sesh" button, reachable only from explicit practice entry points, not from a plain visit to the Sesh tab.
- **Exit criteria:** Test: visiting Sesh via each of the identified practice-entry call sites (Tricks-row Practice action, Stats "Needs attention" Practice action, Home "Practice again" hero button, Home "In rotation" queue rows, Stats trick-detail "Challenge" action) renders the preselected trick plus a Start-sesh action only, with no other setup UI, and starting immediately begins that trick's countdown; a plain visit to the Sesh tab — including for a returning user whose `lastSesh` would otherwise prefill a trick — still renders the full setup picker exactly as today, unchanged.
- **Notes:** "Should only be accessible via the practice flow" is the requester's own scoping language. This must be a genuinely distinct signal from the existing `trickId`/`lastSesh` mechanism in `src/app/sesh/index.tsx` — that mechanism is *already* the everyday returning-user experience (every plain Sesh visit reads `lastSesh.trickId` the same way an explicit deep link reads its `trickId` param), so reusing it as-is for this variant would silently change the default Sesh-tab UX for all returning users, which is out of scope. Likely shape: a new param (e.g. a `mode`/`practice` flag) alongside `trickId`, and a new early-return branch in `Sesh`/`TrickPicker` gated on both that flag and `validatedPreferredTrickId` (already computed in `sesh.tsx`, confirms the trick is actually practiceable) being present. [T066] (goal → practice entry point) depends on this landing first — it's meant to be one of the "distinct instances of the practice UX flow" that converge on this new variant rather than the legacy full-picker link. Implemented via a `mode=practice` search param sent alongside `trickId` from all five call sites, gated in `Sesh` on both the raw param and `validatedPreferredTrickId` resolving against `practiceTricks`; added a `QuickStart` component reusing existing styled primitives, and a shared `handleTrickSelected` so both quick-start and the full picker record sessions identically. Fixed an existing `neglected-tricks-section.test.tsx` assertion that hardcoded the old href shape. Verified with `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (full suite, after running `yarn install` in this worktree whose `node_modules` was missing on entry).

### [T062] Exclude Kentei-assessment sessions from Stats and Home aggregation

- **Area:** Stats / Home / persistence (practice-history domain)
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Landings recorded during a Kentei assessment write ordinary `PracticeSessionRecord`s — identical in shape to regular Sesh/ad hoc multi-trick practice, with no field distinguishing origin — so they currently dilute the Stats screen's "By trick" list and Overview metrics, the per-trick history/personal-best page, and Home's momentum/streak/recent-session summary, all of which pull the full unfiltered practice-history pool.
- **Notes:** Umbrella only — complete T062.1 and T062.2. Kentei rank promotion (`KenteiRankAutoPromotion`/`selectLacedTrickIds`) deliberately depends on assessment-sourced landings flowing into the same session pool ("a full pass through a rank promotes you automatically" is Profile's own stated promise) — any fix must filter *display* reads only, never what gets written or what promotion logic counts. Going-forward only: sessions already recorded from past assessments predate any new origin tag and can't be reliably reclassified after the fact; no retroactive cleanup/migration is in scope, consistent with this being local-first prototype data. Both children (T062.1, T062.2) are complete.

### [T062.2] Filter Stats and Home reads to exclude Kentei-assessment sessions

- **Area:** Stats / Home
- **Priority:** Medium (inherited from parent T062)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Using T062.1's new field, exclude Kentei-assessment-sourced sessions from Stats' Overview metrics and "By trick" list (`src/app/stats/index.tsx`), the per-trick detail/personal-best page (`src/app/stats/[trickId].tsx`, a separate independent read site — does not inherit filtering from the Stats index screen), the neglected-tricks "Needs attention" section, and Home's momentum/streak/recent-session summary (`src/app/index.tsx`). Depends on T062.1.

- **Notes:** Four independent read sites identified, all needed the same filter applied since none shared a filtered intermediate value: `src/app/stats/index.tsx` (feeds Overview, By-trick, and neglected-tricks in one `sessions` pull), `src/app/stats/[trickId].tsx` (its own separate `usePracticeHistory()` call), and `src/app/index.tsx` (Home). `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` reads `usePracticeHistory()` independently and was deliberately left untouched. Added `selectRegularSessions` (filters out `origin === 'assessment'`) to `src/features/stats/model/practice-history.ts` as the single shared selector, applied at each of the three call sites (including Home's `summaryNow` derivation, so an assessment session's `completedAt` doesn't count as "now" either), while keeping `aggregatePracticeStats`, `selectNeglectedFocusedTricks`, `getPersonalBest`, and `createHomeSummary` themselves origin-agnostic per the plan. New coverage: unit tests for `selectRegularSessions`; screen-level tests for `stats/index.tsx` (Overview/By-trick totals, empty state when only assessment sessions exist), `stats/[trickId].tsx` (personal best and Past-seshes list exclude an assessment session that would otherwise win), and an extension to Home's existing test file (Momentum and "Last sesh" reflect only the regular session, even when the assessment session is more recent). Added a regression test to `kentei-rank-auto-promotion.test.tsx` proving promotion still fires off assessment-only lacing. Verified with `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (full suite, after running `yarn install` in this worktree whose `node_modules` was missing on entry).

### [T056] Show the rank's trick list before skipping it in a Kentei assessment

- **Area:** Sesh (Kentei assessment)
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done

A user can't tell what they're giving up before tapping it. Implemented per the plan (`.claude/plans/T056.md`): `MultiTrickSesh` now holds a local `pendingSkip` state, splitting the old immediate `handleSkipRank` into `handleSkipRankRequest`/`handleConfirmSkip`/`handleCancelSkip`. A new `SkipRankConfirmation` panel (full-screen replace, same pattern as the existing `laceAcknowledgment` panel) lists the specific tricks `sequence.tricks.slice(fromIndex, targetIndex)` would discard, reusing `describeSkippedRange` for its heading when supplied. Canceling only clears local state — no dispatch, no `sequence`/`isFirstCountdown` mutation — so the run is provably unaffected. Confirming runs the same commit logic as before, unchanged. Updated `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx`: inserted a confirm-button press into every existing test that presses "Skip this rank", plus new coverage for the preview (lists tricks, records nothing yet) and cancel (dismisses, resumes exactly, subsequent completion still records). Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes.

- **Description:** While working through an assessment, nothing on screen shows which rank/class the user is currently on — `Countdown`/`ActiveSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) render a generic "Sesh in progress" eyebrow regardless of context, even though the trick objects flowing through already carry `kenteiRank`/`kenteiLevel`/`kenteiClass` (`TrickSummary`, via `selectAssessmentTricks`).
- **Exit criteria:** During an active/countdown trick within a Kentei assessment, the current rank/class is visibly rendered; regular single-trick Sesh and ad hoc multi-trick screens (where custom tricks may have no Kentei metadata at all) are unchanged.
- **Notes:** Added `isTrickSummary`/`formatKenteiStanding` to `src/features/tricks/model/trick.ts`. Threaded a new opaque `showRankStanding?: boolean` prop from `AssessmentEntry` → `MultiTrickSesh` → `Countdown`/`ActiveSesh` (same pattern as `attemptsReminder`/`onSkipRank`); inside `Countdown`/`ActiveSesh` it's combined with a runtime `isTrickSummary` narrow of the already-present `trick` prop and rendered as a new `RankStandingLabel` right after the `Eyebrow` in both. `MultiTrickSeshEntry` and the dev sandbox never pass the flag, so regular/ad hoc Sesh screens are unchanged by construction. Added unit tests for the two new `trick.ts` helpers, `Countdown`/`ActiveSesh` rank-standing rendering (including the defensive "flag true but no Kentei metadata" case), `MultiTrickSesh` forwarding, and an `AssessmentEntry` integration test asserting the label through countdown and active phases. Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes.

Verified in-browser: a Kentei assessment run (`hideTimer` true, no trick notes) now shows clear spacing between "You have 5 attempts." and the Laced button; a regular timed Sesh (timer visible) renders identically to before; `Countdown`'s `AttemptsReminder` placement (a separate render path, unaffected by this change) still looks correct above the countdown number. ### [T053] Add breathing room before auto-advancing on a lace

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** In ad hoc multi-trick practice (`MultiTrickSeshEntry`/`MultiTrickSesh`) with "advance after one lace" enabled, landing a trick and moving to the next happens in one synchronous beat (`trickLaced` then `seshEnded` dispatched back-to-back in `handleLaced`, `src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) — the user never sees an acknowledgment of the landing before the next trick's countdown appears. Insert a brief (roughly 1-3 second) "Trick landed"-style acknowledgment before advancing.
- **Exit criteria:** Test: with advance-on-lace enabled in ad hoc multi-trick practice, landing a trick shows acknowledgment copy for the configured window before the next trick's countdown begins.
- **Notes:** Implemented per the committed plan. Added `pauseAfterLace?: boolean` (default off) to `MultiTrickSesh` and a new component-local transient state, `laceAcknowledgment`, set from `handleLaced` instead of dispatching `seshEnded` immediately when both `advanceOnFirstLace` and `pauseAfterLace` are set. A `useEffect` keyed on `laceAcknowledgment` starts a single `setTimeout` (`LACE_ACKNOWLEDGMENT_DURATION_MS = 1500`, mirroring `Countdown`'s single-timeout-with-cleanup pattern) that clears the acknowledgment and dispatches the deferred `seshEnded`; the effect's cleanup clears the timeout on unmount/re-trigger. The `'active'` render branch shows a new panel (reusing the existing `CenteredPanel`/`Eyebrow`/`DisplayTitle`/`BodyCopy` primitives, `accessibilityLabel="Trick landed"`) in place of `ActiveSesh` while `laceAcknowledgment` is set. `MultiTrickSeshEntry` passes `pauseAfterLace={advanceOnFirstLace}`, tying the pause to the same checkbox rather than adding separate configuration. `AssessmentEntry` is untouched — it never passes `pauseAfterLace`, so the Kentei assessment flow keeps its deliberate zero-friction advance from T029.2/T029.4. No `SeshState` reducer changes; the delay is entirely local render state upstream of the existing `'completed'` effect. Added a new `describe('MultiTrickSesh pauseAfterLace', ...)` test block in `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` and forwarded the new prop through `mountMultiTrickSesh`; the existing `advanceOnFirstLace` tests (no `pauseAfterLace`) pass unmodified. Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes.
  Open question flagged, not resolved here: should the Kentei assessment flow eventually get a shorter/optional version of this acknowledgment, or is instant advance intentionally permanent there? This task makes no change to `AssessmentEntry`.

() => dispatch({ type: 'seshEnded', now: Date.now() }) : undefined`, using state already in scope at that call site. ### [T049] Split multi-trick sesh setup into a two-step wizard

Split it into two steps: pick tricks, then configure timing and start.

- **Notes:** Implemented per the committed plan. `MultiTrickSeshEntryStep` gained a `configuringRun` state between `choosingTricks` and `running`; `selectedTrickIds`/`advanceOnFirstLace`/`perTrickDurationMinutes` stayed as independent top-level `useState`, outside the step union, so Back preserves prior selection and timing values for free. Step 1's `TrickDirectory` footer now shows only the selection count and a `Continue` button (disabled at zero selected). Step 2 is a new, non-`TrickDirectory` render branch styled after `assessment-entry.tsx`'s `Content`/`Eyebrow`/`DisplayTitle`/`BodyText` pattern, carrying over the duration field and advance-on-lace row verbatim, plus `Start sesh` (same `handleStart`/disabled condition as before) and a `Back` button (`setStep({ kind: 'choosingTricks' })`, styled like `assessment-entry.tsx`'s `BackButton`). `handleFinished` still resets to `choosingTricks` and clears all three pieces of local state. Verified in-browser at `/sesh/multi-trick`: step 1 shows only the trick list/selection count/Continue; step 2 shows only timing/advance/Start/Back; Back preserves both the trick selection and the timing values entered; starting a timed run from step 2 launched the countdown with the entered duration. Also checked layout at a 320px viewport (nav and step-1 trick list render without clipping). Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes after running `yarn install` in this worktree, whose `node_modules` was missing on entry.

### [T048] Persist open-ended sesh target laces across sessions

- **Area:** Sesh / persistence (profile domain)
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** A target lace count defined for an open-ended sesh (T005.1/T005.2) only lives for the run it was set on — every fresh visit to Sesh setup resets the target field to blank, even though `lastSesh` (`src/features/profile/model/user-profile.ts`) already persists the last-used trick and plan across visits. Persist the target too, carrying it forward until the user changes it.
- **Exit criteria:** Test: set a target, complete (or end) an open-ended sesh, revisit Sesh setup fresh (not via "Repeat sesh") — the previously-set target pre-fills; changing it and starting again persists the new value on the next fresh visit; a profile with no persisted target (including all existing pre-fix profiles) behaves exactly as today, no migration needed.
- **Notes:** Implemented per the committed plan. `LastSeshPlan`'s `openEnded` variant gained an optional `targetLandingCount?: number`; `createLastSesh` now carries `plan.target.landingCount` through when present and otherwise still emits the bare `{ kind: 'openEnded' }` (no explicit `undefined` key) to keep serialized output for untargeted seshes unchanged. `isLastSesh` in `user-profile-persistence.ts` now accepts either the old 1-key `openEnded` shape or a 2-key shape validated with the existing `isValidSeshTargetLandingCount`, rejecting anything else. `src/app/sesh/index.tsx`'s `initialPlan` derivation now calls `createOpenEndedSeshPlan(lastSesh.plan.targetLandingCount)` instead of unconditionally falling back to the bare `OPEN_ENDED_SESH_PLAN` constant. No changes needed in `sesh.tsx` (already pre-fills from `initialPlan.target.landingCount`) or the profile provider. Added round-trip/malformed-value test coverage in `user-profile-persistence.test.ts` and new `createLastSesh` unit tests in `user-profile.test.ts`. Manually verified in-browser: seeding a profile with `targetLandingCount: 7` pre-fills the Sesh setup field; seeding one without it reproduces today's blank field. Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes. `node_modules` was missing on entry to this worktree; ran `yarn install` before testing.

### [T047] Allow backing out of the pre-sesh countdown

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** The 3-2-1 `Countdown` screen (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) that runs before a sesh goes active has no cancel control today — a user who starts a sesh by accident is stuck watching it complete. Add a way to back out during the countdown.
- **Exit criteria:** From the countdown screen, a visible Back/cancel control returns to trick/setup selection without recording a session — verified for single-trick Sesh (`src/app/sesh/index.tsx`) at minimum, dispatching the existing `seshReset` action (already unconditional in the reducer — `sesh.ts`'s `seshReset` case matches from any status, so this is UI-only wiring, not a reducer change).
- **Notes:** Implemented per the committed plan. `Countdown` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) gained an optional `onCancel?: () => void` prop, rendering a bordered `CancelButton` (reusing `Button`'s secondary variant) above the existing lower-emphasis "Skip this rank" link. Single-trick `Sesh` wires `onCancel={() => dispatch({ type: 'seshReset' })}` on its countdown-status render. Per the Notes' explicit "if picked up together" allowance, also wired the first-trick-only cancel into `MultiTrickSesh` (new `onCancelRun?: () => void` prop, gated by an `isFirstCountdown` piece of local state that flips to `false` on any post-first-countdown advance — normal completion or "Skip this rank" — so Cancel never reappears on later tricks), and its two callers: `multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx` (`onCancelRun={handleFinished}`, reusing the existing reset-to-picker handler since no session was recorded yet) and `assessment-entry.tsx` (`onCancelRun={() => setStep({ kind: 'choosingFlow' })}`). No reducer changes — `seshReset` was already unconditional. Added regression coverage: `sesh.test.tsx` (cancel mid-countdown returns to the trick picker with zero recorded sessions — required an `AsyncStorage.clear()` in that test's `beforeEach` after discovering cross-test practice-history leakage in the full suite run, not needed in earlier tests in the file), `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` (Cancel fires `onCancelRun` on the first trick countdown; does not render on the second trick's countdown after either normal completion or "Skip this rank"; does not render at all when `onCancelRun` is omitted), and `assessment-entry.test.tsx` (Cancel during the first trick's countdown returns to the flow picker). Manually verified in-browser per AGENTS.md: started a single-trick timed sesh, cancelled mid-countdown, confirmed Stats showed no phantom practice-history entry. `node_modules` was missing on entry to this worktree; ran `yarn install` before testing. Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes.

### [T038.1] Derive neglected focused tricks from practice history

- **Area:** Stats
- **Priority:** Medium (inherited from parent T038)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** Implement a pure selector that ranks focused tricks by practice neglect, using focused-list order as deterministic tie-breaking and limiting its result to three.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests confirm empty input, never-practiced tricks before practiced tricks, practiced tricks ordered by oldest recent practice, deterministic ties, unrelated practice records having no effect, and the three-item limit.
- **Notes:** Added `src/features/stats/model/neglected-tricks.ts` exporting `selectNeglectedFocusedTricks<T extends { id: string }>(focusedTricks, sessions)` and `MAX_NEGLECTED_TRICKS = 3`. Kept the input generic over any `{ id: string }`-shaped trick (rather than importing a specific home/tricks trick type into `stats`) so T038.2 can pass whatever trick shape it resolves focused tricks into and get the same object back in each result's `trick` field, alongside an optional `mostRecentPracticeAt` derived from `PracticeSessionRecord.completedAt`. Ranking: builds a `Map<trickId, mostRecentCompletedAt>` from sessions, pairs each focused trick with its recency and original index, sorts never-practiced before practiced, then by ascending `mostRecentPracticeAt`, with explicit index-based tie-breaking (not just relying on stable sort) so ties are deterministic by focused-list order for both never-practiced and equal-recency groups, then slices to `MAX_NEGLECTED_TRICKS`. No UI state consulted, nothing persisted — pure function over its two inputs. Added `src/features/stats/model/neglected-tricks.test.ts` covering all six exit-criteria cases (empty input, never-practiced-before-practiced, oldest-recent-practice-first ordering using multiple sessions per trick, deterministic ties across both never-practiced and equal-recency groups, unrelated trick IDs in history having no effect, and the three-item cap with five focused tricks). Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean via `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`). T038 stays open in `TASKS.md`/`Backlog` — T038.2 (rendering) is still Not started and the umbrella isn't done until both children are.

- **Area:** Home
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Correct the labels on Home practice summaries so they describe the rolling-window calculations currently implemented.
- **Exit criteria:** Tests assert unambiguous labels equivalent to "Last 24 hours," "Last 7 days," "Last 30 days," and "Last 365 days" while representative boundary calculations remain unchanged; the summary is verified in-browser at 320 px.
- **Notes:** Renamed the four `TIME_LOGGED_LABELS` entries in `src/features/home/components/daily-home.tsx` from calendar-ish copy ("Today"/"This week"/"This month"/"This year") to explicit rolling-window copy ("Last 24 hours"/"Last 7 days"/"Last 30 days"/"Last 365 days"), and exported the map so `src/features/home/components/daily-home.test.tsx` (new file) can assert on it directly without needing a router-aware full render (`DailyHome` renders `expo-router`'s `Link`, which `react-test-renderer`'s `create()` can't mount standalone). Did not touch the Momentum section's existing "Last seven days" eyebrow — already unambiguous — or `calculateTimeLogged`'s rolling-window math in `src/features/stats/model/time-logged.ts`, per the task's explicit non-goal; its existing boundary tests in `time-logged.test.ts` pass unchanged. Verified in-browser at a 320px viewport using a throwaway Playwright script (`npm install --no-save playwright`, reverted `yarn.lock` after) against an independent `yarn expo start --web --port 8093` server in this worktree, seeding `localStorage` with an onboarded profile and two practice-history records (one recent, one over a year old) to unlock all four gated windows simultaneously: confirmed all four labels ("Last 24 hours", "Last 7 days", "Last 30 days", "Last 365 days") render without text overflow or wrapping and the page has no horizontal overflow at that width. Full verification via `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test/lint/tsc/web-export) passed.

### [T001] Add laced count to trick list

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Trick list items display how many times each trick has been laced (landed), derived from practice history.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the trick list renders the correct laced count per trick from a given set of practice-history records; spot-checked in-browser against real data.
- **Notes:** Purely derived/display — no new persistence.

### [T002] Keep device awake during active sesh

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Prevent the device screen from sleeping while a sesh is in the active (or countdown) state; restore normal sleep behavior once the sesh ends or isn't active.
- **Exit criteria:** Keep-awake engages on entering the active sesh state and releases on leaving it — verified via a state-transition test; screen-stays-on behavior confirmed manually on-device, since actual screen-sleep isn't something an automated test can observe.
- **Notes:** Manual on-device check is a necessary supplement to the automated test here, not a substitute for it. Web-verified (Screen Wake Lock API) via the dev server across the full countdown → active → completed lifecycle, with no console errors; iOS/Android on-device screen-sleep behavior is still an open verification item consistent with the project's overall "web first" posture (see AGENTS.md § Verification and local development). Implementation: `shouldKeepScreenAwake` in `src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts` derives whether the screen should stay awake from Sesh status (countdown/active); `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` drives `expo-keep-awake`'s `activateKeepAwakeAsync`/`deactivateKeepAwake` off that derivation, tagged `sesh-active`, with `.catch` no-ops since wake-lock support/permission varies by browser and is a best-effort enhancement, not a blocker for practicing.

### [T003] Link directly into Sesh from any trick in Tricks list

- **Area:** Tricks / Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Add an action on each trick-list item that starts a Sesh containing that trick, regardless of whether it's currently in the user's focused/selected tricks.
- **Exit criteria:** Tapping the action for any trick — focused or not — navigates to Sesh with that trick loaded and ready to practice; verified in-browser for one focused and one unfocused trick.
- **Notes:** Check whether Sesh's entry points currently assume the trick is in `selectedTrickIds` (`src/app/sesh.tsx` / Sesh state machine) before implementing. Confirmed during implementation: `src/app/sesh.tsx` and `selectSeshPracticeTricks` in `src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts` already handle a `trickId` param independent of `selectedTrickIds` (the preferred trick is prepended even when not focused), so no Sesh-side changes were needed. Added a "Practice" row action to every trick in `TrickDirectory` (`src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx`), using an Expo Router `Link` to `/sesh?trickId=<id>`, for both standard and custom tricks, focused or not. Verified in-browser for a focused trick and, after unfocusing it, the same now-unfocused trick.

### [T004] Display trick unlocked status derived from lace history

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Trick list shows a locked/unlocked indicator per trick, where unlocked means at least one completed practice-history record exists for that trick ID.
- **Exit criteria:** A trick with ≥1 practice-history record renders unlocked; a trick with none renders locked — verified by test.
- **Notes:** Derived half only. T006 layers a manual override on top of this. Implementation: `selectUnlockedTrickIds` in `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts` derives the unlocked set from practice-history session `trickId`s (a trick counts as unlocked even if its only record has zero laces, same convention as the existing `selectLacedCountByTrickId`). `TrickDirectory` (`src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx`) takes an optional `unlockedTrickIds` prop and renders an "Unlocked"/"Locked" badge next to the trick name when it's supplied; `src/app/tricks.tsx` wires it from `usePracticeHistory()`. Verified by unit test and spot-checked in-browser against real practice history.

### [T005.1] Define a sesh-end target before starting

- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Open-ended sesh setup lets the user optionally define a target trick + landing count before starting.
- **Exit criteria:** Starting an open-ended sesh with a defined target stores it on the sesh's initial state; starting without one behaves exactly as today — verified by test.
- **Notes:** Parent [T005] stays `Not started` — [T005.2] (gating the end action on the target) is still outstanding. Implementation: `src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts` adds a `SeshTarget` (`{ landingCount }`) carried as an optional `target` on the `openEnded` `SeshPlan` variant, plus `createOpenEndedSeshPlan(landingCount?)` (mirrors `createTimedSeshPlan`'s validation pattern, 1–999 whole laces) and `isValidSeshTargetLandingCount`. No reducer changes were needed — `SeshPlan` already flows unchanged from `trickSelected` through to the `active`/`completed` states, so a defined target lands on the sesh's initial state for free. `TrickPicker` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` gained an optional "Target laces" field shown only for the open-ended format; leaving it blank reproduces today's untargeted plan exactly. T005.2 is left to read `state.plan.target` and `landings.length` to compute progress and gate the end action — nothing here computes or displays progress yet.

### [T005] End-of-sesh target criteria (open-ended flow)

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** In the open-ended sesh flow, let the user define a target before starting (land Trick X, Y times) and gate ending the sesh on meeting it, with an explicit override always available.
- **Notes:** Decision resolved during scoping — hard gate with override: the primary end action is disabled until the target is met, but an "end anyway" action is always present. Both children ([T005.1], [T005.2]) are now `Done`.

### [T005.2] Track progress and gate the end action

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Sesh tracks progress toward the defined target through the active session and gates the primary end action until it's met.
- **Exit criteria:** With an unmet target, the primary end action is disabled and a separate "end anyway" action ends the sesh; once the target is met, the primary end action is enabled — verified by test.
- **Notes:** Implementation: `src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts` adds two pure derivations — `getSeshTargetLandingCount(plan)` (the plan's target landing count, or `undefined` for timed/untargeted plans) and `isSeshTargetMet(plan, landingCount)` (always `true` when there's no target, so the UI can apply it unconditionally). No reducer changes — this is a UI-only gate; "end anyway" dispatches the same `seshEnded` action as the primary button. `ActiveSesh` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` computes `targetLandingCount`/`targetMet` from these, disables `EndButton` (`disabled`, `accessibilityState`, `$disabled` opacity, mirroring the existing `LacedButton`/`TrickChoice` pattern) until met, renders a progress readout (`TargetStatus`, modeled on `ChallengeStatus`'s boolean color swap) only when a target exists, and shows an always-functional "End anyway" text action beneath the secondary-actions row only while a target is set and unmet. Verified by new `sesh.test.ts` cases covering both derivations' boundaries (timed plans, untargeted open-ended, and a targeted plan at 0/9/10/11 against a target of 10), plus in-browser spot-check via the Expo dev server confirming the disabled → enabled transition and "End anyway" visibility.

### [T006] Manual trick-unlock override

- **Area:** Tricks / persistence
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Allow the user to manually mark a trick as unlocked independent of lace history, as a togglable flag layered on top of (not replacing) T004's lace-derived status.
- **Exit criteria:** Setting the manual flag on a trick with no lace history shows it unlocked; clearing the flag returns it to lace-derived status; a trick with real lace history stays unlocked regardless of the flag — verified by test.
- **Notes:** Decision resolved during scoping — toggle, not a one-way override. Implementation: `UserProfile` (`src/features/profile/model/user-profile.ts`) gains an optional `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` field plus `manualUnlockToggled`/`manualUnlockRemoved` reducer actions, mirroring the existing `selectedTrickToggled`/`selectedTrickRemoved` pattern; `removeManualUnlock` is wired into custom-trick deletion (`src/app/tricks.tsx`) alongside `removeSelectedTrick` so deleting a custom trick doesn't leave an orphaned flag. Persistence (`src/features/profile/persistence/user-profile-persistence.ts`) validates/round-trips the new field with the existing `isStringArray` helper, following the `lastSesh`/`streakThresholdMs` optional-field convention. `selectUnlockedTrickIds` (T004's lace-derived selector) is left untouched; a new `selectEffectiveUnlockedTrickIds` in `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts` unions the laced set with the manual list — plain set union, so it's a toggle, not a one-way override. `TrickDirectory` (`src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx`) gained an optional `manualUnlock` prop rendering a small "Manual" toggle next to the existing Unlocked/Locked badge, reflecting the raw manual flag rather than the effective unlocked status. Verified by unit tests for all three exit-criteria scenarios and in-browser spot-check on `/tricks`: toggling manual-unlock on an unlaced trick flipped its badge to Unlocked and back to Locked on clear, while a trick with real lace history stayed Unlocked through both toggle states.

### [T007] Auto-promote Kentei rank on completing current rank

- **Area:** Profile / Tricks
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** When the user has laced every trick in their currently assessed Kentei rank, update the profile's assessed rank to the next one automatically.
- **Exit criteria:** Lacing the last remaining trick in the current rank updates `profile.kenteiRank` to the next rank with no confirmation step — verified by test.
- **Notes:** Decision resolved during scoping — automatic and silent, not confirmation-gated. Implementation: `isKenteiRankComplete`/`selectPromotedKenteiRank` in `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts` are pure selectors — the latter cascades through multiple already-complete ranks in one call and never advances past the highest rank present in `KENDAMA_KENTEI_TRICKS`, checking only standard tricks (same precedent as `getTricksForRank`). `UserProfile` (`src/features/profile/model/user-profile.ts`) gains a `kenteiRankAdvanced` reducer action, exposed as `advanceKenteiRank` from `UserProfileProvider` following the existing action-creator pattern. Since `UserProfileProvider` sits above `PracticeHistoryProvider` in `src/app/_layout.tsx` and can't read practice history itself, the cross-domain reaction lives in a new non-rendering component, `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` (`src/features/profile/state/kentei-rank-auto-promotion.tsx`), rendered as a sibling of `AppTabs` inside `PracticeHistoryProvider` — it reads both providers via hooks and dispatches `advanceKenteiRank` in a `useEffect` only when the promoted rank differs from the current one. Verified by unit tests covering rank-1-minus-one-trick (no promotion), last-trick-laced (promotes), max-rank ceiling, and multi-rank cascade, plus the `kenteiRankAdvanced` reducer case; manually verified in-browser by seeding practice-history localStorage with laces for all five rank-1 tricks and confirming `profile.kenteiRank` flipped from 1 to 2 with the Profile screen's rank tile and the Tricks screen's rank grouping both reflecting rank 2 on reload, with no confirmation dialog.

### [T008] First-lace celebration

- **Area:** Sesh / Tricks
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** When a trick is laced for the first time ever (no prior practice-history record for that trick ID), show a distinct celebratory UI moment.
- **Exit criteria:** Completing a sesh that lands a trick with zero prior laces triggers the celebration UI; a trick with prior laces does not — verified by test.
- **Notes:** "Extra flashy" tiering by time/attempts spent before landing is a separate, unscoped idea — not in scope for this task. "First lace" is defined as no prior recorded session for the trick with `laceCount > 0` — a prior zero-lace record (e.g. an ended-early sesh with no laces) doesn't disqualify it. Implementation: `isFirstLaceForTrick(priorSessions, summary)` in `src/features/stats/model/practice-history.ts` is a pure predicate, doc-commented that `priorSessions` must be history as it stood before the current summary was recorded. `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` computes it once, in the existing sesh-completion `useEffect` (guarded by `recordedSummaryId` so it can't re-run once `sessions` includes the just-recorded session), storing the result in a `firstLaceCelebration` state flag — following the same "compute once at the transition, don't re-derive reactively" pattern already used for `personalBest`. The flag is cleared on both `onReset` and `onRepeat` so it doesn't leak into a subsequent sesh. `CompletedSesh` takes a new `isFirstLace` prop and renders a `FirstLaceBanner` (yellow card, red eyebrow, spring+opacity entrance reusing the `Countdown` component's animation pattern, own accessibility label `"First lace ever on {trick name}"`) above the existing headline when true; kept visually and conceptually distinct from `ResultBanner` (personal-best comparison, a different concept). Verified by new unit tests in `practice-history.test.ts` covering: no prior record, prior zero-lace record, prior record with laces, and a zero-landing summary — plus `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all passing.

### [T022] Prompt for a note at the end of every timed sesh

- **Area:** Sesh / Notes
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Extends [T010.2]. Today a note can only be added from the wrap-up screen of a timed sesh that landed nothing, which means the user has no way to capture a thought after a sesh that went well. Prompt on every completed timed sesh, with copy that differs depending on whether the trick was laced.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the prompt appears for a timed sesh with landings and for one without, with different copy for each, and that the note created carries the correct practice-session reference in both cases; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** `shouldPromptSeshNote` in `src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts` dropped its `landings.length === 0` condition — it now returns true for any `plan.kind === 'timed'` summary, regardless of outcome; open-ended seshes still never trigger it, per the explicit scoping decision from T010.2. `SeshNotePrompt` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` now branches its idle-step copy on `summary.landings.length > 0`: zero landings keeps the original "Didn't land it this time." / "what to try differently next sesh" copy, one-or-more landings gets new "Landed it." / "what worked this time?" copy. The four prompt buttons (idle's Add/Not-now, writing's Save/Cancel) gained distinct `accessibilityLabel`s ("Add a note", "Dismiss note prompt", "Save note", "Cancel note") so tests can select them unambiguously — previously only the celebration banner had a label to key off of. Fixed the now-broken `CompletedSesh first-lace celebration` tests, which rendered `<CompletedSesh>` with a landed timed summary but no `NoteProvider`; since `shouldPromptSeshNote` now also fires for that case, `SeshNotePrompt` mounts and calls `useNoteActions()`, which throws outside a provider. Wrapped both renders in `<NoteProvider>` using the async-hydration pattern from `kentei-rank-auto-promotion.test.tsx` (`await act(async () => { root = create(<NoteProvider>...) })`), switching those `it`s to async.
