Added a new `CompletedSesh note prompt` describe block in `sesh.test.tsx` covering laced vs. unlaced copy, plus a practice-session-reference test that drives the full idle → writing → save flow via accessibility labels and asserts the created note's `practiceSessionId` (read through a small `useNotes()`-backed sibling component, mirroring the Harness pattern in `kentei-rank-auto-promotion.test.tsx`) equals `createPracticeSessionRecordId(summary)` for both a laced and an unlaced sesh. `sesh.test.ts`'s "does not trigger for timed sesh with landings" test flipped to "triggers for a timed sesh that landed the trick at least once", asserting `true`; the open-ended-sesh test was left unchanged. Verified by updated/new unit tests in `sesh.test.ts` and `sesh.test.tsx`, `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web`, and an in-browser walkthrough of three flows: a 1-minute timed sesh with a lace (laced copy appears, note saves), a 1-minute timed sesh with no lace (no-lace copy appears), and an open-ended sesh (no prompt at all, as before).

### [T010.2] Auto-prompt for a note on a no-lace timed sesh

- **Area:** Tricks / persistence (new domain)
- **Priority:** Medium (inherited from parent T010)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** When a timed sesh completes without landing its trick, prompt the user to add a note, pre-linked to that sesh via its practice-history record ID. Depends on T010.1.
- **Exit criteria:** A timed sesh ending without a lace triggers the prompt, and a note created from it carries the correct sesh reference; a timed sesh that ends with a lace, and any open-ended sesh, never trigger the prompt — verified by test.
- **Notes:** The practice-history record ID was previously computed by an identical string template (`${startedAt}:${endedAt}:${trickId}`) independently in two places — `createPracticeSessionRecord` and an inline literal in `sesh.tsx`'s dedup-ref effect. Extracted into a single exported pure function, `createPracticeSessionRecordId(summary)` in `src/features/stats/model/practice-history.ts`, used by both `createPracticeSessionRecord` and the sesh note prompt below, with a unit test asserting the two stay in sync. Added `shouldPromptSeshNote(summary)` to `src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts` — a pure predicate, true only when `plan.kind === 'timed'` and `landings.length === 0`; false for every open-ended case (targeted, untargeted, with or without landings) and any timed sesh with at least one landing, covered by new unit tests in `sesh.test.ts`. Wired into `CompletedSesh` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`: when `shouldPromptSeshNote(summary)` is true, a new colocated `SeshNotePrompt` subcomponent renders below the landing history, following the file's existing pattern of colocating sesh-specific banners (`FirstLaceBanner`, `ResultBanner`) rather than a new file. `SeshNotePrompt` has local `idle → writing → saved` (or `dismissed`) state; on save it calls `useNoteActions().addNote` with `practiceSessionId: createPracticeSessionRecordId(summary)`, guaranteeing the note's sesh reference matches the practice-history record actually persisted for that sesh. No manual reset logic was needed — `CompletedSesh` unmounts whenever `state.status` leaves `'completed'`, so a fresh completion always starts the prompt from scratch. Both parent [T010] and this child are now `Done`. Verified by new unit tests in `practice-history.test.ts` and `sesh.test.ts`, `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all passing.

### [T010] Sesh/trick notes

- **Area:** Tricks / persistence (new domain)
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Let the user attach a note to a trick, optionally tied to the specific sesh that prompted it, without touching the immutability of practice-history records.
- **Notes:** Decisions resolved during scoping:
  - New standalone "Notes" domain (own reducer/validation/persistence adapter, per the existing per-concern pattern) — not merged into practice history or custom tricks.
  - A note requires a trick ID **and** a trick-name snapshot captured at write time (same convention practice-history already uses), so it survives a later rename or deletion of a custom trick. Standard Kentei tricks can't be deleted, so this only bites for custom tricks, but the record shape doesn't need to special-case that.
  - A note optionally carries a practice-history record ID, for notes born from a specific sesh (see T010.2). General trick notes omit it.
  - The Notes domain itself is **not** immutable — the immutability invariant protects Stats derivation specifically; notes don't feed any derived calculation, so editing/deleting a note later is fine.
  - Both children ([T010.1], [T010.2]) are now `Done`.

### [T009] Home-screen time logged (day/week/month/year)

- **Area:** Home / Stats
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Home screen shows total practice time logged for day, week, month, and year. A longer window only renders once enough real time has passed since the user's first recorded practice for that window to be meaningful.
- **Exit criteria:** With practice history spanning less than a window's natural period since the first record, that window is hidden; once enough real time has elapsed, it renders with the correct total — verified by test.
- **Notes:** Gated on elapsed real time since first use, not merely a record existing in the window. Thresholds measured from the first practice-history record: hide "week" before 7 days of history, "month" before ~30 days, "year" before ~365 days. Windows are rolling (last 24h/7d/30d/365d from `now`), not calendar-aligned — matches the existing 7-day "momentum" rolling window already in `home-summary.ts`. Implementation: `calculateTimeLogged(sessions, now)` in `src/features/stats/model/time-logged.ts` is a pure derivation returning only the windows that are unlocked — "day" always, "week"/"month"/"year" gated on `now - firstRecordAt >= windowMs` where `firstRecordAt` is the earliest session ever recorded (not merely the earliest inside that window — a session more than a year old still unlocks the year gate even though it no longer contributes to the year total). `createHomeSummary` exposes the result as `HomeSummary.timeLoggedWindows`; `home-summary.ts`'s existing `SEVEN_DAYS_MS` momentum constant was replaced with the shared `WEEK_MS` export to remove the duplicate. `DailyHome` (`src/features/home/components/daily-home.tsx`) renders a new "Time on the string" section between the practice queue and the Momentum section, mapping each window in the array to a labeled tile (Today/This week/This month/This year) via `formatDuration` — no gating logic in the UI layer, since the model only ever returns unlocked windows. Verified by new unit tests in `time-logged.test.ts` (including an exact-boundary case per window and the "unlocks from the true first record even when that record has aged out of the window" case) plus end-to-end cases through `createHomeSummary` in `home-summary.test.ts`; `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass.

### [T010.1] Notes domain and basic note CRUD on a trick

- **Area:** Tricks / persistence (new domain)
- **Priority:** Medium (inherited from parent T010)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Add the Notes persistence domain and a minimal UI to create, view, edit, and delete a note attached to a trick, with an optional sesh reference.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms: creating a trick-only note stores the trick ID and name snapshot; creating one with a sesh reference also stores the practice-history record ID; editing and deleting both work; renaming or deleting the source custom trick afterward leaves the note displaying its snapshotted name rather than breaking.
- **Notes:** New standalone Notes domain, own reducer/validation/persistence adapter, mirroring `src/features/custom-tricks/`: `src/features/notes/model/note.ts` (`Note`, `NoteCollection`, `validateNoteCreation`/`validateNoteEdit`, `createNote`/`createNoteId`, `noteReducer`, `selectNotesForTrick`), `src/features/notes/persistence/note-persistence.ts` (versioned `daily-dama:notes` storage key, duplicate-id rejection, malformed-document recovery), and `src/features/notes/state/note-provider.tsx` (`NoteProvider`/`useNotes`/`useNoteActions`, same hydration-fallback and write-queue pattern as `CustomTrickProvider`), wired into `src/app/_layout.tsx` inside `PracticeHistoryProvider`. `NoteCreationInput` requires a non-empty `trickId` and `trickName` snapshot and treats `practiceSessionId` as optional — general trick notes (this task) omit it; sesh-linked notes are T010.2's job and out of scope here. UI: `src/features/notes/components/trick-notes-screen.tsx` (`TrickNotesScreen`) lists a trick's notes newest-first with inline add/edit forms and an inline (non-`Alert`) delete confirmation on the row, matching the app's existing no-`Alert.*` convention. `src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx` gained an optional `notesAction` prop rendering a "Notes" row action for every trick — standard and custom alike, deliberately independent of the custom-trick-only `customActions` gate. `src/app/tricks.tsx` wires a `notesTrick` screen-swap state alongside the existing `editingTrick`/`deletingTrick` pattern. Because a deleted custom trick has no more directory row to open, the "note survives trick deletion" claim is verified at the model layer only (`note.test.ts`'s byte-for-byte-unchanged case after `customTrickReducer` rename + delete on the separate custom-tricks state) rather than via a live post-deletion UI path — deliberately out of scope per the plan. Verified by new unit tests in `note.test.ts` and `note-persistence.test.ts`, `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web`, and an in-browser walkthrough on `/tricks` confirming add/edit/delete all work through the "Notes" row action.

### [T011] Custom practice goals

- **Area:** Tricks / persistence (new domain)
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Let the user define a standing goal for a specific trick — either a cumulative lace-count target ("land Whirlwind 25 times") or a cumulative practice-time target ("practice Whirlwind for 30 minutes") — with progress tracked automatically across sessions from practice history.
- **Notes:** Umbrella entry; done now that both [T011.1] and [T011.2] are Done. Decisions resolved during scoping: no streak/"in a row" goal type (no failed-attempt tracking, deliberately); "generic improvement goals" resolved to the time-based variant of the same per-trick mechanic, not a separate category; both goal types scoped to a single trick for v1; progress derived from practice history at read time (sum of landings, or sum of sesh duration, for that trick ID), not a separately-maintained running counter; a goal record carries a trick-name snapshot alongside the trick ID, same convention as T010's Notes.

### [T011.1] Goals domain and goal-creation UI

- **Area:** Tricks / persistence (new domain)
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T011)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Add a persistent Goals domain, and UI to create a goal for a trick: either a target lace count or a target cumulative practice time.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms creating a count-based goal and a time-based goal each store the trick ID, trick-name snapshot, goal type, and target value correctly; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** New standalone Goals domain mirroring `src/features/notes/`: `src/features/goals/model/goal.ts` defines `Goal` as a discriminated union on `kind: 'count' | 'time'` (`targetCount` vs. `targetMs`, matching the `durationMs` convention used elsewhere rather than reusing Sesh's per-session bounds), with its own generous bounds (`MIN/MAX_GOAL_TARGET_COUNT` 1–100,000, `MIN/MAX_GOAL_TARGET_MINUTES` 1–100,000) since a cumulative goal isn't capped like a single sesh. `validateGoalCreation`, `createGoal`/`createGoalId`, `goalReducer` (idempotent add, plus delete), and `selectGoalsForTrick` all follow the Notes domain's exact shape. `src/features/goals/persistence/goal-persistence.ts` (versioned `daily-dama:goals` storage key) additionally rejects a record carrying both or neither of `targetCount`/`targetMs` for its `kind`. `src/features/goals/state/goal-provider.tsx` (`GoalProvider`/`useGoals`/`useGoalActions`) uses the same hydration-fallback/write-queue pattern, wired into `src/app/_layout.tsx` alongside `NoteProvider`. UI: `src/features/goals/components/trick-goals-screen.tsx` (`TrickGoalsScreen`) lists a trick's goals newest-first with an inline delete confirmation, and an add form with a two-option kind picker (reusing the radio-toggle pattern from `tricks.tsx`'s Kentei-rank picker) whose numeric field label/placeholder and validation switch between "Target lace count" and "Target minutes"; target display formats time goals via `formatDuration` from `src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts`. `src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx` gained an optional `goalsAction` prop rendering a "Goals" row action for every trick, independent of the custom-trick-only `customActions` gate, following the existing `notesAction` prop exactly. `src/app/tricks.tsx` wires a `goalsTrick` screen-swap state alongside `notesTrick`. No progress/achievement display here — that's explicitly T011.2's job. Verified by new unit tests in `goal.test.ts` (including the trick-rename/delete-survival case, mirroring Notes) and `goal-persistence.test.ts`, `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web`, and an in-browser walkthrough on `/tricks` confirming both a count-based and a time-based goal can be created via the "Goals" row action, display correctly, and survive a page reload.

### [T011.2] Goal progress and achievement

- **Area:** Tricks / persistence
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T011)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Compute each goal's progress by deriving it from practice history (summed landings, or summed sesh duration, for that trick), and mark the goal achieved once the target is reached.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms a count-based goal's progress reflects the trick's total lace count from history and marks achieved once it reaches the target; a time-based goal's progress reflects cumulative sesh duration for that trick and marks achieved the same way.
- **Notes:** Added `selectGoalProgress(goal, sessions)` to `src/features/goals/model/goal.ts` — a pure derivation (no new persisted field, keeping the existing "derive, don't cache" principle) that filters `PracticeSessionRecord`s by `goal.trickId`, sums `laceCount` for `kind: 'count'` goals or `durationMs` for `kind: 'time'` goals, and returns `{ goal, current, target, achieved }` with `achieved: current >= target`. Wired into `src/features/goals/components/trick-goals-screen.tsx`: `TrickGoalsScreen` now reads `usePracticeHistory()` and maps each trick goal through `selectGoalProgress` before rendering; `GoalRow` takes the derived `GoalProgress` instead of a raw `Goal` and renders `current / target` (laces, or `formatDuration` for time goals) plus an "Achieved" badge (styled like `RankBadge` in `trick-card.tsx`) once the target is reached. Verified by new unit tests in `goal.test.ts` (count-goal and time-goal progress below/at target, plus a zero-matching-sessions case, all with a foreign-trick session mixed in to prove filtering), `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web`, and an in-browser walkthrough on `/tricks` → Big cup → Goals confirming both goal kinds render live progress from practice history. Parent [T011] is now `Done` — both children complete.

### [T012] Multi-trick sequential practice (shared core)

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** A sequencing layer that steps through an ordered list of tricks one at a time, reusing the existing single-trick practice mechanics for each trick (land it, or move on without landing), auto-advancing without requiring the user to manually restart between tricks. Each trick's outcome is recorded as its own practice-history record, exactly as today's single-trick Sesh already does — no change to the practice-history record shape.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms that, given an ordered list of 3 tricks, landing or moving past each in turn produces 3 separate practice-history records in the same order, and the run ends after the last trick — verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** New `src/features/sesh/model/trick-sequence.ts` is a pure, React-free ordering helper (`createTrickSequence`, `getCurrentSequenceTrick`, `isTrickSequenceFinished`, `advanceTrickSequence`) with its own unit tests in `trick-sequence.test.ts`. `Countdown` and `ActiveSesh` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` were exported (no other change to that file, so regular single-trick Sesh behavior and its reducer are untouched) and reused by a new sibling component, `src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx` (`MultiTrickSesh`), which owns its own `useReducer(seshReducer, …)` instance seeded straight into `countdown` for the first trick (skipping the picker — the trick list and plan are already given), records each completed trick via the same `recordCompletedSesh`/`createPracticeSessionRecordId`-guard pattern as `Sesh`, then advances the sequence and either dispatches `trickSelected` for the next trick or calls `onFinished?.()` once the sequence is finished; also mirrors `Sesh`'s `shouldKeepScreenAwake` wiring. An empty trick list is handled as an immediately-finished run (calls `onFinished` once, guarded by a ref, rather than rendering the countdown/active states with nothing to show). Dev-only manual-verification harness added at `src/app/stats/sesh-sequence-sandbox.tsx` (same `__DEV__`-gated, `stats/`-nested pattern as `trick-viz-sandbox.tsx`), linked from Home (`src/app/index.tsx`) next to the existing trick-viz sandbox link. Verified by the new unit tests, `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web`, and an in-browser walkthrough on `/stats/sesh-sequence-sandbox` (`yarn` dev server) stepping through 3 tricks — landing the 1st and 3rd, skipping the 2nd — confirming no 4th countdown appeared and, via `JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('daily-dama:practice-history')).sessions`, that exactly 3 new records landed in order (`big-cup` laceCount 1, `small-cup` laceCount 0, `base-cup` laceCount 1).

### [T013] Kentei assessment

- **Area:** Sesh / Profile
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done — umbrella entry; both children (T013.1, T013.2) complete.
- **Description:** Formal rank assessment built on T012's shared sequencing core. Three entry flows: full bottom-to-top through all ranks, bottom-to-top starting at a chosen rank, or the user's current rank only. Ranks can be skipped during a run. Each trick shows a static "you have 5 attempts" reminder — no attempt counting or fail state in the app.
- **Notes:** Decisions resolved during scoping:
  - Built on T012, not a reimplementation of practice mechanics.
  - Landings during assessment write to the same practice-history domain as regular practice — T007's existing auto-promotion logic fires naturally on a full rank pass; no assessment-specific promotion wiring needed.
  - Skipping a rank leaves it unassessed — not treated as passed, doesn't touch the user's recorded rank.
  - Must be completed in one sitting for v1 — no persisted in-progress assessment state.
  - "5 attempts" is purely informational text, not a counted or enforced limit — there is no "failed attempt" input anywhere in this feature.

### [T013.1] Assessment entry flows and trick-list construction

- **Area:** Sesh / Profile
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T013)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Entry UI to choose one of the three flows (full bottom-to-top, bottom-to-top from a chosen rank, current rank only), and logic to build the correct ordered trick list to hand to T012 from that choice.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms each of the three flows produces the correct ordered trick list — e.g. current-rank-only produces exactly that rank's tricks in order, full bottom-to-top produces every rank's tricks concatenated in rank order — verified in-browser for at least one flow.
- **Notes:** New `src/features/sesh/model/kentei-assessment-run.ts` defines `AssessmentFlow` (`fullBottomToTop` / `fromRank` / `currentRankOnly`) and `selectAssessmentTricks`, a pure filter over `KENDAMA_KENTEI_TRICKS` (already flat and rank-ordered) that never reorders, plus `selectAssessmentRankBounds` deriving min/max rank from the data itself rather than hardcoding 1/12, mirroring `selectPromotedKenteiRank`'s convention in `trick-directory.ts`. Deliberately named and scoped apart from the unrelated, pre-existing `src/features/onboarding/model/kentei-assessment.ts` (the one-time self-declared placement quiz) to avoid confusing the two. New `src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx` (`AssessmentEntry`) is a local 3-step state machine (`choosingFlow` → optional `choosingRank` → `running`) that, once a flow is chosen, calls `selectAssessmentTricks` and renders `<MultiTrickSesh>` (T012) with `OPEN_ENDED_SESH_PLAN`, calling `router.replace('/profile')` on finish; "Current rank only" reads `useUserProfile().kenteiRank` directly. Landings recorded during the run flow through `MultiTrickSesh`'s existing `recordCompletedSesh` path, so T007's auto-promotion logic fires with no extra wiring. Route wiring converted `src/app/profile.tsx` into a directory (`profile/index.tsx` + `profile/_layout.tsx`, mirroring the `stats/` precedent) and added `profile/kentei-assessment.tsx`, gated by `useRequireOnboarding()`; `profile/index.tsx` gained a distinctly-labeled "Start Kentei assessment" entry point alongside the pre-existing "Retake assessment" button (which stays wired to the unrelated onboarding-quiz `restartOnboarding()`). Verified by new unit tests in `kentei-assessment-run.test.ts` covering all three flows plus the max-rank edge case; `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass; and an in-browser walkthrough on `/profile` → "Start Kentei assessment" → "Current rank only" (rank 2) running the full 5-trick sequence (Baseball, Easy big cup/Spike, Small cup/Big cup, Moshikame x10, Airplane — the exact declared order for rank 2), confirming the run ends by replacing the URL to `/profile` and, via `localStorage.getItem('daily-dama:practice-history')`, that exactly 5 new practice-history records landed in that same order.

### [T013.2] Rank skipping and attempt messaging

- **Area:** Sesh / Profile
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T013)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** During a bottom-to-top run, let the user skip an entire rank without attempting it; display the static "5 attempts" reminder per trick during the run. Depends on T013.1.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms skipping a rank advances to the next rank's tricks without writing any practice-history records for the skipped tricks and without affecting the user's kentei rank; the attempt-count message renders per trick — verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Added `advanceTrickSequenceToIndex(state, index)` to `src/features/sesh/model/trick-sequence.ts` — a rank-agnostic "jump forward" primitive alongside `advanceTrickSequence`, clamped so it never moves backward from `currentIndex` and never past `tricks.length`. Added `selectNextRankStartIndex(tricks, fromIndex)` to `src/features/sesh/model/kentei-assessment-run.ts` — the one function that knows about `kenteiRank`; it scans forward from `fromIndex` for the first strictly-greater rank (relying on `selectAssessmentTricks`' guaranteed ascending-rank order) and returns `tricks.length` once there's no higher rank left. `MultiTrickSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) gained two new optional props — `skipTarget?: (currentIndex: number) => number` and `showAttemptsReminder?: boolean` — and a `handleSkipRank` handler that jumps the sequence via `advanceTrickSequenceToIndex` and dispatches `trickSelected` for the next trick or `seshReset` when the run is over; critically, it never dispatches `seshEnded`/`timerElapsed`, so the `'completed'` branch that calls `recordCompletedSesh` is never reached for a skipped rank's tricks, and `seshReset` reuses the existing empty-sequence effect (which calls `onFinished()`) rather than adding new finish-handling logic. `Countdown` and `ActiveSesh` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` gained matching optional `attemptsReminder`/`onSkipRank` props, both absent/hidden by default so the single-trick `Sesh` (which doesn't pass them) is unaffected; a static "You have 5 attempts." line renders near the trick's sequence steps, and a low-visual-weight "Skip this rank" text-button (styled like the existing `EndAnywayButton`) renders when `onSkipRank` is supplied. `src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx` wires `showAttemptsReminder` unconditionally (applies to all three flows) and `skipTarget` only for `fullBottomToTop`/`fromRank` (not `currentRankOnly`, where "skip this rank" would just mean abandoning the only rank in the run). `MultiTrickSesh`'s prop signature stays `PracticeTrick[]`-based with no `kenteiRank` dependency — `skipTarget` is opaque to it — so `src/app/stats/sesh-sequence-sandbox.tsx` (which doesn't pass the new props) is unaffected. Verified by new unit tests in `trick-sequence.test.ts` (jump-forward, clamp-at-end, no-backward-movement) and `kentei-assessment-run.test.ts` (mid-rank skip lands on next rank's first trick, off-by-one check from the second-to-last trick of a rank, last-rank skip returns `tricks.length`, past-the-end `fromIndex` also returns `tricks.length`); `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass; and an in-browser walkthrough on `/profile/kentei-assessment` confirming: "You have 5 attempts." renders on both the countdown and active screens; starting a full assessment (rank 1) and skipping without lacing landed on rank 2's first trick ("Baseball") with Stats (`/stats`) totals unchanged (5 total laces, 11 seshes, Big cup still 3 laces/4 seshes — identical to the pre-skip baseline) and the profile's assessed Kentei rank still showing 02; and starting from rank 12 and skipping landed the run at `/profile` (via the `seshReset` → `onFinished` path), exercising the "no next trick" branch explicitly.

### [T014] Multi-trick sesh variant

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** A casual practice mode built on T012's shared sequencing core, where the user selects an arbitrary set of tricks (not rank-based) to step through in one run.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms selecting N arbitrary tricks and running them through the sequencing core produces N practice-history records, one per trick attempted — verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** New `src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx` (`MultiTrickSeshEntry`) holds a local 2-step state machine (`choosingTricks` → `running`) with its own local `useState<string[]>` selection state — deliberately not the persisted "focused tricks" list on the user profile (`selectedTrickIds`/`toggleSelectedTrick`), since that list drives the separate single-trick Sesh picker and reusing its actions would silently mutate the user's persistent practice list on every ad hoc run. It renders `TrickDirectory` (reusing the existing trick-selection UI, `showFocusedSection` omitted) for selection, then hands the chosen tricks — built via `selectSeshPracticeTricks(selectAvailablePracticeTricks(...), selectedTrickIds)` — to `MultiTrickSesh` (T012) with `OPEN_ENDED_SESH_PLAN`, resetting to `choosingTricks` with cleared selection on `onFinished`. `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` gained one small addition in `TrickPicker`: a "Or work through several tricks in a row." link to the new route. Deviation from the plan: the plan specified `src/app/sesh-multi-trick.tsx` as a flat top-level route file, but the app's tab navigation (`src/components/app-tabs.tsx` for native, `app-tabs.web.tsx` for web) only knows about routes nested under its five declared top-level tab triggers (`index`, `sesh`, `tricks`, `stats`, `profile`) — a flat sibling file with no matching trigger silently redirects to Home on direct navigation (confirmed both on the "sesh-multi-trick" name itself and by testing that a `hidden` extra `NativeTabs.Trigger` didn't fix it either). The existing `profile/kentei-assessment.tsx` precedent (nested inside a `profile/` directory with its own `_layout.tsx` Stack under the `profile` tab trigger) was the correct pattern instead, so `src/app/sesh.tsx` was converted to `src/app/sesh/index.tsx` + a new `src/app/sesh/_layout.tsx` (identical `Stack screenOptions={{ headerShown: false }}` to `profile/_layout.tsx`), and the new screen became `src/app/sesh/multi-trick.tsx` (route `/sesh/multi-trick`) — all existing `href="/sesh"` references elsewhere in the app were unaffected since they still resolve to `sesh/index.tsx`. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all pass, `/sesh/multi-trick` present in the static route list), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: from `/sesh`, clicked through to `/sesh/multi-trick`, selected 2 tricks (Big cup, Small cup) spanning the only rank available in this profile's unlocked set, confirmed "Start sesh" was disabled at 0 selections and showed "2 tricks selected", ran the sesh ending the first trick immediately via "End Sesh" with 0 laces and lacing the second once before ending, confirmed no 3rd countdown appeared and the flow returned to the trick-choosing screen with selection cleared, and via `JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('daily-dama:practice-history')).sessions` confirmed the session count went from 11 to 13 — exactly 2 new records (`big-cup` laceCount 0, `small-cup` laceCount 1). Also confirmed the persisted `selectedTrickIds` on the profile (`["big-cup"]`) was unchanged after the run, verifying the local-selection-state design decision held.

### [T015] Hide the Practice row action outside the Tricks route

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Corrects [T003]. `TrickDirectory` renders the "Practice" link for every row unconditionally (`src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx`), so it also appears in the onboarding selection step and the multi-trick lineup picker, where deep-linking into a single-trick Sesh mid-flow makes no sense. Gate it behind an opt-in prop the way `notesAction`/`goalsAction` already are.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms `TrickDirectory` renders no Practice action when the new prop is absent and one per row when it's supplied; verified in-browser that `/tricks` still shows Practice while the onboarding selection step and `/sesh/multi-trick` show none.
- **Notes:** Follow the existing optional-action prop convention rather than adding a boolean `hidePractice` — the three other row actions (`customActions`, `notesAction`, `goalsAction`) are all absent-by-default, and Practice being the one opt-out is exactly what caused this. Only `src/app/tricks.tsx` should pass it. Implementation matched the plan exactly: `TrickDirectoryProps` gained `readonly practiceAction?: true` (presence-gated, not a boolean, so an accidental `practiceAction={false}` is a compile error rather than a silent no-op), threaded through `extraData` and into `TrickRow` as `showPracticeAction={Boolean(practiceAction)}`, which now gates the existing `<Link>`-wrapped Practice `RowAction` the same way `onOpenNotes`/`onOpenGoals` already gate their own actions. `src/app/tricks.tsx` is the only call site passing `practiceAction` (bare shorthand, matching `hideScrollIndicator`/`showFocusedSection` boolean-prop style already in that file); `multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx` and `onboarding-selection-step.tsx` needed no changes since they never passed the prop. New `src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.test.tsx` is the first component-render test in the repo, using `react-test-renderer` (`@testing-library/react-native` is not installed and wasn't added); `expo-router`'s `<Link>` rendered cleanly under it with no router-context mocking needed. One adjustment beyond the plan: `root.root.findAll` matches every fiber along the tree with a given prop, not just host elements, so counting `Practice ` `accessibilityLabel` matches required filtering to `typeof node.type === 'string'` (host nodes only) to avoid over-counting composite wrappers; without it, 3 fixture tricks with `practiceAction` supplied incorrectly counted 12 matches instead of 3. `describe`/`expect`/`it` are imported explicitly from `@jest/globals` (matching the convention in every existing `.test.ts` file in this repo) rather than relying on ambient Jest globals, which `tsc --noEmit` doesn't pick up by default here. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all pass), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: `/tricks` shows a "Practice" action on every row; `/sesh/multi-trick` shows none; and, after clearing `localStorage` to re-trigger onboarding, clicking through intro → Basic skill level → "Not yet" on the first assessment trick → assessment-complete rank reveal → "Choose tricks" landed on the onboarding selection step ("Choose what to work on first."), which also shows no Practice action on any row.

### [T016.2] Passive laced treatment on trick rows

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T016)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Replace the LOCKED/UNLOCKED badge and the MANUAL toggle in the trick row with a whole-row treatment: a laced row gets a tinted background plus a left rule, and its position number is replaced by a check glyph. An unlaced row renders as it did before any of this landed — no badge, no toggle, and no "0 laces" meta line. Depends on [T016.1].
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms a laced trick row renders the laced treatment and no badge/toggle element, an unlaced row renders neither the treatment nor a laced-count line, and a trick with ≥1 lace still renders its count; verified in-browser at 375 px that no row wraps or overlaps (the failure in the T004 screenshot).
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `TrickDirectoryProps.manualUnlock` deleted entirely (marking now lives in `LacedTricksScreen` from T016.1); `unlockedTrickIds` kept, now driving row rendering directly. `TrickRowProps` lost `showUnlockedStatus`, `manuallyUnlocked`, `onToggleManualUnlock`, and `showLacedCount`; `isUnlocked` renamed to `isLaced` to match `LacedTricksScreen`'s terminology. The `UnlockBadge` and `ManualUnlockToggle` (+ its text) styled components and JSX were deleted outright. `TrickMetaRow`'s gate changed from `isCustomDirectoryTrick(trick) || showLacedCount` to `isCustomDirectoryTrick(trick) || lacedCount > 0`, so the laced-count line now depends only on there being a nonzero count. `Position` renders `'✓'` when `isLaced`, else the zero-padded index as before, with a new `$laced` styled prop swapping its color from red to the ink-green (`#274137`) used elsewhere for the laced state, while keeping `width: 26px` fixed so the glyph swap can't change row width. `TrickRowContainer` gained a `$laced` prop: background tints to `#e5edcf` when `$selected || $laced` (previously `$selected` only), plus a `4px` left border in `#274137` when laced (`0px`/`transparent` otherwise), following the conditional-border idiom already used in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`. `src/app/tricks.tsx` dropped its `manualUnlock={{ manuallyUnlockedTrickIds, onToggle: toggleManualUnlock }}` prop to `TrickDirectory`; both `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` and `toggleManualUnlock` stay in use elsewhere in that file (deriving `unlockedTrickIds`, wiring `LacedTricksScreen`), so no unused-var fallout. Added three new tests to `src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.test.tsx`: a laced trick with a nonzero count renders the check glyph and count text with no `'Locked'`/`'Unlocked'` text and no `accessibilityRole="switch"` node; an unlaced trick with a zero count renders none of that; both render without a `selection` prop so `SelectionMark`'s own `'✓'` (only present when `onToggle` is supplied) can't collide with the laced `Position` glyph when asserting on `'✓'`. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all pass), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server at 375px width on `/tricks`: unlaced rows render with no badge, no toggle, and no laced-count line, with no row wrapping or overlapping (the T004 regression this task fixes); also confirmed at 320px that the top nav (Home/Sesh/Tricks/Stats/Profile) still fits on one line, unaffected by this change.

### [T016.1] Laced-tricks grid screen

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T016)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** A new screen reachable from the Tricks route via a "Laced tricks" action, rendering tricks as compact cards flowing left-to-right (wrapping rows, not one-per-line), each showing only its name and a laced/unlaced state. Tapping a card toggles it.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms tapping an unlaced card dispatches the manual-unlock toggle for that trick ID and the card re-renders laced, and that a trick with lace history renders laced without any manual flag; verified in-browser that the toggle survives a page reload.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. New `src/features/tricks/components/laced-tricks-screen.tsx` exports `LacedTricksScreen`, a presentational component (no provider reads) taking `tricks`, `unlockedTrickIds` (the already-effective lace ∪ manual set), `onToggleTrick`, and `onClose` — mirroring `TrickNotesScreen`/`TrickGoalsScreen`. It reuses `createTrickSections(tricks)` (no `focusedTrickIds`) from `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts` for rank grouping rather than reimplementing it, and renders each section as a lightweight header followed by a `flex-direction: row; flex-wrap: wrap` card grid inside a plain `ScrollView` (no `SectionList`). Each card shows only the trick name and Laced/Unlaced state, with `accessibilityRole="button"` and an `accessibilityLabel` of `"{name}, laced|unlaced"`; tapping always calls `onToggleTrick(trick.id)` unconditionally, including for lace-history tricks (a no-op-looking toggle there, left undisabled per the plan). Wired into `src/app/tricks.tsx` as a new `showLacedTricks` boolean screen-swap branch (after `goalsTrick`, before `creationStep === 'choose'`), passing the same `directoryTricks`/`unlockedTrickIds`/`toggleManualUnlock` already computed for `TrickDirectory`. The `headerAction` slot on `TrickDirectory` now wraps `DefineButton` and a new `LacedTricksButton` (`variant="secondary"`, label "Laced tricks") in a small wrapping row, touching only `tricks.tsx`'s own styled-components block. New `src/features/tricks/components/laced-tricks-screen.test.tsx` follows the `trick-directory.test.tsx` convention (`react-test-renderer` + `act`, no providers): one test builds a stateful harness (`useState` for `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds`, deriving `unlockedTrickIds` via `selectEffectiveUnlockedTrickIds` from an empty lace-history set, wiring `onToggleTrick` the way `tricks.tsx` does) to confirm tapping an unlaced card's `onPress` dispatches the trick's ID and the card re-renders laced; the other renders `LacedTricksScreen` directly with a trick ID present only in `unlockedTrickIds` (simulating lace history, absent from any manual list) and confirms it renders laced. One deviation from the plan's test sketch: finding a card by `accessibilityLabel` plus `typeof node.type === 'string'` (as `trick-directory.test.tsx` does for counting) matched a non-Pressable ancestor without an `onPress`, so the finder was changed to match on `accessibilityLabel` plus `typeof node.props.onPress === 'function'` instead, which reliably resolves to the host `Pressable`. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all pass), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: completed onboarding, opened `/tricks`, clicked "Laced tricks", confirmed Rank 1/Rank 2 cards wrap left-to-right in a grid (not one-per-line), tapped the unlaced "Small cup" card and watched it flip to laced, then hard-reloaded `/tricks` and re-opened "Laced tricks" to confirm "Small cup" was still laced — the persistence leg of the exit criteria, riding on `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` already round-tripping through `UserProfileProvider`'s AsyncStorage write queue with no changes needed there.

### [T017] Seed laced state from onboarding

- **Area:** Onboarding / Profile
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Onboarding already asks the user, trick by trick, whether they've landed it — but throws those answers away. Carry them into the profile so a user who answered "landed it" during onboarding starts with those tricks laced, and a user who started at an advanced level starts with every trick below their starting rank laced.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the onboarding reducer accumulates the trick IDs answered "landed it", and that completing onboarding at a starting rank above the minimum persists every standard trick below that rank plus every landed trick as manually unlocked — verified in-browser by completing onboarding at an advanced level and checking the laced grid.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `KenteiAssessmentState`'s `assessing`/`rankReveal`/`selecting` variants each gained `startingRank`/`landedTrickIds` fields in `src/features/onboarding/model/kentei-assessment.ts`; `levelSelected` seeds `startingRank` from the chosen level's first rank with an empty `landedTrickIds`, `trickLanded` looks up the current trick via `getCurrentAssessmentTrick(state.rank, state.trickIndex)` before advancing and appends its `id`, and `trickNotLanded`/`rankRevealContinued` carry both fields through unchanged. Added `getSeedLacedTrickIds(startingRank, landedTrickIds)` in the same file — unions every standard trick below `startingRank` with the explicitly landed IDs, de-duplicated via `Set`. `UserProfileAction`'s `onboardingCompleted` gained a `lacedTrickIds` field; the reducer branch in `src/features/profile/model/user-profile.ts` unions it into `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` (via `Set`) rather than overwriting, so a restart-then-recomplete of onboarding can't regress previously-laced tricks. `UserProfileProvider.completeOnboarding` (`src/features/profile/state/user-profile-provider.tsx`) gained a third `lacedTrickIds` parameter passed straight into the dispatch; its one call site, `KenteiOnboarding`'s `onFinish` in `src/features/onboarding/components/kentei-onboarding.tsx`, now computes `getSeedLacedTrickIds(state.startingRank, state.landedTrickIds)` and passes it through. No changes needed to `user-profile-persistence.ts`, which already round-trips `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` generically. Extended `kentei-assessment.test.ts` with cases for landed-ID accumulation (single trick, across a rank rollover, absence on `trickNotLanded`) and three `getSeedLacedTrickIds` cases (below-rank ∪ landed with de-dup, de-dup when a landed ID is also below rank, and the Basic-level rank-1 case returning just the landed tricks); existing state literals throughout the file were updated to carry the new fields. Extended `user-profile.test.ts` with cases for seeding `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` from empty, unioning with a pre-existing list, and de-duplicating overlap. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all pass), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: cleared `localStorage`, ran onboarding choosing Advanced (starts at rank 4), landed the first two rank-4 tricks ("Moshikame x10 or more, Spike" and "Swing spike") then answered "Not yet" on the third ("Around the World") to end the assessment partway through rank 4 (confirmed the rank reveal showed rank 4, not a rollback, since the failure wasn't on the rank's first trick), selected one practice trick and finished onboarding, then opened Tricks → Laced tricks and confirmed every Rank 1–3 trick (15 standard tricks) showed laced, the two explicitly-landed rank-4 tricks showed laced, "Around the World" and every other rank-4-and-above trick showed unlaced — no unintended extras.

### [T016] Laced-tricks surface

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done — umbrella entry; both children (T016.1, T016.2) complete.
- **Description:** Umbrella for reworking how "I can land this trick" is both set and displayed. Corrects [T004] and [T006]: the badge-plus-toggle pair jammed into the trick name row wrecked the row layout, and "manual unlock" was implemented as a literal Manual button rather than as an ordinary way to say "I've laced this." Marking moves to a dedicated compact screen; the trick list keeps only a passive signal.
- **Notes:** Umbrella only, no exit criteria of its own. [T016.1] must land before [T016.2] — the marking UI has to exist somewhere before the toggle is removed from the row. Decisions resolved during scoping:
  - The laced grid covers the same trick set as the directory (standard Kentei tricks plus custom tricks), grouped by rank, from `createProfileDirectoryTricks` — not a rank-limited subset.
  - Toggling stays a plain set union with lace-derived status (`selectEffectiveUnlockedTrickIds`), unchanged from [T006]. A trick with real sesh history reads laced regardless of the manual flag; that's existing behavior and is correct.
  - Trick rows show nothing at all for an unlaced trick — no badge, no "0 laces". There's no reason to tell someone they haven't done something yet.

### [T018] Count manually-laced tricks toward Kentei rank promotion

- **Area:** Profile / Tricks
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Corrects [T007], which does not work as intended. `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` derives rank completion purely from practice-history lace counts (`selectLacedCountByTrickId`), so tricks the user marked laced by hand never contribute and the rank never advances. Promotion should consider a trick complete if it's laced by either source.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms that marking the last unlaced trick in the current rank as manually laced — with no practice history for it — advances `profile.kenteiRank` to the next rank; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `isKenteiRankComplete` and `selectPromotedKenteiRank` in `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts` now take an `effectiveLacedTrickIds: ReadonlySet<string>` (the union of practice-laced and manually-laced trick IDs) instead of the lace-count `Map` they previously checked with `(count ?? 0) > 0`; `selectLacedCountByTrickId`, `selectUnlockedTrickIds`, and `selectEffectiveUnlockedTrickIds` were left untouched. `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` (`src/features/profile/state/kentei-rank-auto-promotion.tsx`) now also destructures `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` from `useUserProfile()`, derives a strict "actually laced" set from `selectLacedCountByTrickId`'s output (filtering to `count > 0`, deliberately not reusing `selectUnlockedTrickIds`, which also counts zero-lace attempts as "unlocked" for the trick-grid UI — a looser, different convention that would have been an unrequested behavior change here), unions it with `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` via `selectEffectiveUnlockedTrickIds`, and passes that set to `selectPromotedKenteiRank`. `trick-directory.test.ts` updated its `lacedCountsForRankOne` helper to `lacedTrickIdsForRankOne` returning a `Set<string>`, updated the `isKenteiRankComplete`/`selectPromotedKenteiRank` describe blocks to match, and added a case reproducing the exit criteria at the selector level: all rank-1 tricks but the last laced via practice history, the last laced only via `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` with no practice-history entry, combined through `selectEffectiveUnlockedTrickIds`, asserting `selectPromotedKenteiRank(1, KENDAMA_KENTEI_TRICKS, effective)` returns `2`. No component-level integration test was added for `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` itself, per the plan — no test in this codebase renders real `UserProfileProvider`/`PracticeHistoryProvider` together (both gate on AsyncStorage hydration), and building that infra was judged scope growth beyond the exit criteria; the selector-level test plus in-browser verification covers it. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all pass), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: seeded `localStorage` with a profile at `kenteiRank: 4`, no practice history, and `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` covering every rank-4 trick except "Lighthouse, Falling in", confirmed `/profile` showed rank `04`, then opened Tricks → Laced tricks and tapped "Lighthouse, Falling in" (a trick with zero practice-history records) to mark it laced, and confirmed `/profile` immediately updated to rank `05` with no Sesh completion involved.

### [T019] Rank-up celebration

- **Area:** Profile
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** When the user's Kentei rank increases, show a celebration reusing the onboarding rank-reveal animation with copy rewritten for a promotion rather than a first-time reveal. It renders as an app-level modal so it can fire from anywhere a promotion can happen — finishing a sesh, marking tricks laced in the grid, completing an assessment.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the celebration renders when the assessed rank advances and does not render on app hydration of an already-advanced profile; verified in-browser that marking the last trick of the current rank as laced in the laced grid triggers it.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan with one structural deviation. `OnboardingRankRevealStep` (`src/features/onboarding/components/onboarding-rank-reveal-step.tsx`) gained optional `eyebrow`/`title`/`copy`/`buttonLabel` props defaulted to the existing onboarding copy, so `kentei-onboarding.tsx`'s call site needed no changes. New `src/features/profile/components/rank-up-celebration-modal.tsx` exports `RankUpCelebrationModal`, a full-screen absolute overlay (matching `AnimatedSplashOverlay`'s pattern — no RN `<Modal>` precedent in this codebase, and the web target favors absolute-fill) wrapping the parameterized reveal step with promotion copy ("Rank up!" / "You leveled up to Rank {rank}." / "Nice!"), with `accessibilityLabel="Rank up celebration"` on the root for test/tooling lookups. The deviation: the plan's `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` change (`advanceKenteiRank(promotedRank); setCelebratingRank(promotedRank);` both called synchronously inside the existing promotion `useEffect`) fails the repo's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule (an error, not a warning) — calling a local `useState` setter synchronously in an effect body is flagged regardless of what else the effect does. Fixed by splitting the two concerns: `advanceKenteiRank` (an external dispatch into a different provider's reducer) stays in a `useEffect` keyed off a `promotedRank` computed each render via `selectPromotedKenteiRank`, since dispatching to another component's context isn't a local-setState effect the lint rule tracks; `celebratingRank` is instead derived using React's "adjust state while rendering" pattern — a `previousKenteiRank` state seeded from the first render's `kenteiRank` (whatever that is, including an already-hydrated high rank) is compared against the current `kenteiRank` on every render, and `setCelebratingRank` fires only when the value actually increased between two committed renders. This is what satisfies the "key off the transition, not the value" requirement: since `previousKenteiRank`'s initial value is whatever hydration produced, there's no prior render to differ from on first mount, so an already-advanced profile can't trigger it, while the true `undefined → N` transition at first-time onboarding completion is excluded by an explicit `previousKenteiRank !== undefined` guard. New `src/features/profile/state/kentei-rank-auto-promotion.test.tsx` is the first test in this codebase to mount real `UserProfileProvider` + `PracticeHistoryProvider` together (both previously judged too much infra to justify in [T018]) — it needed two additional jest mocks not previously present anywhere in the suite: `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage` (via the package's own `jest/async-storage-mock`) and `react-native-reanimated` (via the package's own `mock`, needed transitively because the celebration renders `OnboardingRankRevealStep`, which imports `react-native-reanimated`). The reanimated mock alone wasn't sufficient — `react-native-worklets` (a `react-native-reanimated` 4.x peer) tries to load its native module even through the mock path unless Jest's module resolution is told to skip `.native.ts` files, so `package.json`'s `jest` config gained `"resolver": "react-native-worklets/jest/resolver"` (the resolver the `react-native-worklets` package ships for exactly this). This is a repo-wide jest config change, not scoped to one test file, but every other test suite still passes under it. Test covers both exit criteria: driving `toggleManualUnlock` for every rank-1 trick ID from `KENDAMA_KENTEI_TRICKS` after `completeOnboarding(1, [], [])` renders the celebration (found via its `accessibilityLabel`) and dismissing it via the "Nice!" button removes it; separately, pre-seeding `AsyncStorage` (via `serializeUserProfile`/`USER_PROFILE_STORAGE_KEY`) with a profile already at `kenteiRank: 2` and all rank-1 tricks manually unlocked, then mounting fresh, confirms the celebration never renders. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all pass), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: with a profile already at rank 5, opened Tricks → Laced tricks, marked the first four unlaced rank-5 tricks, then marked the fifth ("Jumping stick") and watched the "Rank up! / You leveled up to Rank 6." overlay animate in over the laced grid; tapping "Nice!" dismissed it cleanly back to the laced grid with `localStorage`'s persisted `kenteiRank` at `6`; a full page reload afterward (hydrating the now-rank-6 profile from storage) rendered the ordinary Tricks screen directly with no celebration, confirming the hydration exit criterion.

### [T020] Show the first-lace celebration during the sesh

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Extends [T008]. The first-lace celebration currently appears only on the wrap-up screen. Show it in the active sesh at the moment it happens, reading "You just landed {trick} for the first time!", and keep it on the wrap-up screen with past-tense copy: "You landed {trick} for the first time".
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the in-sesh celebration renders on the landing that takes a trick from zero prior laces to one and not on subsequent landings in the same sesh; verified in-browser across both the in-sesh and wrap-up appearances.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. Added `hasPriorLaceForTrick(sessions, trickId)` to `src/features/stats/model/practice-history.ts` and rewrote `isFirstLaceForTrick` to call it — a pure refactor, behavior unchanged. `FirstLaceBanner` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` now takes a `tense: 'inSesh' | 'wrapUp'` prop that only changes its title copy ("You just landed … for the first time!" vs "You landed … for the first time"); `accessibilityLabel` stayed as-is since the two usages never mount simultaneously. `Sesh` now captures `hadPriorLaceAtSeshStart` once at sesh start — in `TrickPicker`'s `onSelect` and in `CompletedSesh`'s `onRepeat` (the latter using a fresh `sessions` read at click-time, which matters: after landing a trick once, `sessions` already includes that record, so a same-run repeat correctly evaluates to `true` and the celebration doesn't refire) — and threads it into `ActiveSesh` as an optional prop, defaulting to `undefined`/off for `MultiTrickSesh`'s existing call site (left untouched; wiring multi-trick runs up is out of scope here). `ActiveSesh` derives `showFirstLaceCelebration = hadPriorLaceAtSeshStart === false && landings.length === 1` and renders the banner (`tense="inSesh"`) as the first child of `ActiveContent`; `CompletedSesh` (now exported, matching `ActiveSesh`/`Countdown`) renders it with `tense="wrapUp"`. New `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.test.tsx` drives `ActiveSesh`/`CompletedSesh` directly via `react-test-renderer` with a minimal fixture trick/plan and no-op callbacks — no provider mocking needed — covering: banner present with "You just landed" text exactly on the landing that takes `landings` from length 0 to 1, absent again at length 2; banner never appears when `hadPriorLaceAtSeshStart` is `true`; `CompletedSesh` shows "You landed" (no "just", no "!") when `isFirstLace` is `true` and nothing when `false`. One deviation from a literal reading of the plan: `ActiveSesh` runs a real `setInterval` via its `useNow` hook to tick the on-screen timer, which — combined with real timers and an un-unmounted renderer — kept the Jest process alive indefinitely after the test file's assertions finished; fixed by using `jest.useFakeTimers()` for the `ActiveSesh` describe block and unmounting each renderer at the end of its test, both needed for the suite to actually exit rather than hang. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (380 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: added "Big cup" (zero practice history) to the practice list, started a 5-minute timed sesh, landed it once and confirmed the banner read exactly "You just landed Big cup for the first time!", landed it a second time and confirmed the banner disappeared with the rest of the active UI unaffected, ended the sesh and confirmed the wrap-up screen showed "You landed Big cup for the first time" (no "just", no exclamation mark), then tapped "Repeat sesh" and landed the trick again — confirmed the celebration did not refire on the repeat run.

### [T023] Show a trick's existing notes during the sesh

- **Area:** Sesh / Notes
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** When practicing a trick the user has already written notes for, render those notes in the sesh flow so they can be read at a glance. Static text only — not editable, not tappable. The point is to surface the reminders that were written to make the next sesh go better.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms a trick with existing notes renders them in the active sesh and a trick with none renders no notes section; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `ActiveSesh` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` gained an optional `trickNotes?: readonly Note[]` prop (defaulting to `[]` via `const notesToShow = trickNotes ?? []`, so the pre-existing `sesh.test.tsx` call sites that omit it kept compiling untouched) and a new presentational `TrickNotesPanel({ trickNotes })` subcomponent, rendered right after the `attemptsReminder` block and before `TimerLabel`. It returns `null` on an empty list, otherwise shows the two most recent notes (the list is already newest-first from `selectNotesForTrick`) each as a `Text` capped at `numberOfLines={4}`/`ellipsizeMode="tail"`, plus a static "+{n} earlier note(s) not shown" line when there are more than two — no `Pressable`, no `onPress`, fully static per the task. Styled as a bordered card (`AppColors.paper`/`AppColors.ink`/`AppColors.textMuted`) matching the `NotePromptCard` family, with `accessibilityLabel="Notes from previous sessions"` on the card root for test/verification hooks. `Sesh` reads `const { notes } = useNotes()` and computes `trickNotes` via a top-level `useMemo` keyed off `notes` and the active trick's ID (`state.status === 'active' ? state.trick.id : undefined`), passed into its existing `<ActiveSesh>` call — `ActiveSesh` itself never touches `NoteProvider`, keeping it presentational as the plan required since `MultiTrickSesh` and the assessment run also drive it. `MultiTrickSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) mirrors the same `useNotes()` + `useMemo` wiring off its own reducer state and passes `trickNotes` into its own `<ActiveSesh>` call; no changes were needed in `multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx` or `assessment-entry.tsx` since `NoteProvider` is already mounted above them in `_layout.tsx`. New `describe('ActiveSesh trick notes')` block in `sesh.test.tsx` renders `ActiveSesh` directly (no `NoteProvider` needed, since it takes `trickNotes` as a plain prop): one case with a hand-built `Note` fixture asserts the note body text appears in `root.toJSON()` and the panel's `accessibilityLabel` is found; the other passes `trickNotes={[]}` and asserts the `accessibilityLabel` is absent (not just the text), directly matching the exit criteria's "renders no notes section" wording rather than just checking for missing text. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (386 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: opened Tricks → Notes for "Big cup" (which already had a saved note, "Kept my elbow tucked in, worked great."), started a single-trick timed sesh on it and confirmed the "FROM YOUR NOTES" card rendered that text statically once the countdown finished; started a multi-trick sesh (`/sesh/multi-trick`) on "Big cup" alone and confirmed the same panel rendered there too, exercising the second wiring path; then ran a multi-trick sesh on "Small cup" (zero notes) and confirmed no notes panel rendered at all.

### [T024] Show note count on the trick row's Notes action

- **Area:** Tricks / Notes
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** The Notes row action on a trick reads "NOTES — 2" when that trick has notes, and plain "NOTES" when it has none.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the label includes the count for a trick with ≥1 note and omits the separator and number entirely for a trick with none; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. Added `selectNoteCountByTrickId(notes)` to `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts`, next to `selectLacedCountByTrickId`, importing `Note` as a type from `@/features/notes/model/note` (mirroring the existing cross-feature `PracticeSessionRecord` import) — sums notes per trick ID, leaving tricks with zero notes absent from the map rather than zero-valued, matching the `selectLacedCountByTrickId` convention. `TrickDirectory` (`src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx`) gained an optional `noteCountByTrickId?: ReadonlyMap<string, number>` prop threaded alongside `lacedCountByTrickId` into `SectionList`'s `extraData` and into a new `noteCount` prop on `TrickRow` (`readonly noteCount?: number`, default `0`); the row still reads no providers directly, per the task's explicit constraint. The Notes action label changed from static `Notes` to `{noteCount > 0 ? `Notes — ${noteCount}` : 'Notes'}` — written mixed-case in JSX so the existing `text-transform: uppercase` on `RowActionText` renders it `NOTES — 2`, left untouched. `accessibilityLabel` (`Notes for ${trick.name}`) was left unchanged. Wired real data in `src/app/tricks.tsx`: added `useNotes()` alongside the existing `usePracticeHistory()` call, computed `noteCountByTrickId` via `useMemo(() => selectNoteCountByTrickId(notes), [notes])` next to `lacedCountByTrickId`, and passed it into `<TrickDirectory>`. New `describe('selectNoteCountByTrickId', ...)` block in `trick-directory.test.ts` (mirroring the `selectLacedCountByTrickId` tests) covers summing multiple notes for the same trick, a zero-note trick staying absent from the map, and no cross-trick bleed. Two new component tests in `trick-directory.test.tsx`, using the existing `allText(root)` pattern and supplying `notesAction={{ onOpen: () => {} }}` (required for the Notes `RowAction` to render at all): one asserts `Notes — 2` appears in rendered text for a trick with `noteCountByTrickId={new Map([['trick-1', 2]])}`, the other asserts no rendered text contains `—` and plain `Notes` is present for a trick with a zero-count or absent entry. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (397 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: opened `/tricks` against seed data where "Big cup" already had one saved note, confirmed its Notes action read `NOTES — 1` in both the Focused tricks section and its Rank 1 listing, and confirmed every other trick with no notes (Small cup, Base cup, Moshikame x5, and the rest of the library) showed plain `NOTES`; also checked the top nav bar at a 320px viewport width and confirmed it renders unaffected by this change.

### [T025] Show a trick's goals on the sesh wrap-up screen

- **Area:** Sesh / Goals
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Goals set for a trick currently exist in isolation from the practice they describe. Render the practiced trick's goals, with their derived progress, on the sesh wrap-up screen.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the wrap-up renders each goal for the practiced trick with progress reflecting history including the just-completed sesh, and renders no goals section for a trick with none; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `formatGoalTarget`/`formatGoalProgress` in `src/features/goals/components/trick-goals-screen.tsx` were exported (previously module-private) for reuse — mechanical, no behavior change. `Sesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) now reads `const { goals } = useGoals()` in the same render as its existing `usePracticeHistory()` call and passes both `goals` and the live `sessions` straight through to `CompletedSesh` as plain props — deliberately not frozen into `useState` the way `personalBest`/`hadPriorLaceAtSeshStart` are, since those two exist specifically to exclude the in-progress sesh while goal progress must include it. `CompletedSesh` gained optional `goals?: readonly Goal[]` and `sessions?: readonly PracticeSessionRecord[]` props (both defaulting to `[]` so the pre-existing `sesh.test.tsx` call sites that omit them kept compiling unchanged), computes `trickGoals` via `selectGoalsForTrick(goals, summary.trick.id)` and `goalProgressList` via `selectGoalProgress` per goal (both memoized), and renders a new `SeshGoalsPanel` — a small read-only card list (kind, target, formatted progress, an "Achieved" badge reusing the visual language of `trick-goals-screen.tsx`'s `AchievedBadge` but restyled locally with `sesh.tsx`'s own color literals rather than imported) — right after `LandingHistory` and before `SeshNotePrompt`, only when `goalProgressList.length > 0`. New tests in `sesh.test.tsx`: a `describe('CompletedSesh goals panel', ...)` block with two direct-props cases (one goal with a session showing formatted progress; empty goals rendering no goals section), plus a separate `describe('CompletedSesh goals panel ordering (regression)', ...)` block that mounts a `Harness` wrapped in real `GoalProvider` + `PracticeHistoryProvider` + `NoteProvider` (mirroring the `Harness` pattern in `kentei-rank-auto-promotion.test.tsx`), reading `goals`/`sessions` via the real hooks and exposing `Pressable`s that call `addGoal` and `recordCompletedSesh` directly — this is the test that would have caught the off-by-one-sesh trap the task warned about, since a props-only test can't detect a mistake at the `Sesh.tsx` wiring level. It adds a count goal (target 3), asserts the pre-sesh progress reads `0 / 3 laces`, calls `recordCompletedSesh` with a summary carrying two landings, and asserts progress immediately reads `2 / 3 laces` in the same test run. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (394 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: opened Tricks → Goals for "Big cup" (which already had 4 recorded laces), added a lace-count goal targeting 6, confirmed it read "4 / 6 laces", started an open-ended sesh on Big cup, landed one lace, ended the sesh, and confirmed the wrap-up's new Goals section read "5 / 6 laces" — the just-completed lace counted immediately, not stale at 4 / 6; separately, ran and ended a sesh on "Small cup" (no goals) and confirmed no Goals section rendered on its wrap-up at all.

### [T026] Celebrate goal achievement in the sesh and on the wrap-up

- **Area:** Sesh / Goals
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** When a landing takes a goal from unmet to met, celebrate it during the sesh, and show it again on the wrap-up screen. Depends on [T025].
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the celebration fires on the landing that crosses a count-based goal's target and not on later landings in the same sesh, and that a goal already achieved before the sesh started never fires; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `src/features/goals/model/goal.ts` gained two pure helpers next to `selectGoalProgress`: `getGoalContribution(goal, landingCount, durationMs)` (returns the landing count for a count goal, elapsed/session duration for a time goal) and `isGoalNewlyAchieved(goal, baselineCurrent, contribution)` (true only when the baseline was strictly under target and baseline+contribution reaches it) — both covered by new cases in `goal.test.ts` (not-yet-crossed, exactly-crosses, overshoots, and already-achieved-baseline returns false regardless of contribution). In `sesh.tsx`, the existing hardcoded first-lace banner styled-components (`FirstLaceBannerCard`/`Eyebrow`/`Title`) were generalized into a shared `CelebrationBannerCard`/`Eyebrow`/`Title` shell driven by a new `CelebrationBanner({ accessibilityLabel, eyebrow, title })` component; `FirstLaceBanner` became a thin wrapper over it with byte-identical accessibilityLabel/text output (verified by rerunning the pre-existing first-lace tests before touching goal logic), and a new sibling `GoalCelebrationBanner({ goal, tense })` renders goal copy ("Goal achieved" / "You just reached your goal — {formatGoalTarget(goal)}" in-sesh, past tense on wrap-up) through the same shell. `Sesh` now freezes `goalBaselines` (`GoalProgress[]`) at the moment a trick is picked (in `TrickPicker`'s `onSelect` and `CompletedSesh`'s `onRepeat`) via `selectGoalsForTrick(goals, trickId).map(g => selectGoalProgress(g, sessions))`, and threads it into `ActiveSesh`. `ActiveSesh` derives count-goal celebrations as a pure per-render filter (`isGoalNewlyAchieved(goal, baseline.current, landings.length) && !isGoalNewlyAchieved(goal, baseline.current, landings.length - 1)`, so it self-hides on the next landing with no latch needed), and derives time-goal crossings the same way against `elapsedMs`, then latches genuinely-new ones once via a `useRef<Set<string>>` checked inside a `useEffect` before any `setState` — the ref (not the effect's dependency array) is what prevents re-adding on every 250ms timer tick, and once shown a time celebration stays visible for the rest of the active sesh by design (no auto-dismiss). `Sesh`'s completion effect now also computes `justAchievedGoals` from the pre-record `sessions` snapshot (before `recordCompletedSesh` runs, mirroring the ordering guarantee `isFirstLaceForTrick` already relies on) via `selectGoalsForTrick` + `selectGoalProgress` + `getGoalContribution` + `isGoalNewlyAchieved`, resetting it to `[]` alongside `firstLaceCelebration` in both `onReset` and `onRepeat`; `CompletedSesh` gained an optional `justAchievedGoals?: readonly Goal[]` prop (default `[]`) rendered as `GoalCelebrationBanner`s near the existing first-lace banner. `MultiTrickSesh` was left untouched, consistent with existing precedent. New tests in `sesh.test.tsx`: `ActiveSesh` count-goal cases (crosses on the qualifying landing, not the next one; never fires when the baseline is already at target), an `ActiveSesh` time-goal case using the file's existing `jest.useFakeTimers()` pattern (crosses once with no landings, and further timer ticks don't duplicate the celebration — asserted by comparing banner-node counts before/after rather than a hardcoded length, since styled-components' composite+host node duplication meant `toHaveLength(1)` was the wrong assertion, matching the existing `findBanner` tests' `toBeGreaterThan(0)` pattern), `CompletedSesh` wrap-up cases (shows past-tense banner for a goal in `justAchievedGoals`; shows nothing when it's empty), and a `Sesh`-level wiring regression test that mounts real `GoalProvider` + `PracticeHistoryProvider` + `UserProfileProvider` + `NoteProvider`, adds a count goal, drives the real picker → countdown → active → laced × 3 → "End Sesh" flow (using a `pressByVisibleText` helper added for the End Sesh button, which — unlike other buttons in this file — carries no `accessibilityLabel`), and confirms the wrap-up shows the achieved-goal banner; this is the test that would catch a baseline computed from post-record sessions instead of pre-record ones. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (405 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: on a "Big cup" trick with 5 recorded laces, added a 6-lace count goal (unmet, 5/6), a 3-lace count goal (already achieved, showing an "Achieved" badge), and a 7:00 practice-time goal (unmet, 6:21/7:00); started a 1-minute timed sesh, landed once — the "Goal achieved / You just reached your goal — 6 laces" banner appeared immediately — landed a second time and confirmed the banner was gone; let the sesh run out with no further action and confirmed the wrap-up showed both the 6-laces and 07:00 goals in past tense ("You reached your goal") while the pre-achieved 3-laces goal never appeared in either the in-sesh or wrap-up celebrations.

### [T027] Standalone trick-goals screen

- **Area:** Tricks / Goals
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Goals are currently reachable only per-trick from a row action, which gives no view of what the user is actually working toward. Add a "Trick goals" screen from the Tricks route listing all goals across all tricks, rendering achieved goals visually distinct from outstanding ones.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the screen lists goals from multiple tricks with per-goal derived progress, and that achieved and unachieved goals render distinguishably; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. Added `selectGoalProgressByTrick(goals, sessions)` to `src/features/goals/model/goal.ts`, returning `readonly TrickGoalGroup[]` — one group per trick with ≥1 goal, goals within a group sorted newest-first (matching `selectGoalsForTrick`'s convention) via `selectGoalProgress` per goal, groups sorted by `trickName.localeCompare`; `selectGoalProgress` itself was left untouched. `GoalRow` in `src/features/goals/components/trick-goals-screen.tsx` was exported (previously module-private) for reuse — mechanical, no behavior change. New `src/features/goals/components/all-trick-goals-screen.tsx` exports `AllTrickGoalsScreen({ onClose })`, mirroring `TrickGoalsScreen`'s pattern of reading `useGoals()`/`useGoalActions()`/`usePracticeHistory()` directly rather than prop-threading; it renders the same page shell as `TrickGoalsScreen`/`LacedTricksScreen`, an empty state ("No goals yet. Set one from a trick's Goals action.") when there are no goals, and per-trick `Section`/`SectionHeader`/`SectionTitle` blocks (styling idiom reused from `laced-tricks-screen.tsx`) each followed by that trick's `GoalRow`s — no "add goal" affordance, since goal creation stays exclusive to `TrickGoalsScreen`. Wired into `src/app/tricks.tsx`: a `showAllGoals` state flag, a screen-swap branch alongside the existing `showLacedTricks` one, and a `TrickGoalsButton` (`styled(Button).attrs({ size: 'medium' })`, same convention as `DefineButton`/`LacedTricksButton`) added to `HeaderActions` right after "Laced tricks". Tests: three new cases in `goal.test.ts` for `selectGoalProgressByTrick` (two tricks grouped and sorted by name; multiple goals for one trick newest-first within its group; empty input returns `[]`), and a new `src/features/goals/components/all-trick-goals-screen.test.tsx` mounting a real `GoalProvider` + `PracticeHistoryProvider` harness (mirroring `sesh.test.tsx`'s "CompletedSesh goals panel ordering" pattern) that drives `addGoal` for two different tricks and `recordCompletedSesh` for one of them, asserting both trick names and both progress strings render, that exactly one "Achieved" badge appears, that the empty state renders with no goals, and that a goal disappears from the list after its delete/confirm flow — the last two needed an `AsyncStorage.clear()` in `beforeEach` since the async-storage jest mock otherwise persists goal state across tests within the file. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (411 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: confirmed the "Trick goals" button sits next to "Laced tricks" without overflowing at a 320px viewport width (both wrap onto their own row via the existing `flex-wrap` on `HeaderActions`, consistent with pre-existing behavior); added goals on two different tricks — "Big cup" (3 goals, all already achieved) and "Small cup" (1 new, unachieved lace-count goal) — then opened "Trick goals" and confirmed it read "2 tricks with goals set", showed a "Big cup" section with all three goals bearing an "ACHIEVED" badge, and a "Small cup" section showing "0 / 10 laces" with no badge; confirmed "Close" returned to the trick directory.

### [T028.1] Advance to the next trick after a single lace

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T028)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** A checkbox in multi-trick setup that, when enabled, advances the run to the next trick as soon as the current trick is laced once.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms that with the option enabled a single landing records the trick and advances to the next one, and that with it disabled the run behaves exactly as it does today; verified in-browser across a 3-trick run.
