- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `MultiTrickSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) gained an optional `readonly advanceOnFirstLace?: boolean` prop; the `active` branch's `onLaced` handler now dispatches `trickLaced` and then, when `advanceOnFirstLace` is set and the pre-dispatch state still shows zero landings for the current trick, immediately dispatches `seshEnded` — a second dispatch from the same handler against the existing `completed`-state advance path, not a new advance mechanism, so `sesh.ts`'s reducer needed no changes. `MultiTrickSeshEntry` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx`) owns a local `advanceOnFirstLace` boolean (default `false`, preserving today's behavior), rendered as a checkbox row ("Advance after one lace") in the setup footer next to "Start sesh", styled with `AppColors`/`Radii` following the `trick-directory.tsx` checkbox convention since no shared `Checkbox` component exists yet; the flag is passed through to `MultiTrickSesh` and reset to `false` in `handleFinished`, mirroring the existing `selectedTrickIds` reset. New `src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` (no prior test file existed for this component) covers both paths against a 3-trick fixture: with `advanceOnFirstLace` enabled, one lace per trick advances all the way through to `onFinished` with exactly one recorded landing per trick (asserted via a `PracticeHistoryProvider` consumer harness); with it left at its default `false`, a single lace on the first trick leaves the sesh on that trick's active screen, and only an explicit "End Sesh" press advances it — proving the manual-end path is untouched. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (413 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: selected 3 tricks (Big cup, Small cup, Base cup), checked "Advance after one lace", started the sesh, and confirmed a single "Laced" press per trick walked through all three automatically back to the setup screen (checkbox and selection reset); repeated with the checkbox left unchecked and confirmed a single lace on the first trick did not advance, requiring an explicit "End Sesh" press to move to the next trick.

### [T028.2] Per-trick duration for a multi-trick run

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T028)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Let the user set a duration applied to each trick in the run — three tricks at one minute each means the run moves on after a minute on each trick.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms that a run configured with a per-trick duration passes a timed plan for each trick and advances when that duration elapses, and that leaving it unset preserves today's open-ended behavior; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan — this was UI wiring only, no changes to `MultiTrickSesh`'s sequencing core or `sesh.ts`'s reducer, since the timed-plan → `timerElapsed` → `completed` → advance path already existed and is generic. `MultiTrickSeshEntry` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx`) gained a local `perTrickDurationMinutes` string state (default `''`), deriving a `SeshPlan` via `createTimedSeshPlan`/`isValidTimedSeshMinutes` when a valid value is entered, falling back to `OPEN_ENDED_SESH_PLAN` when blank or invalid — mirroring `TrickPicker`'s duration-field pattern in `sesh.tsx`. The `running` step now carries the plan captured at `handleStart` time (`{ kind: 'running', tricks, plan }`) since the entry's own inputs are gone once a run starts; `MultiTrickSesh` receives `plan={step.plan}` instead of the previously hardcoded `OPEN_ENDED_SESH_PLAN`. Rendered a "Time per trick, minutes (optional)" field in the setup footer between the selection-count text and the "Advance after one lace" row, using new `PerTrickDurationField`/`PerTrickDurationLabel`/`PerTrickDurationInput`/`PerTrickDurationHint` styled components matching `sesh.tsx`'s `DurationField` visual language; "Start sesh" is disabled when an invalid (non-blank, out-of-1–60) value is entered, with the hint text turning red and explaining the valid range. The field and its state reset in `handleFinished` alongside the existing `selectedTrickIds`/`advanceOnFirstLace` resets. `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` gained a `plan` parameter on `mountMultiTrickSesh` (defaulting to `OPEN_ENDED_SESH_PLAN`, so existing calls are unchanged) and a new `describe('MultiTrickSesh per-trick duration', ...)` block that mounts with `createTimedSeshPlan(1)` and, for each of the 3 fixture tricks, advances through the countdown and then 60 seconds with no "Laced" or "End Sesh" press, asserting the run auto-advances through all three tricks unattended, `onFinished` fires once, and a zero-lace session is recorded per trick. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (415 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: with the duration field left blank, started and confirmed the active screen showed "Elapsed time" counting up with no automatic advance until "End Sesh" was pressed — today's open-ended behavior preserved; entered "1", confirmed the countdown screen read "01:00 begins after the countdown", started the run, and confirmed it counted down and, after the minute elapsed with no lace or button press, auto-advanced and (with only one trick selected) returned to a fully reset setup screen (empty selection, empty duration field); confirmed an invalid value ("0") disabled "Start sesh" and turned the hint text red with the expected range message.

### [T028.3] "Next trick" action during a run

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T028)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Add a "Next trick" button to the sesh screen during a multi-trick run that records the current trick and moves to the next one.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms pressing it writes a practice-history record for the current trick with the laces accumulated so far and advances the run, and that it's absent from a single-trick sesh; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `ActiveSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) gained an optional `readonly onNextTrick?: () => void` prop next to `onSkipRank`; when present it renders a new full-width "Next trick" button (`NextTrickButton`/`NextTrickButtonText`) placed as its own row between `LandingHistory` and the `SecondaryActions` (Pause/End Sesh) row, rather than folding it into that row — it wanted the visual weight of a standalone action and there wasn't room to add a third control to the two-button row without cramping it at narrow widths (sanity-checked at 320px). Unlike `EndButton`, it carries no `targetMet` gating — it's always enabled regardless of any target, per the plan. `MultiTrickSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) wires it unconditionally in the `active` branch to `onNextTrick={() => dispatch({ type: 'seshEnded', now: Date.now() })}` — the identical dispatch `onEnd` already uses one line above, documented with a comment noting this reuses the existing completed-effect advance path rather than adding a second one, and is explicitly not the `advanceTrickSequenceToIndex` path `handleSkipRank` uses (which bypasses recording on purpose). No changes to `MultiTrickSeshProps`, `sesh.ts`, or `trick-sequence.ts` were needed — this was UI wiring only. `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` gained a `describe('MultiTrickSesh "Next trick" action', ...)` block: one test lands two "Laced" presses on the first trick, presses "Next trick", and asserts the practice-history record for that trick shows `lace-count:2` and the second trick's name now renders; a second test presses "Next trick" once per trick across the full 3-trick fixture and asserts `onFinished` fires exactly once with one recorded lace per trick, coexisting with the untouched "End Sesh" tests in the same file. `sesh.test.tsx` gained a `describe('ActiveSesh "Next trick" action', ...)` block covering `ActiveSesh` in isolation (button absent when `onNextTrick` is omitted; present and wired when supplied), plus a `describe('Sesh single-trick entry point - "Next trick" absence', ...)` block that mounts the real single-trick `Sesh` entry point, drives it into an active sesh, and asserts "Next trick" never renders there — proving the absence at the actual entry point, not just in `ActiveSesh` isolation. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (419 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: started a multi-trick sesh with Big Cup and Small Cup selected, pressed "Laced" twice on Big Cup, pressed "Next trick", and confirmed the run advanced to Small Cup; checked Stats → Big Cup's trick history and confirmed the most recent past sesh recorded "2 laces" as expected. Separately started a single-trick sesh from `/sesh` and confirmed the active screen shows only "Pause Sesh"/"End Sesh" with no "Next trick" control.

### [T028.4] Multi-trick sesh wrap-up screen

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T028)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** A run through several tricks currently ends by dumping the user back at the trick picker with no summary. Show a wrap-up covering the whole run: each trick attempted, its laces, and its time.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms a completed 3-trick run renders one summary entry per attempted trick in run order with correct per-trick lace counts, and that a skipped trick is absent; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan, with one deliberate scope decision and one refactor beyond it. Scope decision: per-trick celebrations from [T020]/[T021]/[T026] were confirmed _not_ to fire during multi-trick runs today (`MultiTrickSesh`'s `ActiveSesh` call passes `personalBest={undefined}`, `liveChallengeEnabled={false}`, and omits `hadPriorLaceAtSeshStart`/`goalBaselines`) — celebration replay on the wrap-up is out of scope for this pass, not assumed. `MultiTrickSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) gained a `const [summaries, setSummaries] = useState<SeshSummary[]>([])` accumulator, appended to inside the existing `recordedSummaryId.current === summaryId` guard in the `'completed'` effect, so exactly one summary lands per attempted trick. Refactor beyond the plan: rather than adding a separate `isRunFinished` state set via `setState` inside effects (as planned), `isRunFinished` is derived directly at render time — `isTrickSequenceFinished(sequence) && (state.status === 'choosing' || state.status === 'completed')` — since ESLint's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` flagged the planned `setIsRunFinished(true)` call in the `'completed'` effect's else-branch ("Avoid calling setState() directly within an effect"). The derived condition is equivalent for both paths the plan called out: a normal run finishing leaves `state.status` at `'completed'` with nothing left to dispatch; `handleSkipRank` skipping past the last trick dispatches `seshReset`, leaving `state.status` at `'choosing'`, and in both cases `sequence` has already been advanced to a finished index by the same render. This also let the pre-existing `hasReportedEmptySequence` ref and its effect be deleted entirely — the empty-tricks-at-mount case is just another instance of the same derived condition, true from the first render, no effect needed. A new `MultiTrickWrapUp({ summaries, onContinue })` component replaces the terminal "Sequence complete" `CenteredPanel` render (now the non-terminal one-tick fallback), styled on `CompletedSesh`'s precedent in `sesh.tsx`: `ScrollArea`/`ActiveContent` shell, the file's existing `Eyebrow`/`DisplayTitle` ("Sesh complete" / "Nice work."), a `WrapUpRow`-per-summary list (bordered `View` rows, `LandingHistory`'s idiom rather than `SummaryGrid`'s tiles) keyed on `createPracticeSessionRecordId(summary)`, each row carrying `accessibilityLabel="Wrap-up entry: {trick.name}, {landings.length} laces, {formatDuration(durationMs)}"` for reliable test targeting, and a "Finish sesh" button that's now the only place `onFinished` gets called. `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx`'s three pre-existing tests that asserted `onFinished` fired immediately after the run's last trick (advance-on-first-lace, "Next trick" coexistence, per-trick-duration) each gained a `pressByAccessibilityLabel(root, 'Finish sesh')` step between the run finishing and the `onFinished` assertion, plus an added `expect(onFinished).not.toHaveBeenCalled()` beforehand — expected fallout of the contract change, not a regression. `mountMultiTrickSesh` gained a passthrough `skipTarget` prop. New `describe('MultiTrickSesh wrap-up', ...)` block: one test runs a 3-trick sequence with distinct lace counts (1, 2, 0) and asserts three wrap-up entries in `TRICKS` order with correct per-trick lace counts (deduping `findAll`'s composite+host-node duplicate matches via `new Set`), that `onFinished` hasn't fired until "Finish sesh" is pressed, and fires exactly once after; a second test runs one trick normally, then uses `skipTarget: () => TRICKS.length` with "Skip this rank" to skip the remainder, and asserts the wrap-up shows only the attempted trick's entry with the skipped tricks' names entirely absent. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (421 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: selected Big cup/Small cup/Base cup with "Advance after one lace" enabled, laced once each on the first two tricks (auto-advancing), then ended the third with zero laces via "End Sesh" — the wrap-up rendered all three tricks in order ("Big cup — 1 lace — 00:13", "Small cup — 1 lace — 00:18", "Base cup — 0 laces — 00:32") under "Sesh complete / Nice work.", and pressing "Finish sesh" returned cleanly to the trick picker with selections reset.

### [T029.1] Assessment-specific end-action copy

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T029)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** During an assessment run the end action reads "End assessment" rather than "End Sesh".
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the end action renders "End assessment" when the assessment flow drives the run and "End Sesh" otherwise; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `ActiveSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) gained an optional `readonly endActionLabel?: string` prop, defaulted to `'End Sesh'` via a default parameter, and the primary end button's text now renders `{endActionLabel}` instead of a hardcoded string (`EndAnywayButtonText` untouched — the exit criteria only covers the primary end action). Since `AssessmentEntry` renders `MultiTrickSesh`, not `ActiveSesh`, directly, the prop threads opaquely through `MultiTrickSeshProps`/`MultiTrickSesh` the same way `showAttemptsReminder`/`advanceOnFirstLace` do, documented as "opaque to this component, forwarded to `ActiveSesh` as-is." `AssessmentEntry` (`src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx`) passes `endActionLabel="End assessment"` on its `MultiTrickSesh` call in the `'running'` branch. No other caller (`sesh-sequence-sandbox.tsx`, `multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx`, single-trick `Sesh`) needed changes — `undefined` flows through to `ActiveSesh`'s own default. `sesh.test.tsx` gained a `describe('ActiveSesh end action label', ...)` block asserting `'End Sesh'` renders by default and `'End assessment'` renders (with `'End Sesh'` absent) when the prop is supplied, mounting `ActiveSesh` directly per the file's existing minimal-props pattern. `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` extended `mountMultiTrickSesh`'s props/JSX to accept `endActionLabel` and added a `describe('MultiTrickSesh endActionLabel', ...)` test proving the real threading path — mounting through `MultiTrickSesh` with `endActionLabel="End assessment"` and asserting the active-trick screen shows "End assessment" with "End Sesh" absent, which is the layer that would have caught a forgotten pass-through. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (424 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: `/stats/sesh-sequence-sandbox` (a regular `MultiTrickSesh` run) showed "End Sesh" on the active screen; `/profile/kentei-assessment` → "Current rank only" → active trick screen showed "End assessment" instead.

### [T029.2] Skip the countdown between assessment tricks

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T029)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Moving to the next trick during an assessment goes straight to the active state, without the 3-second countdown.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms that in an assessment run advancing to the next trick transitions directly to `active`, while a regular multi-trick run still passes through `countdown`; verified in-browser across a multi-rank assessment.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. `MultiTrickSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) gained an optional `readonly skipCountdownBetweenTricks?: boolean` prop and a `seedNextTrick(trick)` helper (wrapped in `useCallback` on `[plan, skipCountdownBetweenTricks]` to satisfy `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` in the completed-effect that calls it) that dispatches `trickSelected` followed immediately by `countdownCompleted` when the flag is set — relying on the same "multiple synchronous dispatches fold in order against queued state" behavior `handleLaced`'s paired `trickLaced`/`seshEnded` dispatches already use. Both post-mount seed sites that literally match the Notes' "dispatching `trickSelected`" description now go through it: the `'completed'` effect's auto-advance and `handleSkipRank`'s "Skip this rank" jump. The `useReducer` lazy initializer that seeds the very first trick at mount is untouched by design — the task's "between assessment tricks" framing only covers trick-to-trick transitions, not the run's own opening countdown; a scope call, not an oversight, and out of scope for this task if it turns out the first countdown should also disappear. `AssessmentEntry` (`src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx`) passes `skipCountdownBetweenTricks` (unconditionally, not gated on `isMultiRankFlow` — a single-rank assessment still steps through several tricks within that rank) on its `MultiTrickSesh` call. The reducer's state graph in `src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts` was not touched, per the Notes. `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` gained a `skipCountdownBetweenTricks` passthrough on `mountMultiTrickSesh` and a new `describe('MultiTrickSesh skipCountdownBetweenTricks', ...)` block: one test confirms the first trick still requires `advanceThroughCountdown()` before "Laced" appears, then presses Laced/"Next trick" and asserts the second trick's name and "Laced" are present with no countdown wait; a second test pairs the flag with `skipTarget` and confirms "Skip this rank" also lands directly in `active` on the target trick. The regular (flag-omitted) case that still requires `advanceThroughCountdown()` between every trick was already covered by the pre-existing "Next trick" and `advanceOnFirstLace` describe blocks, so no new test was added for that half. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (428 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: started a "Full assessment" run, confirmed the first trick ("Big cup") showed the "Get ready" 3-2-1 countdown, laced it and pressed "Next trick" — the second trick ("Small cup") rendered straight into "Sesh in progress" active state (elapsed timer already running, "Laced" button present) with no countdown screen shown.

### [T029.3] Hide the timer during an assessment

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T029)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** The elapsed-time readout isn't rendered during an assessment run — there's nothing to race, and showing a clock makes the user feel rushed.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the timer and its label are absent in an assessment run and present in a regular sesh, while the recorded practice-history record still carries a real non-zero `durationMs`; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan. Added an opaque `readonly hideTimer?: boolean` prop threaded `AssessmentEntry` → `MultiTrickSesh` → `ActiveSesh`, the same "forwarded as-is, opaque to intermediate components" pattern already used for `endActionLabel`/`skipCountdownBetweenTricks`. `ActiveSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) gates only the JSX render of `TimerLabel`/`Timer` on `!hideTimer` — `useNow`, `elapsedMs`, `displayedTime`, `formattedTime`, the timed-plan auto-complete effect, and time-goal-crossing detection all keep computing unconditionally, so `onTimerElapsed` and the elapsed time captured at `seshEnded` are unaffected. `MultiTrickSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) forwards `hideTimer` straight through to its `ActiveSesh` call, never reasoning about the value itself. `AssessmentEntry` (`src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx`) passes `hideTimer` (boolean shorthand) on its `MultiTrickSesh` call. `sesh.test.tsx` gained a `describe('ActiveSesh timer visibility', ...)` block mounting `ActiveSesh` directly with a timed plan, asserting `'Time remaining'` renders when `hideTimer` is omitted and is absent when it's set. `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` gained a `session-${index}-duration:...` accessibility label on the `SessionSummary` test harness (mirroring the existing `lace-count` label) plus a `findSessionDurationMs` helper, and a `describe('MultiTrickSesh hideTimer', ...)` block: one test confirms `'Elapsed time'`/`'Time remaining'` are both absent during a `hideTimer: true` run, a second confirms `'Elapsed time'` renders during a regular run, and a third advances fake timers by 2 seconds during a `hideTimer: true` run, presses `'Laced.'` then `'End Sesh'`, and asserts the recorded session's `durationMs` is greater than 0 — the direct proof that hiding the display didn't touch the underlying elapsed-time math. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (433 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: a regular timed sesh showed "Time remaining" ticking down as expected; a Kentei assessment run ("Current rank only") showed no timer or label on the active trick screen while "Laced.", the attempts reminder, "Next trick", "Pause Sesh", and "End assessment" all rendered normally, and the lace timeline still showed real elapsed time (`+00:12`) per landing; after ending the assessment, Stats showed a non-zero recorded practice time for the trick, confirming `useNow` kept running under the hood.

### [T029.4] Auto-advance on lace during an assessment

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T029)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Expose [T028.1]'s advance-on-first-lace behavior in the assessment flow, so pressing Laced moves straight to the next trick. Depends on [T028.1].
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms an assessment run with the option active records the trick and advances on the first landing; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Confirmed wiring-only, as the Notes anticipated: [T028.1] already left the full `advanceOnFirstLace` mechanism in `MultiTrickSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) — the `active` branch's `handleLaced` dispatches `trickLaced` then, when `advanceOnFirstLace` is set and the pre-dispatch state showed zero prior landings for the current trick, immediately dispatches `seshEnded`, reusing the same completed-state advance path `skipCountdownBetweenTricks`'s `seedNextTrick` already relies on. The open call — always-on vs. user-selectable — resolved to **always on, unconditional**, matching the pattern of every other assessment-shaping prop (`showAttemptsReminder`, `endActionLabel`, `skipCountdownBetweenTricks`, `hideTimer`): an assessment trick's product shape is "try until you land it, then move on" (there's no attempt-counting/fail state anywhere in the feature per [T013]'s own scoping), and `AssessmentEntry`'s flow-selection screens are a simple linear picker with no existing settings-toggle affordance, so introducing one here would be new UI surface for a decision the feature already makes implicitly. The only change was adding `advanceOnFirstLace` (boolean shorthand) to the `MultiTrickSesh` call in `AssessmentEntry`'s `'running'` branch (`src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx`) — no changes to `multi-trick-sesh.tsx`, the `seshReducer` in `sesh.ts`, or `trick-sequence.ts`. No new test file: the pre-existing `describe('MultiTrickSesh advanceOnFirstLace', ...)` block in `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` (from [T028.1]) already mechanically proves "records the trick and advances on the first landing" against the exact `MultiTrickSesh` component `AssessmentEntry` renders, following the same no-dedicated-`AssessmentEntry`-test precedent as [T029.1]–[T029.3]. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (433 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean). In-browser verification surfaced and resolved an environment gotcha unrelated to the feature code: the Browser pane's `preview_start` tool reused/launched the dev server from the shared root checkout's working directory rather than this worktree, so the first browser pass was silently exercising pre-edit code. Started an independent `yarn expo start --web --port 8097` directly in this worktree instead, then walked through onboarding → `/profile/kentei-assessment` → "Full assessment": lacing "Big cup" advanced immediately (no countdown, per [T029.2]) straight into "Small cup" active, then "Base cup", then "Moshikame x5" — three consecutive auto-advances, not a one-shot fluke. `/stats` afterward showed exactly one recorded lace each for Big cup, Small cup, and Base cup. Separately confirmed non-leakage: `/stats/sesh-sequence-sandbox` (a regular `MultiTrickSesh` run without the prop) required an explicit "Next trick"/"End Sesh" press after a single lace, staying on the first trick with 1 landing recorded — proving the assessment-only wiring didn't affect the shared default.

### [T029] Kentei assessment run refinements

- **Area:** Sesh / Profile
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Umbrella for corrections to [T013]'s assessment run. The run currently inherits practice-sesh chrome that misrepresents what an assessment is: a countdown between every trick, a running timer implying time pressure, and an "End sesh" label for what ends an assessment.
- **Notes:** Umbrella only, no exit criteria of its own. [T029.3] depends on [T028.3] ("Next trick" in the shared core) and [T029.4] depends on [T028.1] (advance-on-lace in the shared core) — this entry deliberately doesn't reimplement either. Decision resolved during scoping: hiding the timer is a display change only; assessment records still capture real `durationMs`, since Stats derivation and the time-logged windows shouldn't develop a blind spot for assessment practice. All five children ([T029.1]–[T029.5]) are Done; closed out alongside [T029.5], the last to land.

### [T029.5] Assessment wrap-up screen

- **Area:** Sesh / Profile
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T029)
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Show a wrap-up when an assessment run completes, instead of replacing the route to `/profile` with no summary. Covers which tricks were attempted and laced, which ranks were skipped, and the resulting assessed rank.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms a completed assessment renders per-trick outcomes and the resulting rank, and that a skipped rank is reported as skipped rather than failed; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** The generic wrap-up mechanism (`MultiTrickWrapUp` in `src/features/sesh/components/multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) was already in place from [T028.4]; this task added the two assessment-specific pieces the Notes anticipated. Added two new optional `MultiTrickSesh` props, both opaque to the component (it still knows nothing about ranks or assessments): `describeSkippedRange?: (startIndex: number, endIndex: number) => string`, called from `handleSkipRank` with the pre-jump and target indices and accumulated into a new `skippedEntries` state array (mirroring the existing `summaries` accumulation); and `wrapUpFooter?: ReactNode`, rendered after the skipped section, above "Finish sesh". `MultiTrickWrapUp` renders `skippedEntries` as a second list, styled with `AppColors.textMuted`/`AppColors.borderMuted` (not red), each row labeled `Wrap-up skipped: {entry}` and reading "{entry} — Skipped" — deliberately distinct from the attempted-trick rows so a skipped rank never gets narrated as a fail/zero-lace outcome, matching [T013]'s "skipped = unassessed" decision. `AssessmentEntry` (`src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx`) wires `describeSkippedRange` (guarded by `isMultiRankFlow`, same as the existing `skipTarget`) to produce copy like "Rank 10 — 3 tricks skipped", and `wrapUpFooter` unconditionally to a `ResultCopy` styled component reading "You're assessed at Rank {kenteiRank}." or a not-yet-ranked fallback, sourced from `useUserProfile()` so it re-renders automatically if `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` (already mounted at the app root per [T019], confirmed Done) advances the rank after the run's last landing — no promotion banner needed here, that modal already renders independently on top of this screen. No new test file, following the established [T029.1]–[T029.4] precedent of testing only through `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx`: extended `mountMultiTrickSesh` to forward both new props, and added two tests to the existing `describe('MultiTrickSesh wrap-up', ...)` block — one asserting the footer is absent during an active run and present only once the wrap-up renders, the other running a lace-then-skip sequence and asserting a `Wrap-up skipped:`-labeled row exists, contains neither `laces` nor `0 laces`, and that the skipped-past tricks produce no `Wrap-up entry:` row. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (435 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on an independent `yarn expo start --web --port 8100` server run directly in this worktree (avoiding the reused-server gotcha [T029.4]'s notes flagged): seeded a hydrated profile via `localStorage`, ran `/profile/kentei-assessment` → "Start from a rank" → rank 10, laced two tricks, used "Skip this rank" three times across ranks 10/11/12, and confirmed the wrap-up showed three attempted rows (including a 0-lace row for a trick ended without lacing), three visually muted "Rank N — M tricks skipped — Skipped" rows, and a "You're assessed at Rank 3." footer; "Finish sesh" routed back to `/profile` as before. Separately re-ran `/stats/sesh-sequence-sandbox` (a plain multi-trick sesh with neither prop) through to its wrap-up and confirmed no skipped section and no footer appeared, proving the new props don't leak into non-assessment callers.

### [T021] Personal-best celebration

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** When a user beats their previous best for a trick at a given duration, celebrate it in the sesh the same way a first lace is celebrated. This must only fire when there's a genuine previous best to beat — a first-ever sesh on a trick is not a personal best.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms the celebration fires on the landing that exceeds an existing personal best, and does not fire when no prior comparable attempt exists or when the count merely ties the best; verified in-browser.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan exactly. `ActiveSesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) gained `const showPersonalBestCelebration = personalBest !== undefined && landings.length === personalBest + 1;` right after the existing `showFirstLaceCelebration` line — the "just crossed" idiom already used for count-goal celebrations, true for exactly one render (the landing that exceeds the best), false at the tie and false again one landing later. It fires independent of `liveChallengeEnabled`/`showChallenge`, which stays exactly as it was for the existing opt-in live-progress readout. A new `PersonalBestBanner({ trickName, personalBest })` sits beside `FirstLaceBanner`, reusing the existing `CelebrationBanner` primitive (no new styled-components needed) with `accessibilityLabel="New personal best on {trickName}"` and title "You just beat your best of {personalBest} / on {trickName}!". Rendered in `ActiveSesh`'s celebration stack alongside `FirstLaceBanner`. TS's control-flow analysis of aliased conditions (TS 4.4+) narrows `personalBest` from `number | undefined` to `number` at the `showPersonalBestCelebration ? <PersonalBestBanner personalBest={personalBest} /> : null` call site, same mechanism already relied on for `showChallenge`/`hasBeatenBest` — no extra guard needed, confirmed by a clean `tsc --noEmit`. `sesh.test.tsx` gained a `findPersonalBestBanner` helper beside `findBanner`/`findGoalBanner`, and a new `describe('ActiveSesh personal-best celebration', ...)` block: one test mounts with `personalBest={2}`, `liveChallengeEnabled={false}` (proving independence from the opt-in) and steps landings from 1 → 2 (tie, no banner) → 3 (crosses, banner appears, contains "New personal best") → 4 (banner gone again); a second test mounts with `personalBest={undefined}` and steps landings 1 through 4, asserting the banner never appears, covering both "no comparable prior attempt" and the deliberately-untouched `isEligibleTimedAttempt`/open-ended-sesh behavior the task's Notes called out as out of scope. One test-assertion deviation from the plan: the initial draft asserted `JSON.stringify(root.toJSON())).toContain('You just beat your best of 2')`, but the JSX title's interpolated children serialize as separate array entries (`"You just beat your best of ", "2", ...`), not a concatenated string, so the assertion was changed to check for the static eyebrow text `'New personal best'` instead — the `findPersonalBestBanner` accessibility-label check already covers the interpolated content precisely. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (423 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on the Expo dev server: started a 1-minute timed sesh on Big cup (existing personal best of 2 laces at 1:00 from prior seeded data) with "Reveal at the end" selected (`liveChallengeEnabled=false`), tapped "Laced" to 2 (tie with best — no banner), tapped to 3 (crossed — "New personal best / You just beat your best of 2 on Big cup!" banner appeared immediately, with no live-progress readout ever shown since the opt-in was off), and tapped to 4 (banner gone).

### [T028] Multi-trick run controls

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done — umbrella entry; all four children (T028.1–T028.4) complete.
- **Description:** Umbrella for the controls a multi-trick run is missing. Extends [T012]/[T014]: a run currently steps to the next trick only when the user manually ends each one, offers no way to advance mid-trick, and drops the user back at the picker with no summary of what they just did.
- **Notes:** Umbrella only, no exit criteria of its own. Children are ordered but largely independent; [T028.4] should land last since it reports on everything the others change. Decisions resolved during scoping:
  - "Next trick" **records** the trick being left, with whatever laces occurred — same practice-history write `End Sesh` performs. It means "I'm done with this one", not "discard this." Consequently it feeds Stats, goals, and rank promotion normally. This deliberately differs from assessment's existing "Skip this rank", which records nothing.
  - When both auto-advance-on-lace and a per-trick duration are configured, whichever condition is met first advances the run.
  - These controls belong to the shared `MultiTrickSesh` core so the assessment flow ([T029]) inherits them rather than reimplementing them.

### [T030] Make the Profile screen scrollable on mobile

- **Area:** Profile / navigation
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** On mobile the Profile screen doesn't scroll, the navigation menu paints over the first section, and the assessed-rank tile isn't visible at all. Its content is taller than the viewport with nowhere to go.
- **Exit criteria:** Verified in-browser at 375 px that the assessed-rank tile is visible, unobscured by the navigation, and that every section down to the streak-threshold save action is reachable by scrolling.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan exactly, confirming the root cause: `ProfileScreen` (`src/app/profile/index.tsx`) rendered its content in a plain `styled.View` (`Content`, with `flex: 1; justify-content: center`) directly inside `Screen`, and `expo-router/html`'s `<ScrollViewStyleReset />` sets `html`/`body`/`#root` to `overflow: hidden` on web, so nothing outside a real RN `ScrollView` can scroll — the vertical centering clipped the tile off the top and the Save button off the bottom symmetrically. Fix: dropped `align="center"` from `Screen`, wrapped `Content` in a new `ProfileScroll` (`styled(ScrollView)`, `flex: 1; width: 100%;`) with `contentContainerStyle={{ paddingBottom: 120 + BottomTabInset }}`, and changed `Content` from `flex: 1; justify-content: center; padding: 40px 22px 130px;` to `align-self: center; padding: 40px 22px 0;` — matching the established `StatsScroll`/`StatsContent` and `FormScroll`/`FormContent` pattern used elsewhere. Single-file change (`src/app/profile/index.tsx`), no other files touched. No new test was added, per the task's own Notes: this is a layout/overflow defect only visible through real scroll behavior, and a test asserting a `ScrollView` node exists would prove nothing about the actual clipping bug. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (435 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on an independent `yarn expo start --web --port 8092` server run directly in this worktree: seeded a hydrated, onboarded profile (`kenteiRank: 4`) via `localStorage`, and at 375px width confirmed the "Assessed Kentei rank" tile renders fully visible at the top, unobscured by the floating nav, and that scrolling down reaches the Daily streak section with the Save button fully visible above the bottom edge. Also sanity-checked desktop width (800px) — content renders centered and layout-neutral as expected — and re-confirmed the 320px web-nav-fits constraint from `AGENTS.md` is unaffected (this change doesn't touch `app-tabs.web.tsx`).

### [T031] Accept paused Sesh records when restoring practice history

- **Area:** Practice history / persistence
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Fix the runtime-validation mismatch that rejects valid completed Sesh records containing paused time. The Sesh records active practice duration, while the current history validator assumes duration must exactly equal the wall-clock interval between start and completion.
- **Exit criteria:** A test creates a Sesh that is started, paused, resumed, and completed, converts it into a history record, serializes it, and restores it successfully; the same test boundary confirms non-paused records still restore and records whose active duration exceeds their total wall-clock span are rejected.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan exactly — the bug was isolated to a single equality check. `src/features/stats/persistence/practice-history-persistence.ts`'s `isSessionRecord` changed `durationMs === completedAt - startedAt` to `durationMs <= completedAt - startedAt` (active practice time can be less than wall-clock elapsed time when a session was paused, but never more). No changes were needed to `completeSesh` (`src/features/sesh/model/sesh.ts`), `createPracticeSessionRecord`, or the document version — all already correctly treat `durationMs` as active-practice time, not wall-clock elapsed time. Added a new integration-style test in `practice-history-persistence.test.ts` that drives the real `seshReducer` through `trickSelected` → `countdownCompleted` → `seshPaused` (+10s) → `seshResumed` (+40s) → `seshEnded` (+70s), producing a genuine paused summary (40s active vs. 70s wall-clock), converts it via `createPracticeSessionRecord`, and asserts the serialize/deserialize round trip succeeds. The existing "loads valid completed sessions" test already covers the non-paused boundary (`durationMs === completedAt - startedAt` exactly) and stays green under the new `<=`. Added a rejection case to the existing malformed-record `it.each` list: `HISTORY.sessions[0]` with `durationMs` incremented by 1 (exceeding wall-clock span), confirmed still rejected. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (437 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean).

### [T032] Prevent onboarding seed data from immediately promoting the assessed rank

- **Area:** Onboarding / Profile
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Correct onboarding completion seeding so that tricks at the user's final assessed rank are not marked complete. The current seed boundary can immediately satisfy promotion requirements and advance the user one rank beyond the assessment result.
- **Exit criteria:** An integrated regression test starts assessment at Rank 4, resolves the result as Rank 3, and confirms the persisted profile remains Rank 3, Rank 3 tricks are not seeded as completed, lower prerequisite ranks remain seeded as intended, and no immediate unearned rank-up is produced.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan exactly. Root cause: `getSeedLacedTrickIds` (`src/features/onboarding/model/kentei-assessment.ts`) used the assessment's *entrance* rank (`startingRank`) as the "assume everything below is already known" boundary rather than the *resolved* rank (`state.rank`). When the very first trick at the entrance rank is failed, the resolved rank drops one below the entrance rank while `landedTrickIds` stays empty for that rank — so the old boundary seeded the entire never-verified resolved rank as laced, and `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` immediately promoted one rank further on first render. Fix: renamed the function's first parameter to `rank` and changed the filter to `trick.kenteiRank < rank`, updated the one call site in `kentei-onboarding.tsx` to pass `state.rank` instead of `state.startingRank`, and added a comment on the function naming this failure mode so it isn't silently reverted. Left `startingRank` on `KenteiAssessmentState` untouched — removing it is a broader refactor out of scope here. Added a pure-function regression test and a reducer-driven regression test in `kentei-assessment.test.ts` (entrance Rank 4 → immediate first-trick failure → resolved Rank 3, asserting Rank 1/2 tricks seeded and Rank 3 tricks excluded), and the integrated provider/reducer regression test required by the task Notes in `kentei-rank-auto-promotion.test.tsx`: drives the real `kenteiAssessmentReducer` to the same Rank 3 outcome, calls the real `getSeedLacedTrickIds`, mounts the real `UserProfileProvider`/`PracticeHistoryProvider`/`KenteiRankAutoPromotion` tree, presses a parameterized `Harness`'s `completeOnboarding(3, [], lacedTrickIds)`, and asserts no rank-up celebration renders and the persisted profile (read back via `AsyncStorage` + `deserializeUserProfile`) has `kenteiRank === 3` with Rank 1/2 tricks in `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds` and no Rank 3 tricks. That test initially failed due to AsyncStorage-mock state leaking across tests within the file (a prior test's advanced profile persisted into this one); fixed by explicitly clearing `USER_PROFILE_STORAGE_KEY` before mounting, matching the file's existing convention of explicit prior-state setup. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (440 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean).

### [T033] Require a real lace for automatic laced status

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Separate "practiced" from "laced." A trick should become automatically laced only when practice history contains at least one successful lace; merely including a trick in a zero-lace Sesh must not mark it laced.
- **Exit criteria:** Selector and integration tests confirm that a zero-lace completed Sesh leaves its tricks unlaced, a positive-lace completed Sesh marks its tricks laced, the manual override still marks a trick laced, the Tricks directory and Laced screen use the same derivation, and rank promotion follows the corrected definition.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan exactly. The bug lived in `selectUnlockedTrickIds` (`src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts`), which marked a trick unlocked as soon as any practice-history record existed for it regardless of `laceCount` — its own doc comment documented this as intended, and an existing test (`'unlocks a trick even when its only record has zero laces'`) locked in the bug. Renamed it to `selectLacedTrickIds` and changed the body to `sessions.filter((session) => session.laceCount > 0)`, mirroring the `laceCount > 0` predicate `hasPriorLaceForTrick` already uses in the practice-history domain. Renamed `selectEffectiveUnlockedTrickIds` → `selectEffectiveLacedTrickIds` (union-with-manual-override logic unchanged) and updated the doc comment above `isKenteiRankComplete`. Rank promotion (`src/features/profile/state/kentei-rank-auto-promotion.tsx`) turned out to already have its own correct inline `laceCount > 0` computation — it was never affected by the bug, contrary to a literal reading of the task description — so the fix there was deduplication: replaced the inline recompute with calls to the newly-renamed shared selectors, closing the drift risk of two independently-maintained "laced" definitions. Updated all call sites (`src/app/tricks.tsx`, `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.test.ts`, `src/features/tricks/components/laced-tricks-screen.test.tsx`) to the renamed exports; left `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds`, `toggleManualUnlock`/`removeManualUnlock`, and the `unlockedTrickIds` prop on `TrickDirectory`/`LacedTricksScreen` untouched per the task's own Notes. `trick-directory.test.ts` gained an inverted assertion for the old "unlocks a trick even when its only record has zero laces" case (now `'leaves a trick unlaced...'`, asserting `false`) plus a new case proving a trick counts as laced when any one of several sessions lands a lace, not just the first. Added the real regression coverage the exit criteria needed in `kentei-rank-auto-promotion.test.tsx`: extended `Harness` with a `recordLandingCount` prop wired to `usePracticeHistoryActions().recordCompletedSesh` (one `Pressable` per rank-1 trick, `record-${trickId}`), and two new integration tests — zero-lace completed Seshes for every rank-1 trick leave `kenteiRank` at 1 with no celebration, while landing-count-1 Seshes for every rank-1 trick promote to `kenteiRank` 2 with the celebration firing. The positive-lace test initially failed because both new tests reused the same `startedAt`/`endedAt` (`0`/`5000`), and `createPracticeSessionRecordId` derives its id from `startedAt:endedAt:trickId` — the first test's zero-lace sessions were already sitting in the shared AsyncStorage practice-history mock, so the second test's same-id landing-count-1 sessions were silently deduplicated by the reducer's existing-id check; fixed by also clearing `PRACTICE_HISTORY_STORAGE_KEY` at the start of both new tests, not just the user-profile key. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (443 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on an independent `yarn expo start --web --port 8092` server run directly in this worktree: completed a zero-lace open-ended Sesh on Big cup and confirmed it stayed unlaced in both the Tricks directory and the Laced tricks screen, then landed one lace on Big cup and confirmed it became laced (with "1 lace" shown) in both screens, then manually toggled Small cup laced on the Laced screen with no practice history and confirmed it showed laced in the Tricks directory too via the shared `unlockedTrickIds` derivation in `tricks.tsx`.

### [T035] Add a global notes archive

- **Area:** Tricks / Notes
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** Add an all-notes screen reachable from Tricks so users can review, edit, and delete notes without opening tricks one at a time.
- **Exit criteria:** Tests and browser checks at desktop and 320 px confirm that Tricks links to an All Notes screen, notes are listed with their stored trick-name snapshots and useful date context, notes can be edited and deleted there, orphaned notes remain visible and manageable after a custom trick is deleted, and the empty state is coherent.
- **Notes:** Followed the All Trick Goals screen's routing convention: a new `showAllNotes` boolean state in `src/app/tricks.tsx` gates a full-screen `AllNotesScreen`, opened via a new "All notes" secondary button alongside "Laced tricks" and "Trick goals". Unlike the goals screen, notes are rendered as a flat list sorted by `createdAt` descending (`selectAllNotesSorted`, added to `src/features/notes/model/note.ts`) rather than grouped by trick — grouping by `trickId` risked a stale section header if a trick was renamed after some of its notes were created, since each `Note` only stores an immutable `trickName` snapshot from creation time. Each row in the new `src/features/notes/components/all-notes-screen.tsx` shows its own snapshot name, an "Added <date>" or "Edited <date> · added <date>" line (using `formatPracticeDate` from `src/app/stats/index.tsx`), the body, and Edit/Delete actions reusing the same inline-confirm delete pattern as the per-trick `TrickNotesScreen`. Editing swaps in a lightweight in-file `NoteEditForm` (title shows the trick-name snapshot, not "New note" copy) rather than reusing `TrickNotesScreen`'s internal `NoteForm`, since that component isn't exported and the two screens' navigation state shapes differ. Orphaned notes need no special handling: `Note` never joins live against the custom-trick store, so a note whose `trickId` no longer resolves to any current trick still renders, edits, and deletes normally through the same code path — confirmed with a test that adds a note under a `trickId` with no corresponding trick at all. Added `src/features/notes/components/all-notes-screen.test.tsx` (empty state, snapshot listing, orphaned-note edit/delete, in-place edit, delete) following the `AllTrickGoalsScreen` test's harness/mocking conventions. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (458 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (clean), and an in-browser walkthrough on an independent `yarn expo start --web --port 8092` server run directly in this worktree: added a note from Big cup's Notes screen, confirmed it appeared in All Notes with the trick-name snapshot and "Added <date>", edited it and confirmed the "Edited ... · added ..." date context appeared, deleted it and confirmed the empty state returned, and at 320px confirmed the nav bar, header, and buttons render coherently (a `computer` click-timeout on this narrow viewport turned out to be a pre-existing environment quirk unrelated to this change — the same timeout reproduced on the pre-existing "Trick goals" button and a trick row's "Notes" link; a direct JS `.click()` dispatch confirmed the All Notes screen itself renders and functions correctly at 320px).

### [T037] Refresh Home time-based summaries while the app remains open

- **Area:** Home
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Ensure rolling totals and streak information update when time boundaries pass without a new practice-history write.
- **Exit criteria:** Fake-time tests confirm a record naturally ages out of the 24-hour summary while Home remains mounted, streak presentation updates across a local-day boundary, and returning the app to the foreground refreshes both immediately without storing timers, native handles, or current-time values in reducers or persisted state.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan exactly. Root cause confirmed during planning: `src/app/index.tsx` computed `summaryNow` from a `useState(Date.now)` value captured once at mount, so `createHomeSummary` (and the `calculateTimeLogged`/`calculateStreak` it calls, both already `now`-parameterized) never recomputed on time passing alone — only when `sessions` changed. Added `src/features/home/hooks/use-live-clock.ts`, a feature-scoped hook (`useLiveClock`) combining a 60s `setInterval` with an `AppState` `'change'` listener guarded to `state === 'active'` (the listener fires on every transition, not just foregrounding — the guard is required, not polish); both are cleaned up on unmount. No reducer, provider, or persisted state touched — purely local component state, consistent with the existing 250ms `useNow` pattern in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` but deliberately not shared with it (different concern, coarser interval). Wired into `src/app/index.tsx` in place of the frozen `mountedAt`, keeping the existing `Math.max(liveNow, latestSessionCompletedAt)` clamp (a correctness guard against a just-completed session's `completedAt` momentarily exceeding a stale tick, not dead code from the old frozen clock). Added `src/features/home/hooks/use-live-clock.test.tsx` (hook-level: initial value, interval tick, foreground-triggers-immediate-refresh, backgrounding does not, and interval/subscription cleanup on unmount) and `src/app/__tests__/index.test.tsx` (screen-level, mounting the real `CustomTrickProvider`/`UserProfileProvider`/`PracticeHistoryProvider`/`HomeScreen` tree with `AsyncStorage`-seeded profile and practice-history records under `jest.useFakeTimers()`): a record seeded 23h before a pinned "now" ages out of the "Last 24 hours" tile after advancing the fake clock 90 minutes; a two-day streak seeded across a local-midnight boundary stays correctly computed after crossing it; and a foreground `AppState` `'active'` event refreshes the aged-out tile immediately without advancing the interval timer. The screen test's "Last 24 hours" value assertions needed a tile-scoped lookup (`findTimeLoggedTileValue`) rather than a flat text search, since the unrelated "Last sesh" line renders the same mm:ss-formatted duration outside the time-windowed tile. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` (all clean).

### [T058] Let "End assessment" actually end a multi-rank run early

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** "End assessment"/"End Sesh" (`multi-trick-sesh.tsx`) only ever completes the *current* trick and lets the run's completion effect auto-advance to the next one — there is no "abort the whole run" action. For a long sequence (a full bottom-to-top or from-rank Kentei assessment, dozens of tricks) this means a user has to click through every remaining trick one at a time to reach the wrap-up screen, which reads as the button simply not working. `handleSkipRank` already proves the sequence can be jumped to its end (`advanceTrickSequenceToIndex`) — "End" needs the equivalent.
- **Exit criteria:** Test: starting a full (multi-rank) Kentei assessment and pressing "End assessment" partway through immediately reaches the wrap-up screen, not the next trick; the current trick's landings-so-far are recorded exactly as today's per-trick End already does; remaining un-started tricks are not recorded as attempted (consistent with "Skip this rank" writing no history for tricks it skips). As part of verifying this fix, also manually confirm the rank-skip messaging on the wrap-up screen (`describeSkippedRange`/`WrapUpSkippedText`) renders correctly when a rank was skipped during the run this fix makes it fast to reach — this closes out the one item PR 55's QA checklist left unchecked ("confirm rank-skip messaging is correct when a rank is skipped"), which the messaging logic itself doesn't need a fix for (see below) but which couldn't practically be exercised until now.
- **Notes:** Implemented per the committed plan (`.claude/plans/T058.md`), adapted to `multi-trick-sesh.tsx`'s current shape — the file had grown considerably since the plan was drafted (T047's Cancel/onCancelRun, T056's skip confirmation flow, T057's conditional "Next trick", `pauseAfterLace`, `showRankStanding`), so the fix targets `handleEndRun`/the `completed` effect as they exist now rather than the plan's line-number-specific snippets, but the core mechanism is unchanged: a new `endRunRequestedRef`, set synchronously in a new `handleEndRun` wired to `ActiveSesh`'s `onEnd` (replacing the old inline `dispatch({ type: 'seshEnded' })`), read and cleared by the `completed` effect to jump the sequence to its end (`advanceTrickSequenceToIndex(prev, prev.tricks.length)`) plus `dispatch({ type: 'seshReset' })` instead of advancing to the next trick — the same shape `handleConfirmSkip` already uses when a skip lands past the last trick. `onNextTrick` and the timer/first-lace `seshEnded` dispatches are untouched and still auto-advance. Updated `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx`'s pre-existing "does not advance on a single lace... requiring an explicit End Sesh" test (its second half asserted the now-wrong "press End Sesh → advances to the next trick" behavior) and added a new `'MultiTrickSesh "End Sesh" ends the run early'` describe block: ending mid-run with laces-so-far, ending after mixing in "Next trick", ending via a custom `endActionLabel` ("End assessment"), and — standing in for the exit criteria's "manually confirm rank-skip wrap-up messaging" instruction, since this session had no browser-preview tool available — an automated test combining a partial "Skip this rank" with a later "End assessment" press, asserting the wrap-up shows both attempted-trick entries and the skipped-range entry with correct copy in the same render. Also fixed one incidental regression in `assessment-entry.test.tsx` ("shows 'held' copy...") and one in the T047 `onCancelRun` describe block in `multi-trick-sesh.test.tsx` ("does not render Cancel on the second trick countdown, even after the first completes normally"), both of which relied on the old "End" only ends the current trick to walk multi-trick sequences — replaced their walking presses with "Next trick"/left the single "End" press as the terminal action. Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes; `yarn install` was needed first, since this worktree's `node_modules` was missing on entry.

### [T034] Align Kentei assessment language with promotion-only behavior

- **Area:** Sesh / Profile
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Present the existing Kentei flow as a rank challenge rather than an authoritative reassessment. The current state model prevents rank regression, while some assessment language implies the result can replace the user's current rank.
- **Exit criteria:** Entry, in-session, result, and Profile copy consistently describe a challenge or promotion opportunity; tests and a browser walkthrough confirm the UI never claims a lower result will lower or replace the current rank and clearly distinguishes retaining the current rank from advancing.
- **Notes:** Implementation matched the plan exactly. Confirmed by tracing the call chain that the real, timed Kentei assessment flow (`AssessmentEntry` → `MultiTrickSesh` → practice history → `KenteiRankAutoPromotion`) is genuinely monotonic (`selectPromotedKenteiRank` in `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts` only ever increments), so this was legitimately copy-only — no domain-model rescope needed. Left the separate onboarding "Retake assessment" section on `src/app/profile/index.tsx` untouched per the plan's explicit non-goal: that flow (`restartOnboarding`/`completeOnboarding`) genuinely can lower or replace the rank, so its existing "updates your rank" copy stays accurate and rewriting it to promotion-only language would make it less accurate. Added `selectAssessmentResultCopy(startingRank, resultRank)` to `src/features/sesh/model/kentei-assessment-run.ts`, a pure three-way selector (not-yet-earned / advanced / held) replacing the old "You're assessed at Rank N" ternary in `assessment-entry.tsx`'s `wrapUpFooter`. Extended `AssessmentEntryStep`'s `running` variant with `startingKenteiRank: number | undefined`, captured at each of the three `setStep({ kind: 'running', ... })} call sites from `useUserProfile()` at click time (a true pre-run snapshot, safe because it runs synchronously before `MultiTrickSesh` or `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` can react). The wrap-up footer now calls `selectAssessmentResultCopy(step.startingKenteiRank, kenteiRank)`, where the second `kenteiRank` is still read live and reflects any auto-promotion from practice recorded during the run. `src/app/profile/index.tsx`'s "Kentei assessment" section needed no copy change — it already read as promotion-only. Added unit coverage for the new selector in `kentei-assessment-run.test.ts` (all three branches, plus the "first rank ever earned counts as advance, not hold" edge case) and new component-level coverage in `src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.test.tsx` (new file — no prior test existed for this component), driving the real `MultiTrickSesh`/`KenteiRankAutoPromotion` wiring end-to-end for both the "held" case (ending every rank-1 trick via "End assessment" without lacing) and the "advanced" case (lacing every rank-1 trick, exercising real auto-promotion). Also added `src/app/profile/__tests__/index.test.tsx` as a guard on the Profile screen's copy — deviated from the plan's suggested path (`src/app/profile/index.test.tsx`) after discovering mid-implementation that Expo Router's default `require.context` treats every file under `src/app` as a route regardless of extension (confirmed empirically: placing the test directly under `src/app/profile/` produced a spurious `/profile/index.test` entry in `yarn expo export --platform web`'s route list); Metro's file-map excludes `__tests__` directories by default, so moving the test there removes the stray route while keeping the import (`@/app/profile/index`) unchanged. Verified by `yarn test --runInBand` (451 tests passing), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web` (clean, 12 static routes with no stray test route), and an in-browser walkthrough on an independent `yarn expo start --web --port 8099` server run directly in this worktree: seeded a hydrated, onboarded Rank 1 profile via `localStorage`, ran "Current rank only" ending every trick via "End assessment" without lacing and confirmed the wrap-up read "You held Rank 1. Land every trick in a rank to advance." with no assessed/replaced language, then re-ran the same flow lacing every trick and confirmed "Rank up! You're now Rank 2.", the unchanged `RankUpCelebrationModal` still fired with its own already-promotion-only copy ("You leveled up to Rank 2."), and the Profile screen's rank tile read 02 afterward. Re-checked the 320px web nav width (unaffected, copy/selector-only change).

### [T046] Fix transient timer glitch on Sesh pause/resume

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Resuming a paused sesh briefly renders a displayed time equal to the pause duration before flipping to the correct value, for both timed (counts down) and open-ended (counts up) plans. Root cause: `useNow` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) freezes `now` entirely while paused and only refreshes it via a 250ms `setInterval` that doesn't (re)start until after the resume render commits, so for one or more renders a freshly up-to-date `pausedDurationMs` (already includes the full pause) combines with a stale `now` (unchanged since before/at the pause), under-counting elapsed time by exactly the pause duration.
- **Exit criteria:** Test simulates pause at time T, waits several seconds while paused, then resumes — asserts the *first* rendered displayed time after resume is already correct (no transient wrong frame), for both a timed and an open-ended plan. `sesh.test.tsx` currently uses `jest.useFakeTimers()` but has no test that advances fake time across an actual pause→resume transition; this is new coverage, not a regression fix to an existing test.
- **Notes:** Implemented per the committed plan. `useNow` in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx` now tracks the previous `isPaused` value in a `useRef` and, in a `useLayoutEffect` keyed on `isPaused`, calls `setNow(Date.now())` synchronously (before paint) on the `true → false` (resume) transition only — this replaces waiting on the next 250ms interval tick. Deviated from the plan's originally proposed render-time `setState` correction: the React Compiler's `react-hooks/purity` eslint rule (enabled in this repo) rejects calling `Date.now()` (an impure function) directly in the render body, so the fix moved the same edge-detection logic into `useLayoutEffect`, which still commits before the browser paints and is lint-clean. Added two new regression tests in `sesh.test.tsx` (`ActiveSesh pause/resume timer accuracy (regression)`) driving `ActiveSesh` directly through a pause→5s-wait→resume prop sequence for both a timed and an open-ended plan, asserting the first post-resume render already shows the correct elapsed/remaining time (`formatRemainingDuration(290_000)` / `formatDuration(10_000)`) and not the buggy pause-duration-only value; manually reverted the fix locally to confirm both new tests fail without it, then restored it. Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes after running `yarn install` in this worktree, whose `node_modules` was missing on entry.

### [T045] Auto-dismiss in-sesh celebration banners

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** During an active sesh, `CelebrationBanner`-based banners (`FirstLaceBanner`, `PersonalBestBanner`, and `GoalCelebrationBanner` for both count- and time-based goals, all in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) have an entrance animation but no exit animation or timer — each stays visible until the next "Laced" press flips its underlying `landings.length`-derived condition false. Make them auto-fade after a few seconds instead.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms each of the four banner types dismisses on its own a few seconds after appearing (fake timers), without requiring another Laced press; the time-based goal celebration in particular no longer latches for the rest of the sesh once shown (today `celebratingTimeGoalIds` in `sesh.tsx` is append-only and nothing ever clears it — worse than the other three, which at least clear on the next lace). `CompletedSesh`'s wrap-up rendering of `FirstLaceBanner`/`GoalCelebrationBanner` is a static summary screen with no Laced button to race against and is unaffected by this change; `RankUpCelebrationModal` (`src/features/profile/components/rank-up-celebration-modal.tsx`) is a separate, deliberately tap-to-dismiss mechanism, also unaffected.
- **Notes:** Implemented per the committed plan: `CelebrationBanner` in `sesh.tsx` now takes an `autoDismiss?: boolean` prop and runs a second, mount-once `useEffect` with two `setTimeout`s (`CELEBRATION_VISIBLE_MS = 4000`, `CELEBRATION_FADE_MS = 250`) — one to start a symmetric `Animated.timing` fade to `0`, one to flip local `dismissed` state and render `null`. `PersonalBestBanner` hardcodes `autoDismiss`; `FirstLaceBanner`/`GoalCelebrationBanner` pass `autoDismiss={tense === 'inSesh'}`, leaving `CompletedSesh`'s wrap-up rendering untouched. Fixed the time-goal latch at its root by deleting `celebratedTimeGoalIdsRef`/`celebratingTimeGoalIds` and filtering `timeGoalCelebrations` directly off the existing `crossedTimeGoalIds` memo — the banner's own `dismissed` state (persisted across renders since React reuses the same keyed instance) now does the only work needed to prevent re-showing. `useRef` stays imported since `recordedSummaryId` still uses it elsewhere in the file. Updated `findBanner`/`findGoalBanner`/`findPersonalBestBanner` test helpers to match only the rendered host `View` (`node.type === 'View'`), not the `CelebrationBanner`/wrapper component instances — those still carry a matching `accessibilityLabel` prop even after auto-dismiss makes them render `null`, since the prop is passed down rather than consumed, which was silently producing false-positive matches. Discovered and worked around a jest fake-timers quirk: a `setTimeout` registered by an effect that itself runs because of a state update from *inside* an already-executing `setInterval` callback (i.e. a component mounting mid-tick of `useNow`'s 250ms poll) does not reliably fire on later `jest.advanceTimersByTime` calls if those calls are large single jumps — even many seconds' worth. The time-based-goal test (the one case where the banner mounts via that exact interval-tick path) advances past the dismiss window in a loop of 250ms steps instead of one or two large jumps; the other three tests mount the banner via an explicit `root.update` and were unaffected. Full verification (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`) passes after running `yarn install` in this worktree, whose `node_modules` was missing on entry.

- **Priority:** Low

- **Description:** The assessment wrap-up's final button is labeled "Finish sesh" and navigates to `/profile` (`onFinished` in `src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx`) — both the copy and the destination are wrong for this context; the user should land on Home.
- **Exit criteria:** Completing a Kentei assessment and tapping the final wrap-up button lands on Home (`/`); its copy describes that destination rather than "Finish sesh."
- **Notes:** `assessment-entry.tsx` now calls `onFinished={() => router.replace('/')}`. The wrap-up's finish-button copy was hardcoded ("Finish sesh") inside the shared `MultiTrickWrapUp` used by both the assessment and ad hoc multi-trick flows, so a new optional `finishButtonLabel` prop (default `'Finish sesh'`) was threaded through `MultiTrickSesh` -> `MultiTrickWrapUp`'s `FinishButton` text/`accessibilityLabel`, letting `assessment-entry.tsx` pass `"Go to Home"` without touching `multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx`'s default copy or its separate `handleFinished` (which just resets local state, per the task's own note). `RankUpCelebrationModal` is unaffected, as expected. ### [T060] Explain when a Kentei rank didn't advance after an assessment

- **Area:** Sesh / Profile (Kentei assessment)
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `selectPromotedKenteiRank` (`src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts`) requires gapless completion from the user's current stored rank upward, by design — confirmed correct, not a bug. But "Start from a rank" and "Skip this rank" both let a user generate real lace history far above their stored rank while lower ranks stay incomplete (or, for skipped ranks, get zero history at all), which permanently caps promotion at the first gap with no explanation anywhere in the UI. Per product decision, promotion logic itself is not changing — add messaging so this isn't silent.
- **Exit criteria:** Completing an out-of-order assessment (e.g. via "Start from a rank" above the current stored rank, or after skipping ranks) that does not result in a promotion shows copy explaining that earlier/skipped ranks still need to be cleared; a normal in-order assessment that does promote shows no such message.
- **Notes:** Added two pure selectors in `kentei-assessment-run.ts`: `selectStartedAboveStoredRank` (true only for a `fromRank` flow starting strictly above the profile's stored rank) and `selectAssessmentGapNotice` (returns explanatory copy when out-of-order and not promoted, undefined otherwise), each with their own unit tests. `AssessmentEntry` tracks a new `startedAboveStoredRank` flag per running step plus local `skippedDuringRun` state (reset at every flow-start), combines them into `wasOutOfOrder`, and renders the notice as a second `accessibilityLabel="Assessment gap notice"` element beside the existing wrap-up result copy. The only available signal for "a skip happened" is the existing `describeSkippedRange` callback, which `MultiTrickSesh` also invokes during its own render (to preview the skip-confirmation description) — setting state synchronously there triggered a setState-during-a-different-component's-render warning, so the flag flip is deferred via a resolved-Promise microtask (plain `queueMicrotask` is faked and never flushes under this suite's `jest.useFakeTimers()`). Also added the plan's recommended pre-run heads-up copy on the "choosingRank" step, below `RankGrid`, when `kenteiRank` is defined. `selectPromotedKenteiRank` itself is untouched, per the task's own decision. Verified with `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (full suite).

### [T062.1] Tag practice-history records with their originating flow

- **Area:** persistence (practice-history domain)
- **Priority:** Medium (inherited from parent T062)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add an optional origin/source field to `PracticeSessionRecord` (`src/features/stats/model/practice-history.ts`) and thread it through from wherever a session is recorded, so a Kentei-assessment-sourced landing can be distinguished from regular Sesh/ad hoc multi-trick practice at read time.
- **Exit criteria:** Test confirms a session recorded via the Kentei assessment flow carries a distinguishing value on this new field, a session recorded via regular Sesh or ad hoc multi-trick does not (or carries the "regular" value), and existing persisted records with no value at all round-trip correctly (treated as regular practice, no migration required).
- **Notes:** `createPracticeSessionRecord` (`practice-history.ts`) is the single construction site for every `PracticeSessionRecord`, called from both `Sesh` (`sesh.tsx`) and `MultiTrickSesh` (`multi-trick-sesh.tsx`, shared by both the ad hoc multi-trick route and `AssessmentEntry`) — the new field needs to originate as a prop/param passed into `MultiTrickSesh`/`Sesh` (distinguishing `AssessmentEntry`'s usage from `MultiTrickSeshEntry`'s) and flow through `SeshSummary` to the record. Practice-history's persistence schema/validator (`src/features/stats/persistence/practice-history-persistence.ts`) needs the corresponding optional-field update. This is a persistence-domain schema change per AGENTS.md. Added `PracticeSessionOrigin = 'assessment'` and an optional `origin` field to `PracticeSessionRecord`, threaded through `sessionRecorded`/`createPracticeSessionRecord`, the persistence validator (accepts `undefined` or `'assessment'`, so legacy documents load unmodified with no migration), `PracticeHistoryActions.recordCompletedSesh`, and an optional `origin` prop on both `MultiTrickSeshProps` and `SeshProps`, forwarded from their existing `recordCompletedSesh` call sites. `AssessmentEntry` passes `origin="assessment"` to `MultiTrickSesh`; `MultiTrickSeshEntry` and the single-trick `Sesh` route pass nothing (regular practice). The Sesh reducer itself was deliberately left untouched — origin never needed to reach `SeshState`/`SeshSummary`. New/extended tests cover `createPracticeSessionRecord`/`practiceHistoryReducer` origin threading, persistence round-trip/backward-compat/rejection, `MultiTrickSesh`'s origin pass-through, `AssessmentEntry`'s end-to-end tagging, and regular `Sesh`'s no-origin case. Verified with `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (full suite).

### [T061] Remove Profile's "Retake assessment" section in favor of Kentei Assessment

- **Area:** Profile

Remove the former; keep the latter as Profile's sole assessment entry point.

- **Notes:** Confirmed surgical: "Retake assessment" only calls `restartOnboarding()` (dispatches `onboardingRestarted`) — it imports nothing from `src/features/onboarding/` and shares no component code with it. Removing the button and its Profile-side wiring cannot affect the onboarding flow itself, since nothing in `src/features/onboarding/` references Profile. Per AGENTS.md, onboarding's own flow must not be modified by this task — confirmed unnecessary here, since the two are already independent. Removed the "Assessment"/"Retake assessment" section (SectionLabel, Title, Copy, RetakeButton) and the `restartOnboarding` destructure from `src/app/profile/index.tsx`; `restartOnboarding` remains exported from `user-profile-provider.tsx` (used only there now) since onboarding itself must stay untouched, per the task's own scope.
