### [T064] Baseline goal progress at creation time

- **Area:** Goals
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `selectGoalProgress` (`src/features/goals/model/goal.ts`) sums laces/duration across *all* historical practice sessions for a goal's trick, with no filter against when the goal was created — so a goal set against a trick with existing lace history shows inflated progress immediately, e.g. a trick with 50 prior laces and a new "land it 100 times" goal reads 50/100 on creation, not 0/100.
- **Exit criteria:** Test: a trick with N prior laces, then a new goal with target M created against it — progress reads 0/M immediately after creation, not N/M; lacing the trick after creation increments progress normally; existing in-Sesh goal-achievement-celebration detection (`isGoalNewlyAchieved`/`goalBaselines` in `sesh.tsx` — an unrelated use of "baseline," see note) continues to fire correctly, unaffected by this change.
- **Notes:** `Goal`/`GoalBase` (`goal.ts`) already carries a `createdAt` timestamp — this doesn't need a new persisted field or schema/migration, just filtering `selectGoalProgress`'s session sum to `session.completedAt >= goal.createdAt`. Marked Required despite the small fix because `selectGoalProgress` has several call sites (`trick-goals-screen.tsx`, `all-trick-goals-screen.tsx`/`selectGoalProgressByTrick`, and four places in `sesh.tsx`) and the file already uses "baseline" for a different, unrelated concept (`goalBaselines` = progress snapshotted at trick-selection time *within* a sesh, used to detect newly-crossed goals mid-session) — worth a careful pass to avoid conflating the two or missing a call site. Fixed with a single added clause in `selectGoalProgress`'s session filter (`session.completedAt >= goal.createdAt`); every call site (`trick-goals-screen.tsx`, `all-trick-goals-screen.tsx`/`selectGoalProgressByTrick`, and all `sesh.tsx` sites including `goalBaselines`) delegates through it and needed no changes. `goalBaselines`/`isGoalNewlyAchieved` in `sesh.tsx` is confirmed unaffected in kind (still an unrelated in-sesh snapshot concept) and in fact now correctly reflects the creation-time baseline instead of inflated pre-existing history. Added a new `selectGoalProgress` test covering prior-history exclusion, the `completedAt === createdAt` boundary, and post-creation increment. Two existing tests (`sesh.test.tsx`'s "reflects the just-completed sesh..." regression test and `all-trick-goals-screen.test.tsx`'s multi-trick listing test) constructed goals via a real `Date.now()` while using a fixed, small `summary.endedAt` (5000) as the recorded session's `completedAt` — that combination always failed the new `createdAt` filter, since it relied on the pre-fix inflated-progress bug rather than intended behavior. Fixed by pinning `Date.now()` to 0 for the duration of each test so the goal's `createdAt` precedes the fixture's fixed `completedAt`, matching how both timestamps derive from the same real clock in production. Verified with `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (full suite).

### [T065] Show a per-trick goal count in the Tricks list

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** Each trick row's "Goals" action (`src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx`) always renders the bare label "Goals," unlike the adjacent "Notes" action, which shows a count via T024's `selectNoteCountByTrickId` pattern. Add the equivalent for goals.
- **Exit criteria:** Test: a trick with N goals shows "Goals — N" on its row; a trick with 0 goals shows plain "Goals" — matching the notes-count zero-state convention exactly.
- **Notes:** Direct mirror of T024. Added `selectGoalCountByTrickId(goals)` in `src/features/tricks/model/trick-directory.ts` next to `selectNoteCountByTrickId`, importing `Goal` as a type from `@/features/goals/model/goal`; sums goals per trick ID, leaving tricks with zero goals absent from the map (matching the notes-count convention). `TrickDirectory` gained an optional `goalCountByTrickId?: ReadonlyMap<string, number>` prop threaded through `extraData` into a new `goalCount` prop (default `0`) on `TrickRow`; the Goals action label changed from static `Goals` to `{goalCount > 0 ? `Goals — ${goalCount}` : 'Goals'}`. Wired real data in `src/app/tricks.tsx` via `useGoals()` and `useMemo(() => selectGoalCountByTrickId(goals), [goals])`, passed into `<TrickDirectory>` alongside the existing `noteCountByTrickId`. Count includes all goals regardless of achieved status, per the task's stated assumption. New tests: `selectGoalCountByTrickId` describe block in `trick-directory.test.ts` (mirrors the note-count tests — summing, zero-count absence, no cross-trick bleed) and two component tests in `trick-directory.test.tsx` asserting `Goals — 2` renders for a trick with goals and plain `Goals` (no `—` anywhere) for a trick with none. Verified with `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test/lint/typecheck/web-export all pass).

### [T068] Return to Tricks home when the Tricks tab is pressed from a nested view

- **Area:** Navigation / Tricks
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Every nested view under Tricks (trick edit/delete, notes, goals, laced-tricks grid, custom-trick creation — all local `useState` in `src/app/tricks.tsx`) is invisible to the tab bar itself, since the URL never leaves `/tricks`. Pressing the Tricks tab while already on one of these views is a confirmed complete no-op on web today — `expo-router/ui`'s `TabTrigger` only dispatches a tab switch when the pressed tab is *not* already focused, and it already is. Make re-pressing Tricks from a nested view return to the trick list.
- **Exit criteria:** From any of the nested Tricks views listed above, pressing the Tricks tab returns to the trick list — verified on web, iOS, and Android (native uses a different tab primitive, `NativeTabs`, which may already handle this via a platform default or may not — confirm rather than assume).
- **Notes:** This is a narrower, faster fix than [T069]'s full route conversion, addressing the same reported friction without waiting on that larger effort — likely superseded/adapted once (or if) screens migrate to real routes there. Implementation deviated from the Notes' original guess: rather than detecting "tab pressed while focused" in `app-tabs.web.tsx`/`app-tabs.tsx`, the fix reads `expo-router`'s actual source (both `TabTrigger` on web and `NativeTabs` on native always emit a standard React Navigation `tabPress` event on every press, including when already focused, before the redundant-switch guard). `src/app/tricks.tsx` now calls `useNavigation()` and subscribes to `tabPress`, resetting all 7 nested-view `useState` selectors back to their list defaults when `navigation.isFocused()` is true — a single platform-agnostic fix, no changes needed to either `app-tabs` file. `navigation.addListener('tabPress' as never, ...)` uses a narrowly scoped cast because `expo-router`'s `useNavigation()` return type doesn't include `BottomTabNavigationEventMap`. Verified via `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test/lint/typecheck/web-export all pass); this session's tooling had no interactive browser/simulator access, so the fix relies on the source-level confirmation in the plan (`.claude/plans/T068.md`) rather than hands-on click-through on web/iOS/Android — a human or a session with preview/simulator tooling should do a quick manual pass across the nested views listed in the exit criteria before treating iOS/Android as fully confirmed.

Native device validation is deliberately deferred under AGENTS.md's active platform scope.
- **Notes:** Established `/tricks/[trickId]/goals` as a headerless child-stack route. `TrickGoalsScreen` now receives route-owned `onBack` and presents "Back to Tricks"; it never depends on history, so a direct URL deterministically returns to `/tricks`. The `tricks` layout listens only for the Tricks tab's `tabPress` and replaces the child route with `/tricks`, while the index route continues to reset T069.2's remaining local pseudo-screens through the parent tab navigator. The destination's internal `list`/`add` steps intentionally remain local state: Cancel/Save return to list, only the screen-level Back leaves the route; T069.2 must apply the same convention to Notes rather than route unsaved form state. A privileged Expo web server started correctly, but the in-app browser's isolated localhost namespace refused that server; the interactive click-through is therefore unperformed, though the compiled route tree and behavior are covered by automated checks.

### [T081] Preselect direct-practice trick on the Sesh screen with a cancel affordance

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Starting a Sesh from a direct-practice link (e.g. `src/features/stats/components/neglected-tricks-section.tsx:37`, `src/features/goals/components/trick-goals-screen.tsx:141`, `src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx`'s `showPracticeAction` row) passes `trickId` and `mode` params to `/sesh`, but `src/app/sesh/index.tsx:16-29` never reads `mode`, and `Sesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) always starts in the general `TrickPicker` choosing state — `preferredTrickId` only highlights that trick in the full picker instead of preselecting it. Fix this so a direct-practice link renders only the linked trick as a card on the Sesh screen (format/duration/target still adjustable before starting), with no other trick cards shown, plus a "Cancel practice" control that returns to the standard Sesh choosing screen.
- **Exit criteria:** Starting a Sesh via a direct-practice link shows only the linked trick's card (no other tricks listed), format and duration/target remain editable before starting, and a visible "Cancel practice" control returns the user to the standard multi-trick Sesh choosing screen. Automated coverage exercises the new reducer/state path where practical.
- **Notes:** `seshReducer`'s `'choosing'` state now carries an optional `directPracticeTrickId`; `Sesh` lazily initializes its reducer from the `directPracticeTrickId` prop, and `src/app/sesh/index.tsx` computes it from the `trickId`/`mode=practice` route params (separate from the existing `preferredTrickId`, which still just prioritizes within the full picker for plain visits). When set and the trick is found in `availableTricks`, `TrickPicker` renders a single-trick card with direct-practice heading copy, no "Practice several tricks" promo link, and a "Cancel practice" button (`accessibilityLabel="Cancel practice"`) that dispatches the existing `'seshReset'` action, per the plan's design. Falls back to the normal multi-trick picker if the linked trick isn't found (e.g. deleted custom trick), and works even with an empty focused practice list. New reducer tests cover `seshReset` clearing the field and `trickSelected` still transitioning normally from the direct-practice choosing state; new component tests cover single-card rendering, editable timed/open-ended plans, the Cancel control, the unavailable-trick fallback, and the empty-practice-list case. Full verification (`test`, `lint`, `tsc`, web export) passes; native validation remains deferred under the active platform scope.

As a result the picker paints edge-to-edge on the all-trick Trick Goals screen.

Verified in-browser (headless Chrome via Playwright against the worktree's own dev server): the picker's row text now sits at the same x-offset (66px) as the Tricks tab at a 320px viewport, and a full goal-creation flow through the picker (choose a trick, set a lace-count target, save) completes successfully. ### [T094] Make the web splash overlay reflect the user's theme

- **Area:** App shell / Theming
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Two distinct splashes exist: the native `expo-splash-screen` (`app.json`, build-time-only `"#208AEF"`, cannot react to runtime state) and the web-only `AnimatedSplashOverlay` (`src/components/animated-icon.web.tsx`), rendered from `src/app/_layout.tsx:24` before `UserProfileProvider`/`AppThemeProvider` mount. Make `AnimatedSplashOverlay` reflect the user's last-applied theme background (or the app's default theme background pre-selection) by reading a synchronously-available cached value ahead of normal provider hydration. Update `app.json`'s static splash `backgroundColor` to match the app's default theme background as the build-time baseline.
- **Exit criteria:** A user who has saved a custom background color sees `AnimatedSplashOverlay` render in that color on the next web refresh, verified in-browser (reload, computed-style check before the main app UI mounts). A user with no saved theme sees it render in the app's default theme background. `app.json`'s static `backgroundColor` literal is updated to match the resolved default.
- **Notes:** The overlay mounts before `AppThemeProvider` resolves theme through its normal async-hydration path, so this needs a synchronous pre-hydration read rather than consuming `ResolvedTheme` through context — this is the architectural risk that made the ticket `Required` rather than `Implementer`. The native `app.json` splash literal can only ever reflect the app's default, not a live per-user value — that limitation is inherent to build-time native config, not a shortfall of this ticket. Implemented a new DI'd, unit-tested `readCachedThemeBackgroundColor` (`src/features/profile/persistence/read-cached-theme-background.ts`), which synchronously parses `window.localStorage`'s cached user-profile document (reusing `deserializeUserProfile`) and resolves it through `resolveTheme`. `animated-icon.web.tsx` adopts the same `useSyncExternalStore`-based hydration-safe pattern already established in `use-color-scheme.web.ts` to avoid an SSR crash and a hydration-mismatch warning, rendering the resolved default (`AppColors.sage`, `#dfe8ca`) on the server/first paint and swapping to the real cached color synchronously before the browser's next paint. `app.json`'s splash `backgroundColor` updated from `#208AEF` to `#dfe8ca` to match; the overlay's previous hardcoded `#f4c94d` was actually the accent-yellow default, not the background-sage default, so this is a real visual fix even for users with no saved theme. Verified in-browser via a scripted headless-Chrome CDP session (CPU-throttled, network-cache disabled) driving two reload scenarios: with no saved profile the overlay's computed `background-color` is `rgb(223, 232, 202)` (`#dfe8ca`) throughout; with a saved custom `themeBackgroundColor` (`#ff00ff`) the overlay renders the sage default first (pre-hydration/SSR paint) then swaps to `rgb(255, 0, 255)` before dismissing, with no hydration-mismatch console warning (only the two pre-existing known-benign warnings). Full web-only verification passes; native validation remains deferred under the active platform scope.

### [T080] Fix Kentei assessment exit and stale wrap-up leak

- **Area:** Navigation / Sesh
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** The Kentei assessment wrap-up screen's "Go to Home" button (`src/features/sesh/components/assessment-entry.tsx:65-66`) calls `router.dismissTo('/')`, but the wrap-up screen lives on the nested Profile stack (`/profile/kentei-assessment`, pushed via `router.push` in `src/app/profile/index.tsx:56`) and `'/'` is not part of that stack's history, so the call is a no-op and the button does nothing. Separately, `src/app/profile/_layout.tsx` has no `tabPress` listener resetting its stack on tab re-press, unlike `src/app/sesh/_layout.tsx` and `src/app/tricks/_layout.tsx` — so if a user routes around the stuck wrap-up screen, re-entering Profile can still show the stale wrap-up instead of the normal Profile screen.
- **Exit criteria:** From the Kentei wrap-up screen, pressing "Go to Home" navigates to the Home tab. Separately, after completing a Kentei assessment and pressing "Go to Home," navigating to Profile shows the normal Profile screen, not a stale wrap-up — checked both immediately and after re-pressing the Profile tab.
- **Notes:** Replaced the no-op `router.dismissTo('/')` (target href not present in the Profile stack's own history) with `router.navigate('/')`, which resolves across the full route tree to the Home tab. Added a `tabPress` listener to `src/app/profile/_layout.tsx` mirroring the existing pattern in `sesh/_layout.tsx` / `tricks/_layout.tsx`, resetting the Profile stack to `/profile` on every Profile-tab press so a stale wrap-up screen never reappears, whether checked immediately after "Go to Home" or after a later re-press. New `src/app/profile/__tests__/layout.test.tsx` covers the listener; `assessment-entry.test.tsx` updated for the `navigate` mock. Full web-only verification passes; native validation remains deferred under the active platform scope.

### [T086] Add a visual color picker for theme colors

- **Area:** Profile / Theming
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Replace `ColorField`'s raw hex-only `TextInput` in `ThemeColorPicker` (`src/features/profile/components/theme-color-picker.tsx`) with a real visual color-picker control (swatches/spectrum/sliders) for background and accent color, vetted for a free/permissive license and React Native Web compatibility. Keep manual hex entry available as a secondary input.
- **Exit criteria:** Component tests confirm picking a color via the visual picker saves it through the same `onSave`/profile-provider path as today, manual hex entry still works, and an in-browser check confirms the picker renders and functions on web.
- **Notes:** Corresponds to story S001. No third-party color-picker library is currently a project dependency — vet license (MIT/BSD/Apache-2.0 only) and React Native Web support before adding one; a hand-built picker (e.g. HSV wheel + slider) is an acceptable fallback if no suitable library is found. Touches the same file as T085.2 — expect to coordinate/rebase if both are in flight together. Implemented a hand-built HSV picker (`src/features/profile/components/color-spectrum-picker.tsx`) using `react-native-svg` (MIT, Expo-supported, renders real `<svg>` on web) for the gradient square/hue bar, and RN's raw Responder System (`onResponderGrant`/`onResponderMove` directly on the `View`, not `PanResponder`) for drag handling, since `PanResponder`'s internal gesture-state math requires a full `touchHistory` (`pageX`/`pageY` touch bank) that plain synthetic `nativeEvent.locationX/Y` objects can't satisfy in `react-test-renderer` — a deviation from the plan's PanResponder approach, functionally equivalent but simpler to test. New `src/shared/color/hsv.ts` (HSV↔hex conversion, unit-tested) backs it. Wired into `ColorField` via `onChange={setHexText}`, reusing the existing hex-`TextInput`/Save/`onSave` path unchanged. Component and integration tests cover corner-case drags on both the SV square and hue bar; existing manual-hex-entry tests pass unmodified. In-browser check on `yarn expo start --web`: dragging the SV square and hue bar live-updates the swatch/hex text/contrast hint, Save persists, and after reload the picker's puck/gradient resync to the saved color; manual hex entry still works and the picker resyncs to it after Save (by design, not on every keystroke, to avoid disrupting an in-progress drag). Full web-only verification passes; native validation remains deferred under the active platform scope.

Deviated from the literal exit-criteria wording for the laced button's label/count color: wiring it to `theme.text` (as originally specified) turns out to be genuinely illegible in the current default theme, because `deriveAutomaticButtonBackgrounds` and `deriveAutomaticTextColor` both independently maximize contrast against the same page background and land on the same fixed neutral (`AppColors.ink`) -- dark text on an identically dark button. ### [T102] Resync the color picker's swatch/hex text when a preset changes the saved color

- **Area:** Profile / Theming
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `ColorField`'s `hexText` (`theme-color-picker.tsx:93`) is seeded via `useState` on mount only and never resyncs when `persistedValue`/`fallbackColor` change later — e.g. after selecting a preset via `setThemeColors`, which updates profile state but leaves the picker's swatch/hex text showing the stale prior color. Add a resync effect, mirroring the pattern `ColorSpectrumPicker` already uses for its own resync (`color-spectrum-picker.tsx:58-67`), so `hexText` updates when `persistedValue` changes to a new defined value, without clobbering an in-progress manual edit.
- **Exit criteria:** Component test: mount `ThemeColorPicker`, simulate applying a preset (parent re-renders with new `persistedValue` props matching what `setThemeColors` produces), and assert each `ColorField`'s swatch/hex text update to the preset's values without remounting. Existing hex-input-then-save and drag-then-save tests continue to pass unmodified.
- **Notes:** Story S018. See T101's note on file overlap. Implemented the resync using the same guarded-setState-during-render pattern `ColorSpectrumPicker`'s own `persistedHex` resync uses (not a `useEffect`), so it resyncs `hexText` within the same commit as an external `setThemeColors` call rather than in a follow-up render, avoiding a visible flash of the stale value. Added a new test that applies a preset via `setThemeColors` from *outside* the `ColorField` (through a probe component calling `useUserProfileActions`), distinct from the field's own hex-input-then-save flow, and asserts both fields' hex text/swatch update without remounting.

### [T109] Mobile-web cold-start correctness pass

- **Area:** Web launch surface (cross-cutting)
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** Drive the cold-start flow (onboarding intro → Kentei assessment → Tricks/Sesh) in the browser preview tool at representative mobile-web viewport widths (375px and 320px) and fix any console errors, broken states, or layout breaks found.
- **Exit criteria:** With the dev server running, navigating the full cold-start flow at 375px and 320px shows zero console errors or warnings beyond the two known-benign ones documented in AGENTS.md, no visually broken or clipped layout, and no dead-end states; any defect found is fixed in this commit if it's self-contained to one area, otherwise filed as a new `Needs scoping` entry rather than expanding this ticket's scope.
- **Notes:** Drove the full flow (intro → level select → assessment loop, both "Landed it"/"Not yet" branches and all four Kentei levels tried → rank reveal → trick selection → theme step → `/sesh`, then Tricks tab) at 375px and 320px via a scripted browser session, checking console output and DOM-level overflow on every step. Found and fixed two self-contained defects, both pre-existing (not specific to any one viewport): (1) `TrickCard`'s `box-shadow` CSS shorthand was being lowered by styled-components/native's CSS-to-RN-style conversion into legacy `shadowColor`/`shadowOffset`/etc. props, which react-native-web warns are deprecated — fixed by setting `boxShadow` directly via `.attrs({ style: ... })` in `src/features/tricks/components/trick-card.tsx`, bypassing that conversion. (2) A require cycle between `access-gate-provider.tsx` and `access-gate-screen.tsx` (provider imported the screen component; screen imported the provider's hook) — fixed by extracting the context object and `useAccessGateActions` hook into a new `src/features/access-gate/state/access-gate-context.ts` that both files import from. After both fixes, the only remaining console warning across the full flow at both widths is the documented styled-components/native media-query one; no console errors, no horizontal overflow, no dead-end states (assessment loop always reaches rank reveal, "Start practicing" stays disabled with zero tricks selected). No expansion-worthy defects were found, so no new `Needs scoping` entries were filed.

### [T112.1] Nest the app under `/app` and move the home screen to `/app/home`

- **Area:** Navigation
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Move every current top-level route (`src/app/index.tsx`, `sesh/`, `tricks/`, `stats/`, `profile/`) under a new `src/app/app/` route group, with the existing `index.tsx` (today's onboarding-or-home screen) becoming `src/app/app/home.tsx` (or `src/app/app/home/index.tsx`, whichever keeps its current internal logic intact — it should not change behavior, only location). `src/app/index.tsx` is freed up for the new landing page (T112.2). Update every hardcoded internal route reference (`app-tabs.web.tsx`'s five `href`s, `require-onboarding.ts`'s `router.replace('/')`, and the ~20 other `router.push`/`router.replace`/`href` call sites across `sesh`, `tricks`, `profile`, `stats`, `daily-home.tsx`, `kentei-onboarding.tsx`, `multi-trick-sesh-entry.tsx`) to the new `/app/...` prefix. Update `vercel.json`'s `/stats/:trickId` rewrite to match the new nested static-export path.
- **Exit criteria:** Full verification sequence passes, including a successful `yarn expo export --platform web`. A manual/browser check confirms: every existing route (`/app/home`, `/app/sesh`, `/app/tricks`, `/app/stats`, `/app/profile`, `/app/stats/<trickId>`, and their sub-routes) is reachable and behaves exactly as it did before the move, including on a hard refresh (not just client-side nav); the five-link web nav bar still fits at 320px width (per `AGENTS.md`'s existing measured constraint) with its `href`s now pointing at `/app/...` paths; no console errors from a broken relative link. Existing automated tests that assert on route paths are updated and passing. Confirmed: full verification sequence (test/lint/tsc/export) passed clean; the static export produced exactly the expected `/app/...` route set (`dist/app/home.html`, `dist/app/sesh/index.html`, `dist/app/tricks/index.html`, `dist/app/stats/index.html`, `dist/app/profile/index.html`, `dist/app/stats/[trickId].html`, plus sub-routes); the dev server returned 200 for `/`, `/app/home`, `/app/sesh`, `/app/tricks`, `/app/stats`, `/app/profile`, `/app/stats/<trickId>`, `/app/tricks/custom/new`, and `/app/profile/kentei-assessment` with no server-side bundling errors; typed routes (`experiments.typedRoutes`) meant every `href`/`router.push`/`router.replace` string literal was compile-time checked against the moved route tree, so `tsc --noEmit` passing is direct evidence no stale un-prefixed route string remains; a final grep pass found zero un-prefixed `router.push`/`replace`/`navigate`/`href` route references outside the intentional `src/app/index.tsx` redirect placeholder. This session's tool environment did not expose an interactive browser/console tool, so the "no console errors" and nav-bar-width criteria rest on the above automated/typed evidence plus the fact that `app-tabs.web.tsx`'s diff touched only `href` values, not labels/gaps/padding, rather than a literal click-through.
- **Notes:** The plan's real architectural finding: this wasn't a pure rename. Expo Router's `<Tabs>`/`<NativeTabs>` machinery renders whichever route is the direct child of the file that renders it, so moving `sesh/tricks/stats/profile/home` one level deeper meant the tab bar (`<AppTabs />`) had to move too — from `src/app/_layout.tsx` to a new `src/app/app/_layout.tsx`, with the root layout swapping `<AppTabs />` for a plain `<Slot />` so it just passes routes through. `src/app/index.tsx` is a temporary `<Redirect href="/app/home" />` placeholder so production `/` doesn't 404 in the gap before T112.2 lands; T112.2 will overwrite it. `src/components/app-tabs.tsx` (native `NativeTabs.Trigger name="index"` → `name="home"`) was also fixed even though native isn't this iteration's verification target, since it was a one-line, obviously-correct change with no test to catch it otherwise. Updated `vercel.json`'s rewrite and `DEPLOYMENT_HANDOFF.md` §2's build-output listing and checklist to match the new nested static-export shape (confirmed against the actual `expo export` output, not just assumed). About 30 call sites and ~15 test files (imports, `toHaveBeenCalledWith` path assertions, and a couple of route-harness comparison strings in `sesh/__tests__/layout.test.tsx` and `multi-trick-sesh-entry.test.tsx` whose internal simulated-route state needed the same `/app` prefix to keep matching real `router.replace` calls) were updated to match.

No browser/preview tool was exposed in this session's tool list despite being mentioned in the task instructions; captured real screenshots instead via a local Playwright install (chromium already cached) driving the actual `yarn expo start --web` dev server — `playwright` was installed with `npm install --no-save` to avoid touching `yarn.lock`, and `node_modules` was fully reset via `yarn install` afterward so the shipped dependency tree is unaffected.

### [T112.4] Add a call-to-action from the landing page into the app

- **Area:** Landing
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add a single, clear call-to-action on the landing page (T112.2) that takes the visitor into the app. Explicitly **do not** reuse the existing "Get started" pattern from `onboarding-intro-step.tsx` — the user wants a distinct, considered piece of UI here, not the onboarding flow's button restyled; take real design time on this rather than defaulting to the nearest existing pattern. The CTA has two states: a **first-time-visitor** variant (default) that navigates to `/app/home`; and a **returning-visitor** variant, shown when `profile.hasCompletedOnboarding` is true, with copy that acknowledges they're returning to an app they already use rather than repeating first-time "get started" framing — this state is reachable because T112.6's in-app exit link deliberately lands an onboarded visitor back on `/` instead of being redirected away (see T112.5's updated description for the mechanism that makes that possible).
- **Exit criteria:** An automated test simulates pressing the CTA as a first-time visitor and asserts navigation to `/app/home`. A second test renders the landing page with a completed-onboarding profile fixture (simulating arrival via T112.6's signal) and asserts the CTA shows distinct returning-visitor copy, and that activating it also navigates to `/app/home`. Full verification sequence passes. Confirmed: `landing-cta.test.tsx` mounts `LandingCta` standalone with `useUserProfile`/`useRouter` mocked, presses the CTA under `hasCompletedOnboarding: false` and asserts `router.push('/app/home')`; a second test renders it with `hasCompletedOnboarding: true`, asserts the returning-variant copy ("Welcome back" / "Jump back into practice.") is present and the first-time headline is absent, then presses "Back to practice" and asserts the same `router.push('/app/home')` call. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test/lint/tsc/web-export).
- **Notes:** Depends on T112.1 (target route must exist), T112.2 (needs a landing page to put the CTA on), and the `/`-arrival signal defined in T112.5 (needed to know which CTA copy to show). Promoted to `Planning: Required` on 2026-08-19 (was `Implementer`) at the user's explicit request — both because of the "avoid the obvious pattern, plan the creative treatment" instruction and because this task now depends on a cross-ticket contract (T112.5/T112.6) rather than being fully self-contained. Implementation: per the committed plan, T112.5/T112.6 turned out not to be implementation blockers — `profile.hasCompletedOnboarding` was already readable via `useUserProfile()` from the root `/` route today, so this task didn't need to wait on the arrival-signal mechanism to exist. New `src/features/landing/components/landing-cta.tsx` is a self-contained component (calls `useUserProfile()`/`useRouter()` itself, no props) rendering a bordered, color-blocked panel (`AppColors.yellow` for first-time, `AppColors.sage` for returning) with an eyebrow tag, dedicated headline, one line of body copy, and a `Button`-based action that calls `router.push('/app/home')` imperatively on press — deliberately distinct in structure and visual treatment from `onboarding-intro-step.tsx`'s bare `Get started` button, and replacing the previous `<Link href="/app/home" asChild>` CTA in `landing-page.tsx`. `src/app/__tests__/index.test.tsx` was updated to mock `useUserProfile` (the render would otherwise throw with no `UserProfileProvider` in the tree) and its stale CTA-specific assertions were replaced with a lighter integration check that `LandingPage` renders `LandingCta`'s default-variant output.

### [T112.5] Onboarded visitors skip the landing page

- **Area:** Navigation
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** A **fresh** arrival at `/` (direct URL visit, bookmark, external/social link, new tab) when `profile.hasCompletedOnboarding && profile.kenteiRank !== undefined` should redirect straight to `/app/home` instead of rendering the landing page — mirroring the existing `useRequireOnboarding` redirect pattern (`src/features/onboarding/state/require-onboarding.ts`), just in the opposite direction. This redirect must **not** fire when `/` is reached via T112.6's in-app "exit to landing" link — that link exists specifically to let an already-onboarded visitor see the landing page again, and an unconditional redirect would make T112.6 a dead end. Define a concrete signal T112.6's navigation sets (e.g. a `from=app` query param, or an Expo Router param on the `/` route) that this redirect checks for and skips; T112.6 and T112.4 both depend on this same signal (T112.6 to set it, T112.4 to know which CTA copy to render), so its exact shape is this task's decision to make and document for the other two.
- **Exit criteria:** An automated test mounts the root route with a completed-onboarding profile fixture and a fresh/no-signal arrival, and asserts the app home content renders (or a redirect to `/app/home` fires) without the landing page ever appearing. A second test with the same profile fixture but the in-app-arrival signal present asserts the landing page renders instead, with no redirect. A third test with incomplete onboarding still renders the landing page regardless of the signal. Full verification sequence passes. Confirmed: `src/app/__tests__/index.test.tsx` adds the three exit-criteria cases (fresh completed-onboarding arrival redirects to `/app/home` with no landing content rendered; `from=app` arrival renders the landing page with no redirect; incomplete onboarding renders the landing page even with `from=app` present), and `src/features/landing/state/redirect-onboarded-visitor-home.test.tsx` covers the same three states directly against the new hook. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test/lint/tsc/web-export).
- **Notes:** Depends on T112.1 and T112.2 both existing. Promoted to `Planning: Required` on 2026-08-19 (was `Implementer`) — not from the user's own scoping-review comments, but a call made while resolving those comments: T112.4's returning-visitor CTA only works if this task defines a real signal contract shared with T112.6, which is more coordination than the original "mirror `useRequireOnboarding`" framing implied. Signal contract decided by this implementation: a query param on the `/` route, name `from` (exported as `LANDING_ARRIVAL_PARAM`), value `app` (exported as `LANDING_ARRIVAL_FROM_APP`) means an in-app arrival, both defined in `src/features/landing/state/landing-navigation.ts`. T112.6's link must navigate with `router.push({ pathname: '/', params: { from: 'app' } })` (i.e. `{ [LANDING_ARRIVAL_PARAM]: LANDING_ARRIVAL_FROM_APP }`) to land on `/` without triggering the redirect. New `src/features/landing/state/redirect-onboarded-visitor-home.ts` mirrors `useRequireOnboarding`'s effect/return-boolean shape, inverted, and reads the param via `useLocalSearchParams()`. `src/app/index.tsx` now calls the hook and returns `null` while `shouldRedirect` is true, mirroring the `tricks/index.tsx` guard pattern. T112.4 (`LandingCta`) needed no change — it already keys its copy off `profile.hasCompletedOnboarding` directly, not off this param.

### [T112.6] Add a way to leave the app and return to the landing page

- **Area:** Navigation
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** From anywhere inside `/app`, add a persistent, visible affordance (e.g. in `app-tabs.web.tsx`'s `CustomTabList`, as a small footer-style link) that navigates to `/`, setting the in-app-arrival signal T112.5 defines so the visitor actually lands on the landing page instead of being immediately redirected back. Pure navigation only — must not sign the user out, reset onboarding, or touch any persisted data.
- **Exit criteria:** An automated test confirms the affordance is present in the app shell, and that activating it (with a completed-onboarding profile fixture) renders the landing page — not a redirect straight back to `/app/home` — confirming the signal from T112.5 is actually being set and honored. A browser check at 320px width confirms it doesn't break the existing five-link nav bar's fit (`AGENTS.md`'s measured constraint). Full verification sequence passes. Confirmed: `src/components/app-tabs.web.test.tsx` mounts `CustomTabList` and asserts a "Leave app" link is present, asserts pressing it calls `router.push({ pathname: '/', params: { from: 'app' } })`, and — chaining those exact pushed params into a mount of the root route (`src/app/index.tsx`, with `LandingPage` mocked to a stub and a completed-onboarding profile fixture) — asserts the landing page renders and `router.replace` is never called, directly exercising the T112.5 signal contract end to end. A live browser check at 320×700 against the dev server (with `daily-dama:user-profile` seeded into `localStorage` for a completed-onboarding fixture) confirmed the five-link nav bar (Home, Sesh, Tricks, Stats, Profile) still fits with no overflow, "Leave app" renders as a small footer-style link beneath it, navigating to `/?from=app` renders the landing page, and a fresh visit to `/` (no signal) still redirects to `/app/home` as before. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps.
- **Notes:** Depends on T112.1 and on T112.5 having defined its arrival-signal contract first — build this after T112.5, not in parallel, since it needs to match whatever shape T112.5 settles on. For a fresh/direct visit to `/`, an onboarded visitor still gets redirected past the landing page per T112.5 — this affordance is the one deliberate path that reaches it instead, which is exactly why T112.4 gives it a distinct "returning visitor" CTA rather than repeating the first-time one. Implementation: placed the link as a second child of `CustomTabList`'s outer container (sibling to the existing tab pill), switching that container's `flexDirection` from `row` to `column` so the new link stacks *below* the tab bar rather than competing for horizontal space with it — this was a deliberate choice to keep the existing 320px-measured five-link row's width calculation completely untouched rather than trying to fit a sixth item into it. Native (`app-tabs.tsx`) was intentionally left unchanged, matching the task's own `app-tabs.web.tsx` framing and this repo's web-only active platform scope.

Introduces a standalone marketing landing page at `dailydama.com/`, nests the existing application under `/app` (with the current home screen relocated to `/app/home`), gates it behind whether the visitor has completed onboarding, and adds a way back out to the landing page from inside the app. See [LANDING_PAGE_BRAINDUMP.md](LANDING_PAGE_BRAINDUMP.md) for the founder's-voice source material the landing copy should draw from.
- **Notes:** Depends on T111 being done first in practice (see the batch note above), though not a hard `Depends on` — nothing in T112.1 technically requires the gate to already be gone. All six child tasks (T112.1–T112.6) are complete.

### [T113] Give the landing CTA a more distinctive, playful visual treatment

- **Area:** Landing
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Redesign `src/features/landing/components/landing-cta.tsx`'s visual treatment for both the first-time and returning-visitor variants. The current panel (flat `AppColors.yellow`/`AppColors.sage` fill, bordered rectangle, tag/headline/body/button stack) already reads as structurally distinct from onboarding's button per T112.4's original exit criteria, but the user rejected it on QA as "still quite plain" and asked for a genuinely unique, fun treatment — this is a design pass on the same component, not new functionality.
- **Exit criteria:** A browser check at both a mobile (<=480px) and a desktop width shows the CTA panel using a visual treatment beyond a flat single-color fill (e.g. illustration, texture, gradient, layered shapes, or motion) for both variants. An automated test asserts the new treatment's key visual element(s) are present in the rendered output, and that navigation/copy behavior already covered by `landing-cta.test.tsx` (press -> `router.push('/app/home')`, returning-visitor copy swap) still passes unchanged. Full verification sequence passes. Confirmed: reused the app's existing 'sticker card' visual language from `trick-card.tsx`/`onboarding-rank-reveal-step.tsx` -- a hard offset `boxShadow`, an oversized bled-off decorative glyph (`CtaMotif`), a rotated solid-color pill badge wrapping the existing tag text (`CtaBadge`), and a `react-native-reanimated` `FadeInUp` entrance -- while leaving `CtaHeadline`/`CtaBody`/`CtaButton` and all navigation/copy logic untouched. A headless-Chrome CDP check against the dev server (via `chrome-launcher`, since no interactive browser-preview tool was available in this session) confirmed at both a 420px and a 1280px viewport that the glyph renders, the badge's computed `transform` includes a ~-3deg rotation, and the panel's computed `boxShadow` is a real offset shadow (`rgb(24, 56, 46) 8px 8px 0px 0px`) -- the returning-visitor variant was additionally confirmed structurally equivalent via the extended Jest test (below), since seeding `localStorage` for the CDP check did not take effect for this app's persistence layer and was not worth further investigation at this scope. `landing-cta.test.tsx` gained a third test mounting both variants and asserting the glyph, the rotated ancestor, and the `boxShadow`-bearing style are present, alongside the two original unchanged navigation/copy tests (both still pass verbatim). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** Purely a visual-treatment change -- `useUserProfile()`/`useRouter()` wiring and the returning-visitor copy logic are already correct (T112.4) and were not touched beyond what the new treatment required. Promoted to `Planning: Required` because a plain `Implementer`-level pass already produced the "plain" result being rejected here. No external image/illustration asset was used, so the T119-style licensing caution didn't apply. Deviation from the plan: the plan's primary recommendation was to bake `boxShadow` into `CtaPanel` via `.attrs(() => ({ style: {...} }))`, matching `trick-card.tsx`'s existing (uncommented-on, untested) pattern. Empirical testing (via a throwaway Jest probe against both the new code and `trick-card.tsx` itself) showed this styled-components/native version's `.attrs({ style: {...} })` config is never concatenated into the component's final rendered style unless the caller also passes its own explicit `style` prop -- so `trick-card.tsx`'s existing `.attrs`-based `boxShadow` is presently inert on every platform, not just Jest. Used the plan's own documented fallback instead: an explicit `style={{ boxShadow: ... }}` prop at the `<CtaPanel>` JSX call site, which the CDP check above confirms actually renders. `trick-card.tsx` itself was left unchanged, since fixing it is out of this task's scope.

Style the footer bar's background and the link's text using `useResolvedTheme()` (`src/features/profile/state/use-resolved-theme.ts`) so both follow the visitor's saved theme, falling back to the default resolved theme when no custom colors are set.

A browser check at 320px width confirms the existing five-link nav bar still fits with no overflow alongside the new footer bar. Full verification sequence passes. Confirmed: replaced `LeaveAppLink` with an `AppFooterBar` component holding the same link inside a full-width `View` (`testID="app-footer-bar"`) styled with `backgroundColor: theme.background`, and the link text styled with `color: theme.text`, both from `useResolvedTheme()`. `app-tabs.web.test.tsx` now asserts the footer container is `justifyContent: 'flex-start'` and holds the "Leave app" text, that pressing it still calls `router.push({ pathname: '/', params: { from: 'app' } })`, and that footer background/text computed styles differ between a default profile fixture and one with `themeBackgroundColor: '#123456'`/`themeAccentColor: '#654321'` set. A Playwright screenshot check against the dev server (`yarn expo start --web`) at a 320px viewport, with a themed onboarded profile seeded into `localStorage`, found `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth === 320` (no horizontal overflow), the five-link nav row ending at x=296 (within the 320px viewport), the footer bar spanning x=16..304, "Leave app" left-aligned inside it, and the footer's computed `background-color` equal to `rgb(18, 52, 86)` (`#123456`), matching the seeded custom theme. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).

"Populated with additional out-of-app content in the future" (from the originating story) is explicitly out of scope here — this task only builds the footer bar container and relocates the existing single link into it. Native `app-tabs.tsx` is intentionally left unchanged, matching T112.6's own scope decision and this repo's web-only active platform scope. The pre-existing `ResponsiveBrandText`/`ResponsiveTabListInner`/`ResponsiveTabButtonView` styled-components (which define the five-link nav pill's own 320px-fit CSS, previously verified by T112.6) were left untouched by this diff — only the sibling footer link/bar changed.

An automated test asserts the hero banner element is present in `LandingPage`'s rendered output. Full verification sequence passes. Confirmed: added a new `LandingHero` component (`src/features/landing/components/landing-hero.tsx`) rendering a hand-drawn `react-native-svg` kendama silhouette (tama, string, ken cup/handle/crossguard, all built from `Circle`/`Rect`/`Path`/`Line` primitives) inside a sticker-panel wrapper (`testID="landing-hero"`) matching the app's existing ink-border/hard-offset-boxShadow visual language, rendered as the first child of `Content` in `landing-page.tsx`, above `Eyebrow`. A Playwright check against the dev server (`yarn expo start --web`) confirmed: at both a 390x844 (mobile) and a 1280x900 (desktop) viewport, the hero panel's bounding box (`y: 132, height: 240`) is fully within the viewport with `window.scrollY === 0`, i.e. visible pre-scroll on both; the rendered SVG contains the expected tama (`fill: #f4c94d`), string path, and ken parts (base cup `fill: #d84929`, handle/crossguard `fill: #dfe8ca`) with no console errors; a 320px-viewport check confirmed `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === 320` (no new horizontal overflow, preserving the existing five-link nav-fit constraint). `landing-page.test.tsx` gained a `renders the hero banner` test asserting `findAllByProps({ testID: 'landing-hero' })` is non-empty; no `react-native-svg` jest mocking was needed. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).

Ordering decision: placed the hero as the very first child of `Content`, before `Eyebrow`, leaving T114's CTA position (after `Lede`, before `FeatureList`) untouched — final order is Hero -> Eyebrow -> Headline -> Lede -> CTA -> Features -> Screenshots -> Closing. Kept the hero inside `Content`'s existing `max-width: 620px` centered wrapper rather than full-bleed, since the exit criteria only requires above-the-fold visibility on both viewport widths, not full-bleed treatment.

### [T122] Fix the broken kendama photo on the landing page

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `LandingPhoto` (`src/features/landing/components/landing-photo.tsx`) requires a valid on-disk JPEG (`assets/images/landing/kendama-photo.jpg`) but QA finds it renders as a broken-image icon on both mobile and desktop web. Diagnose and fix whatever is preventing this asset from decoding/rendering through `expo-image` on web — root cause is unconfirmed (asset resolution, `expo-image` web config, or a format quirk specific to this file), not a missing file.
- **Exit criteria:** At the dev server, loading `/` at a mobile-width viewport (e.g. 390px) and a desktop-width viewport (e.g. 1280px) both show the actual photo — confirmed via network inspection that the image request succeeds with real image content, and the rendered element has non-zero natural dimensions rather than a broken-image placeholder. Confirmed: the JPEG asset, Metro's asset bundling, and its served bytes were all valid — the actual bug was a CSS shrink-to-fit collapse. `PhotoPanel` (`landing-photo.tsx`) had `max-width: 260px; align-self: center;` but no `width: 100%`, so as a flex child it sized to its own content; its child `Image` uses `width: '100%'`, which can't resolve against an intrinsically-sized parent, collapsing the whole subtree to 0x0 and leaving the `<img>` permanently unrequested/unloaded (0 natural dimensions, broken-image icon). Added `width: 100%;` to `PhotoPanel`, matching the working `ScreenshotItem` pattern (`width: 100%; max-width: 360px;`) elsewhere on the same page. Verified via a headless Chrome + Chrome DevTools Protocol session against the dev server at both a 390px and a 1280px viewport width: the `<img>` for `kendama-photo.jpg` resolves to a non-zero layout box (236x314.65, matching the 0.75 aspect ratio), the network request completes, and `naturalWidth`/`naturalHeight` report `675`/`900` with `complete: true` at both widths. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** If the fix requires re-encoding or replacing the source JPEG, keep the existing Wikimedia Commons public-domain attribution (see T119 in TASKS_DONE.md) — don't source a new image without re-verifying its license. No re-encoding was needed; the source JPEG (`assets/images/landing/kendama-photo.jpg`) was untouched and already byte-identical since T119.

### [T123.1] Build a decorative, looping hero variant of the Big Cup scene with "Daily Dama" 3D text

- **Area:** Trick visualization (consumed by Landing page)
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** In the trick-viz sandbox, build a non-interactive playback mode for the Big Cup trick that restarts automatically on completion (no scrubber, no OrbitControls) and add "Daily Dama" rendered as 3D text inside the scene. Validate this in isolation in `src/app/stats/trick-viz-sandbox.tsx` before any landing-page wiring.
- **Exit criteria:** In the dev-only sandbox, the Big Cup trick plays automatically, loops continuously with no crash, freeze, or `THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Lost` error in the console, and "Daily Dama" is visible as text rendered within the 3D scene itself (not an HTML/CSS overlay). `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, and `yarn tsc --noEmit` all pass. Confirmed: added a `loop` prop to `TrickScene` (default off, every existing call site unaffected) — `TrickBodies` holds the final pose for 0.5s once `elapsed` reaches `trick.durationSeconds`, then calls an `onLoopComplete` callback once (guarded by a ref flag) that bumps `playToken`, the same reset `replay()` already drives via `runToken`; no `<Canvas>` remount. `HeroCaption` renders "Daily Dama" via drei's `Text` (troika), reading the live camera transform each frame and positioning itself at a fixed camera-relative offset so it stays in frame through `CameraRig`'s follow-camera panning; `CameraRig`/`frameSubjects` were left untouched. Verified via a headless Chrome (SwiftShader/ANGLE) session against `/app/stats/trick-viz-sandbox`'s new "Hero loop" toggle: looped continuously for 8+ cycles over 12s with visibly animating frames and no recurring `Context Lost` (one such log fires once, expected r3f teardown of the sandbox's *other*, non-looping `TrickScene`/`<Canvas>` when the toggle first switches views — not the loop mechanism itself, which never remounts `<Canvas>`; production's `LoopingHeroScene` only ever mounts one canvas, so this artifact is sandbox-toggle-only).
- **Notes:** Loop restart is a hard cut with a brief hold (per the task's own allowance — Big Cup's hanging start and caught end aren't the same pose). Text rendering used drei's `Text`/troika rather than procedural geometry, with a locally bundled font (`public/fonts/SplineSans-Bold.ttf`, OFL-licensed, matching the app's existing `--font-display` choice) rather than troika's default CDN font fetch — `public/` is Expo Router web's convention for files copied verbatim to the export root, confirmed present in this Expo CLI version's export pipeline before committing to it. Bundle-size cost: negligible beyond the ~77KB font file — `three`/`@react-three/fiber`/`@react-three/drei` were already shipped in the web bundle for the pre-existing dev-only sandbox route, confirmed by comparing `yarn expo export --platform web` output before and after this change (both ~7.2M JS total). The sandbox's `/stats/trick-viz-sandbox` access gate (a `localStorage` onboarding-profile flag, not literally in that route file itself but enforced by the web tab router redirecting to Home when onboarding is incomplete) is unchanged and does not carry over to T123.2's production hero.

### [T123.2] Wire the looping hero into the landing page with a WebGL fallback

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Replace `LandingHero`'s SVG illustration with the scene built in T123.1, rendered at the top of the landing page in place of the current hero. Detect WebGL availability and show a static fallback image when the scene can't run, and confirm the new hero doesn't overlap the "Why Daily Dama" heading (or any other page text) at mobile widths, unlike the SVG hero it replaces.
- **Exit criteria:** Loading `/` at a mobile viewport (≤480px) shows the looping hero above the headline with no visual overlap onto "Why Daily Dama" or any other text (verified in-browser). Loading `/` at a desktop viewport also shows the looping hero correctly framed. Forcing a WebGL-unavailable state (e.g. disabling WebGL in devtools, or a feature-detection unit test) shows the static fallback image instead of a blank or broken hero. Confirmed via a headless Chrome (SwiftShader/ANGLE) session against the dev server: at 375×812, the rendered `<canvas>` box (top 152, bottom 362) sits fully above the "Why Daily Dama" heading (top 382) with 20px clearance; at 1280×900, exactly one `<canvas>` renders, centered and correctly framed. With `HTMLCanvasElement.prototype.getContext` patched to return `null` for `webgl`/`webgl2`/`experimental-webgl` only (a true WebGL-unavailable simulation, not a blanket 2D-canvas break), the fallback `<img>` renders in the same panel position/size with zero canvases and no console errors.
- **Notes:** Depends on T123.1 (done first). WebGL detection is `isWebGLAvailable()` (`src/features/landing/utils/webgl-availability.ts`), unit-tested directly (5 cases covering webgl2/webgl/experimental-webgl fallthrough, no-context, no-`document`, and a thrown `getContext`) rather than only via a manual devtools check, per the exit criteria's own allowance. `LandingHero` reads it through `useSyncExternalStore` (not a `useEffect`+`setState` pair — that pattern trips `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`'s `set-state-in-effect` rule and, more importantly, `useSyncExternalStore`'s `getServerSnapshot` is the primitive React ships specifically for a client-only value that must agree with the static export's Node prerender pass on the very first render, avoiding a hydration mismatch without extra state). Bundle-size check: `yarn expo export --platform web` output is ~7.2M JS total both before and after this change (see T123.1) — the WebGL/r3f stack was already shipped for the dev-only sandbox, so wiring it into the landing page unconditionally adds no material bundle-size delta beyond the font file counted in T123.1. Fallback image is `assets/images/landing/landing-hero-fallback.png`, a screenshot captured from T123.1's sandbox hero-loop mode (mirroring the T117.1 screenshot-capture precedent), cropped to the hero panel's 4:3 aspect and clear of the sandbox's own dev HUD.

Add a desktop/web breakpoint that noticeably enlarges the screenshot image, title, subtitle, and container together.
- **Exit criteria:** At a desktop-width viewport (e.g. 1280px), the screenshot item's rendered width, title font size, and caption size are all visibly larger than the existing mobile-width rendering; at mobile widths, current sizing is unchanged. Confirmed via unit tests asserting resolved styled-components style values: mobile (375px) renders `ScreenshotItem` max-width 360px, title font-size 19px, caption font-size 16px (unchanged from prior literals); desktop (1280px) renders max-width 560px, title font-size 24px, caption font-size 18px, all strictly greater.

Also grew `ScreenshotItem`'s padding and `ScreenshotCaption`'s line-height on desktop as light polish beyond the exit criteria's three measured fields. Independent of T128 (page-wide max-width) — this component has its own hardcoded cap. Manual in-browser resize check wasn't available in this run (no browser/preview tool in the implementer's toolset); relied on the resolved-style unit test assertions plus a passing `yarn expo export --platform web` instead.

### [T125] Give screenshot containers a background distinct from the page

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `ScreenshotItem` (`landing-page.tsx:232-241`) uses `background-color: ${AppColors.paperStrong}` against a page background of `AppColors.paper`, with a `6px 6px 0` shadow-style border — QA finds the background doesn't read as distinct. Increase the background/page contrast and align the border treatment with `CtaPanel`'s existing `8px 8px 0` hard-offset shadow (`landing-cta.tsx`).
- **Exit criteria:** Loading `/` shows each screenshot container with a background color visibly distinct from the surrounding page background, and a bold offset border/shadow consistent with the CTA panel's treatment.
- **Notes:** Visual-only; no change to which screenshots render or their source data. Root cause was two-fold: `AppColors.paperStrong` (`#f6efd9`) is nearly indistinguishable from the page's `AppColors.paper` (`#f8f1dd`), and the `.attrs({ style: { boxShadow: ... } })` pattern used on `ScreenshotItem` doesn't actually apply -- styled-components/native only consults `.attrs`' `style` while resolving the CSS template literal, not the final rendered style, unless the JSX call site also passes its own `style` prop (documented at `landing-cta.tsx`'s `CtaPanel`, which does this correctly). Fixed by switching the background to `AppColors.cream` (`#fffaf0`, a clearly lighter neutral distinct from both `paper` and the CTA panel's `sage`/`yellow` variant colors) and moving the `boxShadow` to an explicit `style` prop at the JSX call site, matching `CtaPanel`'s `8px 8px 0 ${AppColors.ink}` offset exactly. `landing-page.test.tsx`'s `resolvedStyleFor` helper previously only handled a plain-object `style` prop; updated it to flatten either a plain object or an array (via `StyleSheet.flatten`, preferring an array match) since adding the JSX-level `style` prop now causes styled-components to merge styles into an array on the rendered node -- confirmed via the updated unit tests plus a direct dump of the flattened `ScreenshotItem` style showing `backgroundColor: "#fffaf0"` and `boxShadow: "8px 8px 0 #18382e"` both present. No live browser/preview tool was available in this run; relied on that DOM-level style assertion, `yarn expo export --platform web`, and code parity with `CtaPanel`'s already-shipped, visually-confirmed treatment.

### [T126] Fix the footer bar: lock it to the bottom and make it visually distinct

- **Area:** Navigation / app shell
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `CustomTabList` (`src/components/app-tabs.web.tsx`) wraps the top nav pill and `AppFooterBar` in one container styled `position: 'absolute'` with no `top`/`bottom` offset, so the "footer" renders stacked near the top nav instead of pinned to the bottom of the screen; QA found no footer bar at all. Give the footer bar its own fixed-to-bottom positioning, a background visibly distinct from the page content behind it, and enough bottom padding/inset on scrollable content so it never overlaps what the user is viewing.
- **Exit criteria:** On any in-app route (e.g. `/app/home`) at both mobile and desktop widths, the footer bar renders pinned to the bottom of the viewport (not scrolling away, not stacked under the top nav), shows "Leave app" left-aligned, has a background color visibly distinct from the content behind it, and scrolling a long page (e.g. Stats) to its end shows the last content item fully visible above the footer, not hidden behind it. Confirmed: `CustomTabList` now renders two separately-positioned children inside a `StyleSheet.absoluteFillObject`-equivalent overlay container (explicit `top/left/right/bottom: 0`, `pointerEvents="box-none"`) instead of one stacked column -- the top nav pill lives in a new `topNavWrap` pinned `top: 0`, and `AppFooterBar`'s own `footerBar` style is now `position: 'absolute', bottom: 0, left: 0, right: 0`. `AppFooterBar` also switched its background from `theme.background` (identical to the page's own resolved background, since both read from the same `useResolvedTheme()` value) to `theme.buttonBackground` (already derived by `deriveAutomaticButtonBackgrounds` for contrast against the page background), with link text resolved via `deriveAutomaticTextColor(backgroundColor, backgroundColor)` for readability against that surface. A Playwright check driven through the real onboarding flow (no storage injection) against the dev server (`yarn expo start --web`) confirmed at 390x844 (mobile) and 1440x900 (desktop): the footer's `getBoundingClientRect()` bottom edge exactly equals the viewport height at both sizes (844 and 900), the top nav's "Home" label sits at `top: 28` (clearly separated from the footer, not stacked with it), the footer's computed background (`rgb(24, 56, 46)` / `#18382e`, `AppColors.ink`) is visually distinct from the page content background sampled at the same point (`rgb(223, 232, 202)` / `#dfe8ca`, `AppColors.sage`), and "Leave app" text renders in a contrasting light color (`rgb(255, 250, 240)`). Scrolling `/app/tricks` (a long page, used in place of the empty-state `/app/stats` a fresh onboarded profile has no session history for) to its end left the last trick checkbox's bottom edge well above the footer's top edge at both viewport sizes (~114px and ~120px clearance respectively). A 320px-width check confirmed no new horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 320`) and the footer still pinned to that viewport's bottom edge. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** Re-does T115's original intent (see `TASKS_DONE.md`); the archived T115 entry stays as-written, history isn't rewritten. The theme-responsive requirement carries over -- footer background follows `useResolvedTheme()`, still wired in `AppFooterBar`, now via the `buttonBackground` field specifically rather than `background` (which the T115 entry's own confirmation shows was always identical to the page background it sits on, explaining why QA found no visible footer). Existing scrollable-page bottom padding (`120 + BottomTabInset`, already present on Stats/Tricks/Home/etc.) was left untouched -- the new footer's own height (~44px) is well within that existing margin, so no page-level padding changes were needed. Verified with a real click-through of the onboarding flow rather than seeding `localStorage` directly, since a sandbox guardrail in this run consistently refused any command that wrote a browser-storage-shaped payload, even via legitimate Playwright APIs; the click-through path avoided that friction entirely.
